Rubricae Missalis

All the rubrics of the Missale juxta ritum Sacri Ordinis Praedicatorum (Rome, 1939), in the order of the Missal: the defects in celebrating Mass, the preparation, the rite of Low Mass, the ceremonial of the ministers at the solemn and conventual Mass, Holy Communion, the general rubrics and the colours, with an English translation of our own. The Latin follows the Missal as printed, including the changes made to it up to 1939; the later supplements and the reforms of 1955 and after are not included.

  1. De defectibus in celebratione Missarum occurrentibus Of defects occurring in the celebration of Mass
    1. II De defectibus materiae Of defects of the matter
    2. III De defectu panis Of a defect of the bread
    3. IV De defectu vini Of a defect of the wine
    4. V De defectibus formae Of defects of the form
    5. VI De defectibus ministri Of defects of the minister
    6. VII De defectu intentionis Of a defect of intention
    7. VIII De defectibus dispositionis animae Of defects of the disposition of soul
    8. IX De defectibus dispositionis corporis Of defects of the disposition of body
    9. X De defectibus in ministerio ipso occurrentibus Of defects occurring in the ministry itself
    10. XI De defectibus requisitorum ex parte Sacerdotis, et altaris Of defects in what is required on the part of the priest and of the altar
    11. XII De defectu servitoris Of the lack of a server
    12. XIII De defectu et infirmitate Sacerdotis occurrente Of the failing or illness of the priest
    13. XIV De defectu admixtionis alicujus nocivi in calice, vel hostia Of something harmful falling into the chalice or on the host
    14. XV De defectibus fractionis hostiae, aut congelationis Sanguinis Of the breaking of the host and the freezing of the Blood
    15. XVI De defectibus effusionis Sanguinis, aut casus hostiae Of the spilling of the Blood or the falling of the host
  2. Quomodo sacerdos se praeparare debeat ad Missam How the priest is to prepare himself for Mass

    2. II Quod in sacristia facturus sit What he is to do in the sacristy

    3. III De celebratione Missae coram Summo Pontifice, Cardinale Legato Sedis Apostolicae, Archiepiscopo, aut Episcopo in sua residentia Of the celebration of Mass before the Supreme Pontiff, a Cardinal Legate of the Apostolic See, an Archbishop, or a Bishop in his residence
    4. IV De his, quae omittuntur in Missa pro Defunctis Of what is left out in Mass for the dead
    5. V De his, quae clara voce, mediocri, aut secreto dicenda sunt in Missa Of what is to be said in Mass aloud, in a moderate voice, or secretly
    6. VI De servitoribus Missarum privatarum Of the servers of private Masses

    12. XII Ad lotionem manuum, et ad Secretas At the washing of the hands, and at the Secrets

    17. XVII De ordinanda Missa conformiter ad praedictas Rubricas Of ordering the Mass according to these Rubrics
    18. XVIII De hora celebrandi Missam Of the hour of celebrating Mass
    19. XIX De uniformitate servanda circa Missae celebrationem Of the uniformity to be kept in celebrating Mass
    20. XX De Altari, et aliis requisitis ad Sacrificium Of the altar and the other things required for the Sacrifice
    21. De Coloribus quibus utendum est in ecclesiasticis indumentis, et paramentis altaris Of the colours to be used in the church vestments and the altar hangings

De defectibus in celebratione Missarum occurrentibusOf defects occurring in the celebration of Mass

Sacerdos celebraturus omnem adhibeat diligentiam, ne aliquid ex requisitis ad Sacramentum Eucharistiae conficiendum desit. Potest autem defectus contingere ex parte materiae consecrandae, et ex parte formae adhibendae, et ex parte ministri conficientis. Quidquid enim horum deficit, scilicet materia debita, forma cum intentione, et Ordo sacerdotalis in conficiente, non conficitur Sacramentum. Et his exsistentibus, quibuscumque aliis deficientibus, veritas adest Sacramenti. Alii vero sunt defectus, qui in Missae celebratione occurrentes, etsi veritatem Sacramenti non impediant, possunt tamen aut cum peccato, aut cum scandalo contingere.

The priest who is to celebrate shall take every care that nothing required for confecting the Sacrament of the Eucharist be lacking. A defect may occur on the part of the matter to be consecrated, of the form to be used, and of the minister who confects it. For if any of these is lacking, namely the due matter, the form with the intention, and the priestly Order in him who confects, the Sacrament is not confected. And when these are present, whatever else may be lacking, the reality of the Sacrament is there. There are other defects, however, which occurring in the celebration of Mass, though they do not prevent the reality of the Sacrament, may yet occur with sin or with scandal.

II De defectibus materiaeOf defects of the matter

Defectus ex parte materiae possunt contingere, si aliquid desit ex iis, quae ad ipsam requiruntur. Requiritur enim, ut sit panis triticeus, et vinum de vite: et ut hujusmodi materia consecranda, in actu consecrationis, sit coram Sacerdote.

Defects on the part of the matter may occur if anything is lacking of what it requires. For it is required that it be wheaten bread and wine of the grape, and that this matter to be consecrated be before the priest in the act of consecration.

III De defectu panisOf a defect of the bread

1. Si panis non sit triticeus, vel si triticeus, sit admixtus granis alterius generis in tanta quantitate, ut non maneat panis triticeus, vel sit alioquin corruptus, non conficitur Sacramentum. Si sit confectus de aqua rosacea, vel alterius distillationis, dubium est an conficiatur.

1. If the bread is not wheaten, or if wheaten is mixed with grain of another kind in such quantity that it does not remain wheaten bread, or is otherwise corrupt, the Sacrament is not confected. If it is made with rose-water or some other distilled water, it is doubtful whether it is confected.

2. Si coeperit corrumpi, sed non sit corruptus; similiter si non sit azymus, secundum morem Ecclesiae Latinae: conficitur quidem, sed conficiens graviter peccat.

2. If it has begun to corrupt but is not corrupt, and likewise if it is not unleavened according to the custom of the Latin Church, the Sacrament is confected, but he who confects it sins gravely.

3. Si celebrans ante consecrationem advertit hostiam esse corruptam, aut non esse triticeam: remota illa hostia, aliam ponat, et facta oblatione, saltem mente concepta, prosequatur ab eo loco, ubi desiit. Si id advertit post consecrationem, etiam post illius hostiae sumptionem: posita alia, faciat oblationem, ut supra, et a consecratione incipiat, scilicet ab illis verbis: Qui pridie quam pateretur, et illam priorem, si non sumpsit, sumat post sumptionem Corporis, et Sanguinis, vel aliis sumendam tradat, vel alicubi reverenter conservet. Si autem sumpserat, nihilominus sumat eam, quam consecravit; quia praeceptum de perfectione Sacramenti majoris est ponderis, quam quod a jejunis sumatur.

3. If before the consecration the celebrant notices that the host is corrupt, or not wheaten, he shall remove that host, put another, and having made the offering, at least in his mind, continue from the place where he left off. If he notices it after the consecration, even after consuming that host, he shall put another, make the offering as above, and begin from the consecration, that is from the words Qui pridie quam pateretur; and the former host, if he has not consumed it, he shall consume after the Body and Blood, or give it to others to consume, or keep it reverently somewhere. If he had consumed it, he shall nonetheless consume the one he has consecrated, for the precept of completing the Sacrament weighs more than that of receiving it fasting.

4. Advertendum autem, quod si hoc contingat post sumptionem Sanguinis, apponi debet rursus novus panis, et vinum cum aqua: et facta prius oblatione, ut supra, Sacerdos consecret, incipiendo ab illis verbis: Qui pridie, ac statim sumat utrumque, et Missam prosequatur, ne Sacramentum remaneat imperfectum, et ut debitus servetur ordo.

4. It is to be noted that if this happens after the consumption of the Blood, new bread and wine with water must be set out again, and after first making the offering as above, the priest shall consecrate beginning from the words Qui pridie, and at once consume both and continue the Mass, lest the Sacrament remain incomplete, and that the due order be kept.

5. Si hostia consecrata dispareat, vel casu aliquo, ut vento, aut miraculo, vel ab aliquo animali accepta, et nequeat reperiri: tunc altera consecretur, ab eo loco incipiendo: Qui pridie quam pateretur, facta ejus prius oblatione, ut supra; et illud animal, si capi potest, occidatur, et comburatur, et cineres ejiciantur in sacrarium, vel sub altari.

5. If the consecrated host disappears, whether by some chance such as the wind, or by a miracle, or is taken by some animal, and cannot be found, another shall be consecrated, beginning from Qui pridie quam pateretur, after first offering it as above; and the animal, if it can be caught, shall be killed and burnt, and the ashes thrown into the sacrarium or under the altar.

IV De defectu viniOf a defect of the wine

1. Si vinum sit factum penitus acetum, vel penitus putridum, vel de uvis acerbis seu non maturis expressum, vel ei fuerit admixtum tantum aquae, ut vinum sit corruptum: non conficitur Sacramentum.

1. If the wine has turned entirely to vinegar, or is entirely putrid, or is pressed from sour or unripe grapes, or so much water has been mixed with it that the wine is corrupted, the Sacrament is not confected.

2. Si vero coeperit acescere vel corrumpi, vel fuerit aliquantulum acre, vel mustum tunc de uvis expressum, vel non fuerit admixta aqua, vel fuerit admixta aqua rosacea, seu alterius distillationis: conficitur Sacramentum, sed conficiens graviter peccat.

2. If it has begun to sour or corrupt, or is somewhat sharp, or is must just pressed from the grapes, or no water has been mixed with it, or rose-water or another distilled water has been mixed with it, the Sacrament is confected, but he who confects it sins gravely.

3. Si celebrans ante consecrationem Sanguinis, quamvis post consecrationem Corporis, advertat, aut vinum, aut aquam, aut utrumque non esse in calice: debet statim apponere vinum cum aqua, et facta oblatione, ut supra, consecrare incipiendo ab illis verbis: Simili modo.

3. If before the consecration of the Blood, though after that of the Body, the celebrant notices that either the wine or the water or both are not in the chalice, he must at once put in wine with water, and having made the offering as above, consecrate beginning from the words Simili modo.

4. Si post verba consecrationis advertat vinum non fuisse positum, sed aquam: deposita aqua in aliquod vas, iterum vinum cum aqua ponat in calice, et consecret resumendo a verbis praedictis: Simili modo.

4. If after the words of consecration he notices that not wine but water was put in, he shall pour the water into some vessel, put wine with water in the chalice again, and consecrate, taking up again from the said words Simili modo.

5. Si hoc advertat post sumptionem Corporis, vel hujusmodi aquae: apponat aliam hostiam iterum consecrandam, et vinum cum aqua in calice; offerat utrumque, et consecret, et sumat, quamvis non sit jejunus: vel si Missa celebretur in loco publico, ubi plures adsint, ad evitandum scandalum poterit apponere vinum cum aqua, et facta oblatione, ut supra, consecrare, ac statim sumere, et prosequi cetera.

5. If he notices this after consuming the Body, or such water, he shall set out another host to be consecrated again, and wine with water in the chalice; he shall offer both, consecrate and consume them, though he is not fasting. Or, if the Mass is celebrated in a public place where many are present, to avoid scandal he may put in wine with water, and having made the offering as above, consecrate it, consume it at once, and continue the rest.

6. Si quis percipiat ante consecrationem, vel post consecrationem, totum vinum esse acetum, vel alias corruptum: idem servetur, quod supra, ac si deprehenderet non esse positum vinum, vel solam aquam fuisse appositam in calice.

6. If anyone perceives before or after the consecration that all the wine is vinegar, or otherwise corrupt, the same is to be observed as above, as if he found that no wine had been put in, or only water had been put in the chalice.

7. Si autem celebrans ante consecrationem calicis advertat non fuisse appositam aquam: statim ponat eam, et proferat verba consecrationis. Si id advertat post consecrationem calicis: nullo modo apponat, quia non est de necessitate Sacramenti.

7. If the celebrant notices before the consecration of the chalice that water was not put in, he shall put it in at once and say the words of consecration. If he notices it after the consecration of the chalice, he shall by no means put it in, for it is not necessary to the Sacrament.

8. Si materia, quae esset apponenda, ratione defectus vel panis, vel vini, non posset ullo modo haberi: si id sit ante consecrationem Corporis, ulterius procedi non debet: si post consecrationem Corporis, aut etiam vini, deprehenditur defectus alterius speciei, altera jam consecrata, tunc si nullo modo haberi possit, procedendum erit, et Missa absolvenda, ita tamen ut praetermittantur verba, et signa, quae pertinent ad speciem deficientem. Quod si exspectando aliquamdiu, haberi possit, exspectandum erit, ne imperfectum sacrificium remaneat.

8. If the matter that should be set out because of a defect of bread or wine cannot be had in any way: if this is before the consecration of the Body, he must not go further; if after the consecration of the Body, or even of the wine, a defect of the other species is found, the one being already consecrated, then, if it can in no way be had, he shall proceed and finish the Mass, but leaving out the words and signs which belong to the lacking species. But if by waiting a while it can be had, he shall wait, lest the sacrifice remain incomplete.

V De defectibus formaeOf defects of the form

1. Defectus ex parte formae possunt contingere, si aliquid desit ex iis, quae ad integritatem verborum in ipsa consecratione requiruntur. Verba autem consecrationis, quae sunt forma hujus Sacramenti, sunt haec: Hoc est enim Corpus meum. Et: Hic est enim calix Sanguinis mei, novi et aeterni testamenti, mysterium fidei, qui pro vobis, et pro multis effundetur in remissionem peccatorum. Si quis aliquid diminueret, vel immutaret de forma consecrationis Corporis, et Sanguinis, et in ipsa verborum immutatione, verba idem non significarent, non conficeret Sacramentum. Si quis aliquid adderet, quod significationem non mutaret, conficeret quidem, sed gravissime peccaret.

1. Defects on the part of the form may occur if anything is lacking of what is required for the integrity of the words in the consecration itself. The words of consecration, which are the form of this Sacrament, are these: Hoc est enim Corpus meum. And: Hic est enim calix Sanguinis mei, novi et aeterni testamenti, mysterium fidei, qui pro vobis, et pro multis effundetur in remissionem peccatorum. If anyone shortened or changed anything in the form of the consecration of the Body and Blood, and in that change of words the words did not mean the same thing, he would not confect the Sacrament. If anyone added something that did not change the meaning, he would confect it, but would sin most gravely.

2. Si celebrans non recordetur se dixisse ea, quae in consecratione communiter dicuntur, non debet propterea turbari. Si tamen certo ei constet se omisisse aliquid eorum, quae sunt de necessitate Sacramenti, id est, formam consecrationis, seu partem: resumat ipsam formam, et cetera prosequatur per ordinem.

2. If the celebrant does not remember having said what is ordinarily said in the consecration, he should not be troubled on that account. If, however, he is certain that he has left out something necessary to the Sacrament, that is, the form of consecration or part of it, he shall take up the form again and continue the rest in order.

3. Si vero valde probabiliter dubitat se aliquid essentiale omisisse, iteret formam, saltem sub tacita conditione. Si autem non sunt de necessitate Sacramenti, non resumat, sed procedat ulterius.

3. If he doubts with very good reason that he has left out something essential, he shall repeat the form, at least under a tacit condition. But if the things are not necessary to the Sacrament, he shall not take them up again, but go on.

VI De defectibus ministriOf defects of the minister

Defectus ex parte Ministri possunt contingere quoad ea, quae in ipso requiruntur. Haec autem sunt: In primis intentio; deinde dispositio animae, dispositio corporis, dispositio vestimentorum, dispositio in ministerio ipso quoad ea, quae in ipso possunt occurrere.

Defects on the part of the minister may occur as to what is required in him. These are: first, the intention; then the disposition of soul, the disposition of body, the disposition of the vestments, and the disposition in the ministry itself as to what may occur in it.

VII De defectu intentionisOf a defect of intention

1. Si quis non intendit conficere, sed delusorie aliquid agere.

1. If anyone does not intend to confect, but to do something in mockery.

2. Item si aliquae hostiae ex oblivione remaneant in altari, vel aliqua pars vini, vel aliqua hostia lateat: eae non consecrantur, cum non intendat consecrare nisi quas videt.

2. Likewise, if some hosts remain on the altar through forgetfulness, or some part of the wine, or some host lies hidden, they are not consecrated, since he intends to consecrate only those he sees.

3. Item si quis habeat coram se undecim hostias, et intendat consecrare solum decem, non determinans quas decem intendit: non consecrat, quia requiritur intentio determinata.

3. Likewise, if anyone has eleven hosts before him and intends to consecrate only ten, without determining which ten he intends, he does not consecrate, for a determinate intention is required.

4. Secus, si putans quidem esse decem, tamen omnes voluit consecrare, quas coram se habebat: nam tunc omnes erunt consecratae: atque ideo quilibet Sacerdos talem semper intentionem habere deberet, scilicet consecrandi eas omnes, quas ante se ad consecrandum positas habet.

4. It is otherwise if, thinking there were ten, he nevertheless willed to consecrate all that he had before him: for then all will be consecrated. And therefore every priest should always have such an intention, namely of consecrating all that he has set before him to be consecrated.

5. Si Sacerdos putans se tenere unam hostiam, post consecrationem invenerit fuisse duas simul junctas, in sumptione sumat simul utramque.

5. If a priest, thinking he holds one host, finds after the consecration that there were two joined together, he shall consume both together at the Communion.

6. Quod si deprehendat post sumptionem Corporis et Sanguinis, aut etiam post ablutionem, reliquias aliquas relictas consecratas, eas sumat, sive parvae sint, sive magnae, quia ad idem sacrificium spectant.

6. If after consuming the Body and Blood, or even after the ablution, he finds some consecrated remnants left, he shall consume them, whether small or large, for they belong to the same sacrifice.

7. Si vero relicta sit hostia integra consecrata, eam in tabernaculo cum aliis reponat; si hoc fieri nequit, sequenti Sacerdoti ibi celebraturo, in altari supra corporale decenter opertam, sumendam una cum altera, quam est consecraturus, relinquat; vel si neutrum horum fieri possit, in ipso calice, seu patena decenter conservet, quousque vel in tabernaculo reponatur, vel ab altero sumatur; quod si non habeat quomodo honeste conservetur, potest eam ipsemet sumere.

7. If a whole consecrated host has been left, he shall put it in the tabernacle with the others; if this cannot be done, he shall leave it for the next priest who is to celebrate there, decently covered on the corporal on the altar, to be consumed together with the one he will consecrate; or if neither can be done, he shall keep it decently in the chalice or on the paten until it is put in the tabernacle or consumed by another; and if he has no way of keeping it fittingly, he may consume it himself.

8. Si intentio non sit actualis in ipsa consecratione propter evagationem mentis, sit tamen virtualis, cum accedens ad altare intendat facere quod facit Ecclesia, conficitur Sacramentum, etsi curare debeat Sacerdos, ut etiam actualem intentionem adhibeat.

8. If the intention is not actual at the consecration itself because of wandering of mind, but is virtual, since in coming to the altar he intends to do what the Church does, the Sacrament is confected; though the priest should take care to have an actual intention as well.

VIII De defectibus dispositionis animaeOf defects of the disposition of soul

1. Si quis suspensus, excommunicatus, degradatus, irregularis, vel alias canonice impeditus celebret: conficit quidem Sacramentum, sed gravissime peccat, tam propter communionem, quam indigne sumit, quam propter exsecutionem Ordinum, quae sibi erat interdicta.

1. If anyone who is suspended, excommunicated, degraded, irregular or otherwise canonically impeded celebrates, he confects the Sacrament, but sins most gravely, both because of the Communion which he receives unworthily and because of the exercise of Orders which was forbidden him.

2. Si quis habens copiam Confessarii celebret in peccato mortali, graviter peccat.

2. If anyone who has access to a confessor celebrates in mortal sin, he sins gravely.

3. Si quis etiam in casu necessitatis, non habens copiam Confessarii, in peccato mortali absque contritione celebret, graviter peccat.

3. If anyone, even in a case of necessity, not having access to a confessor, celebrates in mortal sin without contrition, he sins gravely.

4. Secus si conteratur: debet tamen, cum primum poterit, confiteri.

4. It is otherwise if he is contrite; but he must confess as soon as he can.

5. Si in ipsa celebratione Missae Sacerdos recordetur se esse in peccato mortali, conteratur cum proposito confitendi, et satisfaciendi. Si recordetur se esse excommunicatum, vel suspensum aut locum esse interdictum, similiter conteratur, cum proposito petendi absolutionem: ante consecrationem autem, in supradictis casibus, si non timetur scandalum, debet Missam incoeptam deserere.

5. If during the very celebration of Mass the priest remembers that he is in mortal sin, he shall make an act of contrition with the purpose of confessing and making satisfaction. If he remembers that he is excommunicated or suspended, or that the place is under interdict, he shall likewise make an act of contrition with the purpose of seeking absolution; but before the consecration, in the cases mentioned, if no scandal is feared, he must leave the Mass he has begun.

IX De defectibus dispositionis corporisOf defects of the disposition of body

1. Si quis non est jejunus post mediam noctem, etiam post sumptionem solius aquae, vel alterius potus, aut cibi, per modum etiam medicinae, et in quantacumque parva quantitate: non potest communicare, nec celebrare.

1. If anyone is not fasting from midnight, even after taking water alone, or any other drink or food, even by way of medicine and in however small a quantity, he may neither communicate nor celebrate.

2. Si autem ante mediam noctem cibum, aut potum sumpserit, etiam si postmodum non dormierit, nec sit digestus, non peccat: sed ob perturbationem mentis, ex qua devotio tollitur, consulitur aliquando abstinendum.

2. But if he took food or drink before midnight, even if he has not slept since and it is not digested, he does not sin; but because of the disturbance of mind by which devotion is taken away, it is sometimes advised to abstain.

3. Si reliquiae cibi remanentis in ore transglutiantur, non impediunt communionem, cum non transglutiantur per modum cibi, sed per modum salivae. Idem dicendum, si lavando os, deglutiatur stilla aquae praeter intentionem.

3. If remnants of food remaining in the mouth are swallowed, they do not prevent Communion, since they are swallowed not as food but as saliva. The same is to be said if, in washing the mouth, a drop of water is swallowed unintentionally.

4. Si plures Missas in una die celebret, ut in Nativitate Domini: in unaquaque Missa abluat digitos super aliquod vas decens, et in ultima tantum percipiat digitorum ablutionem.

4. If he celebrates several Masses on one day, as on the Nativity of the Lord, at each Mass he shall wash his fingers over some fitting vessel, and take the ablution of the fingers only at the last.

5. Si praecesserit pollutio nocturna, quae causata fuerit ex praecedenti cogitatione, quae sit peccatum mortale, vel evenerit propter nimiam crapulam: abstinendum est a communione, et celebratione, nisi aliud Confessario videatur. Si dubium est, an in praecedenti cogitatione fuerit peccatum mortale, consulitur abstinendum, extra tamen casum necessitatis. Si autem certum est non fuisse in illa cogitatione peccatum mortale, vel nullam fuisse cogitationem malam, sed evenisse ex naturali causa, aut ex diabolica illusione, potest communicare, et celebrare; nisi ex illa corporis commotione tanta evenerit perturbatio mentis, ut abstinendum videatur.

5. If a nocturnal pollution has occurred which was caused by a preceding thought that was a mortal sin, or happened through excess of food and drink, one must abstain from Communion and from celebrating, unless the confessor judges otherwise. If it is doubtful whether there was mortal sin in the preceding thought, it is advised to abstain, except in a case of necessity. But if it is certain that there was no mortal sin in that thought, or that there was no evil thought at all, but that it happened from a natural cause or from an illusion of the devil, he may communicate and celebrate, unless such disturbance of mind has come from that bodily disturbance that it seems he should abstain.

X De defectibus in ministerio ipso occurrentibusOf defects occurring in the ministry itself

Possunt etiam defectus occurrere in ministerio ipso, si quid ex requisitis ad illud desit: quod ex pluribus capitibus provenire potest.

Defects may also occur in the ministry itself, if anything required for it is lacking; and this may come about in several ways.

De defectibus circumstantiarum loci, et temporis. 1. Si celebretur in loco non sacro, vel non deputato ab Episcopo, aut Abbate facultatem habente.

Of defects in the circumstances of place and time. 1. If Mass is celebrated in a place not sacred, or not designated by the Bishop, or by an Abbot having the faculty.

2. Si celebretur in Ecclesia interdicta, aut violata. Si vero, Sacerdote celebrante, violetur Ecclesia ante Canonem, dimittatur Missa: si post Canonem, non dimittatur.

2. If it is celebrated in a church under interdict or violated. If the church is violated while the priest is celebrating, before the Canon, the Mass shall be abandoned; if after the Canon, it shall not be abandoned.

3. Si timeatur incursus hostium, vel alluvionis, vel ruina loci, ubi celebratur: ante consecrationem dimittatur Missa; post consecrationem vero Sacerdos accelerare poterit sumptionem Sacramenti, omissis omnibus aliis.

3. If an attack of enemies, or a flood, or the collapse of the place where Mass is celebrated is feared: before the consecration the Mass shall be abandoned; after the consecration the priest may hasten the consumption of the Sacrament, leaving out everything else.

4. Si non sit tempus debitum celebrandi, quod est ab aurora usque ad meridiem communiter.

4. If it is not the due time for celebrating, which is ordinarily from dawn to midday.

XI De defectibus requisitorum ex parte Sacerdotis, et altarisOf defects in what is required on the part of the priest and of the altar

1. Si Sacerdos celebrat, cum Matutinum cum Laudibus non dixerit.

1. If the priest celebrates without having said Matins with Lauds.

2. Si celebret cooperto capite sine dispensatione.

2. If he celebrates with his head covered without dispensation.

3. Si omittat aliquid ex vestibus sacerdotalibus.

3. If he leaves off any of the priestly vestments.

4. Si vestes sacerdotales, et mappae non sint ab Episcopo, vel ab alio hanc habente potestatem, benedictae.

4. If the priestly vestments and the altar cloths are not blessed by the Bishop, or by another having this power.

5. Si pallae, purificatoria, et corporalia non sint munda, et ab Episcopo, vel ab alio hanc habente potestatem, benedicta.

5. If the palls, purificators and corporals are not clean, and blessed by the Bishop or by another having this power.

6. Si celebret in altari non consecrato, vel tribus mappis non cooperto.

6. If he celebrates on an altar not consecrated, or not covered with three cloths.

7. Si non adsit calix consecratus cum patena conveniens: cujus cuppa debet esse aurea, vel argentea, vel stannea; non aerea, vel vitrea.

7. If there is not a fitting consecrated chalice with its paten, whose cup must be of gold, silver or tin, not of bronze or glass.

8. Si non adsit Missale, licet memoriter sciret Missam, quam intendit dicere.

8. If there is no Missal, even though he knows by heart the Mass he intends to say.

9. Si non adsint luminaria cerea.

9. If there are no wax candles.

XII De defectu servitorisOf the lack of a server

Si non adsit clericus, vel alius deserviens in Missa: vel adsit, qui inservire non debet, ut mulier.

If there is no cleric or other person to serve the Mass, or one is present who ought not to serve, such as a woman.

XIII De defectu et infirmitate Sacerdotis occurrenteOf the failing or illness of the priest

1. Si Sacerdos ante consecrationem graviter infirmetur, vel in syncopem inciderit, aut moriatur, praetermittatur Missa: si post consecrationem Corporis tantum, ante consecrationem Sanguinis, vel utroque consecrato, id accidat, Missa per alium Sacerdotem expleatur ab eo loco, ubi ille desiit; et in casu necessitatis etiam per non jejunum.

1. If before the consecration the priest falls gravely ill, or faints, or dies, the Mass is left off; if this happens after the consecration of the Body only, before that of the Blood, or after both have been consecrated, the Mass shall be completed by another priest from the place where he left off, and in case of necessity even by one not fasting.

2. Si non obierit, sed fuerit infirmus, adeo tamen ut possit communicare, et non adsit alia hostia consecrata: Sacerdos, qui Missam supplet, dividat hostiam, et unam partem praebeat infirmo, aliam ipse sumat.

2. If he has not died, but is ill, yet so that he can communicate, and no other consecrated host is at hand, the priest who completes the Mass shall divide the host, give one part to the sick priest, and consume the other himself.

3. Si, semiprolata forma Corporis, obiit Sacerdos: quia non est facta consecratio, non est necesse, ut Missa per alium suppleatur.

3. If the priest dies when the form of the Body is half spoken, since the consecration has not been made, it is not necessary that the Mass be completed by another.

4. Si vero obierit, semiprolata forma Sanguinis: tunc alter similiter prosequatur Missam, et super eumdem calicem repetat integram formam, ab eo loco: Simili modo postquam cenatum est, vel super alium calicem praeparatum integram formam proferre debet, et hostiam primi Sacerdotis, et Sanguinem a se consecratum sumere, ac deinde calicem relictum semiconsecratum.

4. But if he dies when the form of the Blood is half spoken, another shall likewise continue the Mass and repeat the whole form over the same chalice, from the words Simili modo postquam cenatum est; or he must say the whole form over another chalice made ready, consume the host of the first priest and the Blood he has consecrated himself, and then the half-consecrated chalice that was left.

5. Si quis, extra hujusmodi casus necessitatis, integra Sacramenta non sumpserit, gravissime peccat.

5. If anyone, outside such cases of necessity, does not consume the Sacrament entire, he sins most gravely.

XIV De defectu admixtionis alicujus nocivi in calice, vel hostiaOf something harmful falling into the chalice or on the host

1. Si musca, vel aranea, vel aliquid aliud ceciderit in calicem ante consecrationem: projiciat vinum subtus altare, vel alium locum decentem, et aliud ponat in calice, misceat parum aquae, offerat, ut supra, et prosequatur Missam.

1. If a fly, a spider or anything else falls into the chalice before the consecration, he shall pour the wine out under the altar or in some other fitting place, put other wine in the chalice, mix in a little water, offer it as above, and continue the Mass.

2. Si post consecrationem ceciderit musca, aut aliquid ejusmodi, et fiat nausea Sacerdoti: extrahat eam, et lavet cum vino; finita Missa, comburat, et combustio, ac lotio hujusmodi in sacrarium projiciatur. Si autem non fuerit ei nausea, nec ullum periculum timeat: sumat cum sanguine.

2. If a fly or the like falls in after the consecration, and the priest feels nausea, he shall take it out and wash it with wine; when Mass is finished he shall burn it, and the ashes and the washing shall be thrown into the sacrarium. But if he feels no nausea and fears no danger, he shall consume it with the Blood.

3. Si aliquid venenosum ceciderit in calicem, vel quod provocaret vomitum: vinum consecratum reponendum est in alio calice, et aliud vinum cum aqua apponendum, denuo consecrandum, et, finita Missa, sanguis repositus in panno lineo, vel stupa, tamdiu servetur, donec species vini fuerint desiccatae; et tunc stupa comburatur, et combustio in sacrarium projiciatur.

3. If something poisonous, or something that would cause vomiting, falls into the chalice, the consecrated wine is to be put in another chalice, and other wine with water set out to be consecrated anew; and when Mass is finished the Blood so put aside shall be kept in a linen cloth or tow until the species of wine have dried; then the tow shall be burnt and the ashes thrown into the sacrarium.

4. Si aliquod venenatum contigerit hostiam consecratam: tunc alteram, facta oblatione ut supra, consecret, et sumat modo, quo dictum est; et illa servetur in tabernaculo, loco separato, donec species corrumpantur, et corruptae deinde mittantur in sacrarium; et animal captum occidatur, et comburatur, ut supra dictum est.

4. If something poisonous has touched the consecrated host, he shall consecrate another, having made the offering as above, and consume it in the way described; and the first shall be kept in the tabernacle in a separate place until the species corrupt, and once corrupted they shall be put into the sacrarium; and the animal, if caught, shall be killed and burnt, as was said above.

XV De defectibus fractionis hostiae, aut congelationis SanguinisOf the breaking of the host and the freezing of the Blood

1. Si hostia ante consecrationem inveniatur fracta, nisi populo evidenter appareat, talis hostia consecretur.

1. If the host is found broken before the consecration, unless this is evident to the people, such a host shall be consecrated.

2. Si autem scandalum populo esse possit, alia accipiatur, et offeratur: quod si illius hostiae jam erat facta oblatio, eam post ablutionem sumat.

2. But if it could be a scandal to the people, another shall be taken and offered; and if the offering of that host had already been made, he shall consume it after the ablution.

3. Quod si ante oblationem hostia appareat confracta, accipiatur altera integra, si citra scandalum, aut longam moram fieri poterit.

3. If before the offering the host appears broken, another whole one shall be taken, if this can be done without scandal or long delay.

4. Si propter frigus, vel negligentiam hostia consecrata dilabatur in calicem: propterea nihil est reiterandum; sed Sacerdos Missam prosequatur, omittendo signa, et caeremonias, quae solent fieri cum ipsa; et sumat simul Corpus cum Sanguine, dicens: Corpus, et Sanguis.

4. If through cold or carelessness the consecrated host slips into the chalice, nothing is to be repeated on that account; but the priest shall continue the Mass, leaving out the signs and ceremonies usually made with it, and shall consume the Body together with the Blood, saying Corpus, et Sanguis.

5. Si, sumendo Sanguinem, particula remanserit in calice: digito ad labium calicis eam adducat, et sumat ante purificationem; vel infundat vinum, et sumat.

5. If in consuming the Blood a particle remains in the chalice, he shall bring it with his finger to the lip of the chalice and consume it before the purification, or pour in wine and consume it.

6. Si in hieme Sanguis congeletur in calice, involvatur calix pannis calefactis; si id non proficeret, ponatur in ferventi aqua prope altare, dummodo in calicem non intret, donec liquefiat.

6. If in winter the Blood freezes in the chalice, the chalice shall be wrapped in warmed cloths; if that does not help, it shall be set in hot water near the altar, provided the water does not get into the chalice, until it melts.

XVI De defectibus effusionis Sanguinis, aut casus hostiaeOf the spilling of the Blood or the falling of the host

1. Si per negligentiam aliquid de Sanguine Christi ceciderit: si quidem super terram, vel tabulam, lingua lambatur; et locus ipse radatur, quantum satis est, et abrasio comburatur; cinis vero in sacrarium recondatur.

1. If through carelessness some of the Blood of Christ falls: if on the ground or on a board, it shall be taken up with the tongue, and the place scraped as much as is needed, and the scrapings burnt, the ashes being put into the sacrarium.

2. Si vero super lapidem altaris ceciderit: sorbeat Sacerdos stillam, et locus bene abluatur, et ablutio in sacrarium projiciatur.

2. If it falls on the altar stone, the priest shall take up the drop, and the place shall be well washed, and the washing thrown into the sacrarium.

3. Si super linteum altaris, et ad aliud linteum stilla pervenerit: si usque ad tertium, linteamina ter abluantur, ubi stilla ceciderit, calice supposito, et aqua ablutionis in sacrarium projiciatur.

3. If it falls on the altar cloth and the drop reaches another cloth, or as far as the third, the cloths shall be washed three times where the drop fell, with the chalice held beneath, and the water of the washing thrown into the sacrarium.

4. Quod si in ipso solum corporali, aut si in vestibus ipsis sacerdotalibus ceciderit, debent similiter ablui, et ablutio in sacrarium projici.

4. If it falls on the corporal alone, or on the priestly vestments, they must likewise be washed, and the washing thrown into the sacrarium.

5. Si in substrato pedibus panno, vel tapete, ablui oportebit, ut supra.

5. If on the cloth or carpet beneath the feet, it must be washed as above.

6. At si contingat totum Sanguinem post consecrationem effundi: si quidem aliquid vel parum remansit, illud sumatur, et de effuso reliquo Sanguine fiat, ut dictum est supra.

6. If it should happen that the whole of the Blood is spilt after the consecration: if anything, even a little, remains, it shall be consumed, and with the rest of the Blood that was spilt it shall be done as said above.

7. Si vero nihil omnino remansit, ponat iterum vinum, et aquam, et consecret ab eo loco: Simili modo postquam cenatum est, facta prius tamen calicis oblatione, saltem mentali, ut supra dictum est.

7. If nothing at all remains, he shall put in wine and water again and consecrate from the words Simili modo postquam cenatum est, having first made the offering of the chalice, at least mentally, as said above.

8. Si Sacerdos evomat Eucharistiam: si species integrae appareant, reverenter sumantur, nisi nausea fiat: tunc enim species consecratae caute separentur, et in aliquo loco sacro reponantur, donec corrumpantur; et postea in sacrarium ponantur.

8. If the priest vomits the Eucharist: if the species appear whole, they shall be reverently consumed, unless nausea arises; for then the consecrated species shall be carefully separated and put in some sacred place until they corrupt, and afterwards put into the sacrarium.

9. Quod si species non appareant: comburatur vomitus, et cineres in sacrarium projiciantur.

9. If the species do not appear, the vomit shall be burnt and the ashes thrown into the sacrarium.

10. Si hostia consecrata, vel aliqua ejus particula dilabatur in terram, reverenter accipiatur; et locus, ubi cecidit, mundetur, et aliquantulum abradatur; et pulvis, seu abrasio hujusmodi in sacrarium projiciatur.

10. If the consecrated host, or any particle of it, falls to the ground, it shall be reverently taken up; and the place where it fell cleaned and scraped a little, and the dust or scrapings thrown into the sacrarium.

11. Si ceciderit extra corporale in mappam, seu alio quovis modo in aliquod linteum: mappa, vel linteum hujusmodi diligenter lavetur, et lotio ipsa in sacrarium effundatur.

11. If it falls outside the corporal onto the altar cloth, or in any other way onto some linen, the cloth or linen shall be carefully washed, and the washing poured into the sacrarium.

Possunt etiam defectus in ministerio ipso occurrere, si Sacerdos ignoret ritus, et caeremonias ipsas in eo servandas, de quibus omnibus in sequentibus Rubricis copiose dicetur.

Defects may also occur in the ministry itself if the priest does not know the rites and ceremonies to be observed in it, of all of which it will be fully spoken in the following rubrics.

Quomodo sacerdos se praeparare debeat ad MissamHow the priest is to prepare himself for Mass

I De praeparationeThe preparation

Cum Missam celebrare voluerit, suae conscientiae primo consulat, considerando si in aliquo offenderit Deum, et proximum suum. Postmodum, praevia debita praeparatione, confiteatur peccata sua, antequam induat sacras vestes, Confessore sedente, et ipso genuflexo, discooperto capite, ita ut mundus ad altare accedat, et peracta poenitentia, orationi aliquantulum vacet, dicendo Orationes inferius positas, pro temporis opportunitate.

When he wishes to celebrate Mass, he shall first consult his conscience, considering whether he has offended God or his neighbour in anything. Then, after due preparation, he shall confess his sins before putting on the sacred vestments, the confessor sitting and he kneeling with head uncovered, so that he may come clean to the altar; and having done his penance, he shall give himself a little to prayer, saying the prayers set down below as time allows.

II Quod in sacristia facturus sitWhat he is to do in the sacristy

Cum ad sacristiam celebraturus advenerit, Matutino saltem absoluto, invocato Divino nomine, quo hoc in opere quam maxime, si alias umquam, ipsi procul dubio opus est, accipiat Missale, perquirat Missam, perlegat, et signacula ordinet ad ea, quae dicturus est. Postea abluat diligenter manus, et vultum, dicendo: Da Domine virtutem manibus meis ad abstergendam omnem maculam, ut sine pollutione mentis et corporis valeam tibi servire. Amen. Nec quidquam vel indecens, aut indecorum, ac minus comptum ad Missam audiendam adventantium aspectibus appareat.

When the priest who is to celebrate comes to the sacristy, Matins at least being finished, having called upon the divine Name, which he needs in this work beyond doubt more than in any other, he shall take the Missal, find the Mass, read it through, and set the markers at what he is to say. Then he shall carefully wash his hands and face, saying: Da Domine virtutem manibus meis ad abstergendam omnem maculam, ut sine pollutione mentis et corporis valeam tibi servire. Amen. And nothing unbecoming, unseemly or untidy shall appear to the eyes of those coming to hear Mass.

Deinde calicem praeparet, diligenter ipsum extergendo purificatorio mundo ad hoc praeparato. Purificatorium super os ejus ponat, et super illud patenam cum hostia, quam ab omni scabritie diligenter mundabit, ut non habeat, quod indignum sacrificio judicetur, utpote non fracta, non referta maculis, non perterebrata foraminibus. Ipsam parva palla, et velo serico tegat, et super velum ponat bursam coloris paramentorum, intus habentem corporale plicatum, velique partem anteriorem super bursam reducat.

Then he shall prepare the chalice, carefully wiping it with a clean purificator kept for this. He shall lay the purificator over its mouth, and on it the paten with the host, which he shall carefully clean of every roughness, so that it has nothing that might be judged unworthy of the sacrifice, being neither broken, nor spotted, nor pierced with holes. He shall cover it with the small pall and a silk veil, and on the veil set the burse of the colour of the vestments, with the corporal folded inside it, and draw the front of the veil back over the burse.

His ita dispositis, ad induendum sacras vestes accedens, sit calceatus pedibus, et indutus vestibus omnibus albis Ordinis, et signo Crucis se muniens dicat: In nomine Patris, et Filii, et Spiritus Sancti. Amen. Deinde accipiens amictum circa extremitates, et cordulas, Crucem in medio signando, eum exosculetur, et capiti imponens, dicat: Impone Domine capiti meo galeam salutis, ad expugnandos omnes diabolicos incursus. Amen. Illo caputium circumtegat, et cordulas in modulum crucis circumducens sub brachiis, ad cingulum firmet; aut, si longae fuerint, circumducens per dorsum ante pectus reducat, ac liget: colloquens Deo per orationes, et sanctissimae passionis Christi meditationem.

These things being arranged, coming to put on the sacred vestments, he shall be shod, and clothed in all the white habit of the Order, and signing himself with the cross he shall say: In nomine Patris, et Filii, et Spiritus Sancti. Amen. Then, taking the amice by its ends and strings, he shall kiss the cross in the middle and, putting it on his head, say: Impone Domine capiti meo galeam salutis, ad expugnandos omnes diabolicos incursus. Amen. With it he shall cover the capuce all round, and drawing the strings crosswise under the arms, fasten them at the cincture; or, if they are long, draw them round the back, bring them before the breast and tie them, speaking with God in prayer and in meditation on the most holy Passion of Christ.

Deinde vestem albam induturus, dicat: Dealba me Domine, et munda cor meum, ut in sanguine Agni dealbatus, gaudiis perfruar sempiternis. Amen. Insuper cingulo se cingens, duas tantum extremitates cinguli demittat, ac dicat: Praecinge me Domine cingulo puritatis, et exstingue in lumbis meis humorem libidinis, ut maneat in me virtus continentiae et castitatis. Amen. Minister autem elevet albam nimis longam super cingulum circumcirca, ut honeste pendeat, et tegat vestes, ac ejus fimbrias diligenter aptet, ut ad latitudinem digiti, vel circiter, prope terram aequaliter fluat.

Then, putting on the alb, he shall say: Dealba me Domine, et munda cor meum, ut in sanguine Agni dealbatus, gaudiis perfruar sempiternis. Amen. Girding himself with the cincture, he shall let only its two ends hang down, and say: Praecinge me Domine cingulo puritatis, et exstingue in lumbis meis humorem libidinis, ut maneat in me virtus continentiae et castitatis. Amen. The server shall draw up the alb, if too long, all round over the cincture, so that it hangs becomingly and covers the habit, and carefully arrange its hem so that it falls evenly about a finger's breadth from the ground.

Postea manipulum exosculans in medio, ubi Crux posita esse debet, inducat sinistro brachio, dicendo: Merear Domine portare manipulum fletus et doloris, ut cum exsultatione recipiam mercedem laboris. Amen. Praeterea stolam, simili modo deosculando ejus medium, collo imponat, dicendo: Redde mihi Domine, obsecro, stolam immortalitatis, quam perdidi in praevaricatione primi parentis, et quamvis indignus accedo ad tuum sacrum mysterium, merear tamen gaudium sempiternum. Amen. Ac transversando eam ante pectus in modum crucis, ducat partem a sinistro humero pendentem ad dexteram, et partem a dextero humero pendentem ad sinistram, sicque, cinguli pendentibus extremitatibus ab utroque latere, stolam firmet. Tandem casulam decenter induat, dicendo: Domine, qui dixisti: Jugum meum suave est, et onus meum leve: fac ut istud portare sic valeam, quod consequar tuam gratiam. Amen.

Then, kissing the maniple in the middle, where the cross should be, he shall put it on his left arm, saying: Merear Domine portare manipulum fletus et doloris, ut cum exsultatione recipiam mercedem laboris. Amen. Next he shall put the stole on his neck, kissing its middle in the same way, and saying: Redde mihi Domine, obsecro, stolam immortalitatis, quam perdidi in praevaricatione primi parentis, et quamvis indignus accedo ad tuum sacrum mysterium, merear tamen gaudium sempiternum. Amen. And crossing it before the breast, he shall bring the part hanging from the left shoulder to the right, and the part hanging from the right shoulder to the left, and so fasten the stole with the hanging ends of the cincture on either side. Lastly he shall put on the chasuble becomingly, saying: Domine, qui dixisti: Jugum meum suave est, et onus meum leve: fac ut istud portare sic valeam, quod consequar tuam gratiam. Amen.

Calicem dehinc manu laeva accipiens per nodum, infra cuppam, altera manu bursam corporalis cum velo reducto et patena teneat, ita quod pollex calicem tangat, reliqui autem digiti suprapositi corporale et patenam comprehendant. Et sic reverentiam, inclinato capite, Cruci, vel imagini, quae in sacristia reperitur, exhibendo, ad altare, cooperto capite caputio et amictu, oculis demissis, incessu gravi, erectoque corpore, Deum gemitibus exorando, progrediatur.

Then, taking the chalice in his left hand by the knot below the cup, he shall hold with the other hand the burse of the corporal, with the veil drawn back, and the paten, so that the thumb touches the chalice and the other fingers, laid above, hold the corporal and the paten. And so, bowing his head in reverence to the cross or image that is in the sacristy, he shall go to the altar with his head covered by the capuce and amice, eyes cast down, with grave step and upright body, praying to God with sighs.

Quod si interim ante tabernaculum sacratissimi Christi Corporis transire contigerit, cooperto, ut dictum est, capite, dexterum genu flectat: si ante altare, ubi celebratur Missa, contingat elevari, tunc utrumque genu flectat, et, detecto capite, illud adoret, neque antea surgat, quam celebrans deposuerit calicem super corporale. Quod si in altari patenter sit expositum, vel in illo ministretur, pariter utrumque genu flectat, ac, detecto capite, illud adoret; et mox se erigens, eo modo, quo dictum est, incedere pergat.

If meanwhile he happens to pass before the tabernacle of the most holy Body of Christ, he shall genuflect on the right knee, his head covered as said. If he happens to pass an altar where Mass is being celebrated at the elevation, he shall kneel on both knees and, uncovering his head, adore, and not rise before the celebrant has put the chalice down on the corporal. If the Sacrament is openly exposed on an altar, or is being given from it, he shall likewise kneel on both knees and, uncovering his head, adore; and rising at once, go on in the way described.

Cum vero ad altare pervenerit, inclinatione profunda, genibus non curvatis, ad medium ejus facta; vel flexo unico genu, si in eo sit tabernaculum sanctissimi Sacramenti; vel utroque, si sit expositum: ascendat ad medium altaris, et ibi antequam calicem imponat, eum mox versus cornu Evangelii seponat, quo detur explicationi corporalis locus, quod de bursa accipiens, reverenter explicet in medio altaris. Deinde, amoto velo, et palla, patenam cum hostia super corporale deponat; e calice purificatorium auferens, juxta corporalis partem dexteram in longum extendat.

When he comes to the altar, having made a profound bow at its middle without bending the knees, or a genuflexion on one knee if the tabernacle of the most holy Sacrament is on it, or on both if the Sacrament is exposed, he shall go up to the middle of the altar, and there, before setting down the chalice, at once put it aside towards the Gospel corner, to leave room for unfolding the corporal, which he takes from the burse and reverently unfolds in the middle of the altar. Then, removing the veil and pall, he shall lay the paten with the host on the corporal; and taking the purificator from the chalice, spread it lengthwise beside the right side of the corporal.

Deinde praeparaturus calicem, eum per medium nodi sinistra manu tenens, ampullam vini de manu ministri dextera accipiens, parum vini infundat: deinde, porrigente ministro ampullam aquae, et dicente: Benedicite, signo Crucis ipsam consignet, dicendo: In nomine Patris, et Filii, et Spiritus Sancti, et Minister respondeat: Amen. Postea tantam quantitatem aquae distillet in calicem, quae facillime tota possit in vinum converti; deinde calicem purificatorio abstergat: et sic praeparato, patenam cum hostia imponat, et palla veloque cooperiat, et super corporale in medio altaris collocet; et ad Missale super cussino ad cornu Epistolae parato se conferat, aperiat, et reperiat Missam.

Then, to prepare the chalice, holding it by the middle of the knot with his left hand, and taking the cruet of wine from the server with his right, he shall pour in a little wine; then, the server holding out the cruet of water and saying Benedicite, he shall sign it with the cross, saying: In nomine Patris, et Filii, et Spiritus Sancti; and the server answers: Amen. Then he shall let fall into the chalice so little water that it can very easily be wholly changed into wine; then wipe the chalice with the purificator; and the chalice thus prepared, he shall put on it the paten with the host, cover it with the pall and veil, and set it on the corporal in the middle of the altar; and he shall go to the Missal, made ready on the cushion at the Epistle corner, open it and find the Mass.

Quo facto, revertatur, junctis ante pectus manibus, ad medium altaris, et ibi ambabus manibus caput discooperiat, et caputium decenter aptando, secreto ac humiliter orans dicat: Actiones nostras, quaesumus Domine, aspirando praeveni, et adjuvando prosequere: ut cuncta nostra operatio a te semper incipiat, et per te coepta finiatur. Per Christum Dominum nostrum. Amen. Deinde attente, ac devote ad Missae celebrationem progrediatur.

This done, he shall return with hands joined before the breast to the middle of the altar, and there uncover his head with both hands and, arranging the capuce becomingly, pray secretly and humbly: Actiones nostras, quaesumus Domine, aspirando praeveni, et adjuvando prosequere: ut cuncta nostra operatio a te semper incipiat, et per te coepta finiatur. Per Christum Dominum nostrum. Amen. Then he shall go on attentively and devoutly to the celebration of Mass.

Modus et Ritus dicendi MissamThe manner and rite of saying Mass

Missale ponit Missam Epiphaniae Domini exempli gratia; textus orationum litteris signatur, et rubricae iisdem litteris respondent. Hic rubricae cum verbis orationum, ad quae pertinent, proponuntur.

The Missal takes the Mass of the Epiphany as its example; the text of the prayers is marked with letters, and the rubrics answer to the same letters. Here each rubric is set out with the words of the prayer to which it belongs.

I Ad initium MissaeAt the beginning of Mass

Sacerdos junctis manibus ante pectus, stans immediate infra supremum gradum, vel infra gradum, si fuerit unicus, ante medium altaris, facta profunda inclinatione, incipiat clara et intelligibili voce: In nomine Patris, et Filii, et Spiritus Sancti. Amen, dextera manu signo Crucis se muniens a fronte usque ad pectus, et ab humero sinistro usque ad dexterum.

The priest, with hands joined before his breast, standing immediately below the top step (or below the step, if there is only one) before the middle of the altar, having made a profound bow, begins in a clear and audible voice: In nomine Patris, et Filii, et Spiritus Sancti. Amen, signing himself with his right hand from the forehead to the breast and from the left shoulder to the right.

Junctis manibus, eadem voce dicat: Confitemini Domino quoniam bonus. Minister respondeat: Quoniam in saeculum misericordia ejus. Inclinans profunde, junctis manibus ante pectus, voce mediocri dicat: Confiteor. Pectus non tundat ad mea culpa. Minister dicat: Misereatur tui. Sacerdos respondeat: Amen. Minister dicat: Confiteor (et tibi, Pater). Pectus non tundat.

With hands joined he says in the same voice: Confitemini Domino quoniam bonus. The server answers: Quoniam in saeculum misericordia ejus. Bowing profoundly, with hands joined before his breast, he says in a moderate voice: Confiteor. He does not strike his breast at mea culpa. The server says: Misereatur tui. The priest answers: Amen. The server says the Confiteor (with et tibi, Pater); he does not strike his breast.

Sacerdos dicat: Misereatur vestri. Minister respondeat: Amen. Sacerdos dicat: Absolutionem. Signum Crucis ne faciat. Minister respondeat: Amen. Erectus omnino, junctis manibus ante pectus, dicat: Adjutorium nostrum in nomine Domini, sicque ad altare procedat. Minister respondeat: Qui fecit caelum et terram.

The priest says: Misereatur vestri. The server answers: Amen. The priest says: Absolutionem; he does not make the sign of the cross. The server answers: Amen. Standing fully erect, with hands joined before his breast, he says: Adjutorium nostrum in nomine Domini, and so goes up to the altar. The server answers: Qui fecit caelum et terram.

Inclinans profunde directe versus altare, junctis manibus dicat: Aufer a nobis. Ad Per Christum Dominum nostrum signum Crucis pollice in medio altaris formans, illud exosculetur, extensis hinc et inde manibus, ac super altare positis. Erectus ante altaris medium, signans se signo Crucis ut supra, dicat secreto: In nomine Patris, et Filii, et Spiritus Sancti. Amen; deinde junctis manibus ut prius, ad librum in cornu Epistolae accedat.

Bowing profoundly straight towards the altar, with hands joined, he says: Aufer a nobis. At Per Christum Dominum nostrum, making a cross with his thumb in the middle of the altar, he kisses it, his hands extended on either side and laid on the altar. Standing erect before the middle of the altar, signing himself as above, he says secretly: In nomine Patris, et Filii, et Spiritus Sancti. Amen; then, with hands joined as before, he goes to the book at the Epistle corner.

II Ad Introitum, Kyrie et GloriaAt the Introit, Kyrie and Gloria

Clara et intelligibili voce incipiat Introitum. Ad Gloria Patri aliquantulum capite versus Crucem inclinato, reverentiam exhibeat. Deinde repetat Introitum usque ad Versum exclusive. Kyrie eleison, etc. dicat alternatim cum ministro.

In a clear and audible voice he begins the Introit. At Gloria Patri, bowing his head a little towards the cross, he shows reverence. Then he repeats the Introit up to the verse exclusively. He says the Kyrie eleison, etc., alternately with the server.

Dicto ultimo Kyrie, ad medium altaris, si Gloria dicendum est, junctis manibus, pergat: sin minus, ibi ante librum consistat, et reliqua prosequatur. Ad Gloria manus extensas super altare deponat; ad in excelsis eleventur; ad Deo jungantur; et caput inclinans, revertatur ad cornu Epistolae, et ibi prosequatur reliqua, legens in libro, junctis ante pectus manibus. Ad Adoramus te, Gratias agimus tibi, Jesu Christe, suscipe deprecationem nostram, et iterum ad Jesu Christe caput inclinet.

The last Kyrie said, if the Gloria is to be said he goes with hands joined to the middle of the altar; if not, he stays there before the book and continues the rest. At Gloria he lays his extended hands on the altar; at in excelsis they are raised; at Deo they are joined; and bowing his head, he returns to the Epistle corner and there continues the rest reading from the book, with hands joined before his breast. At Adoramus te, Gratias agimus tibi, Jesu Christe, suscipe deprecationem nostram, and again at Jesu Christe, he bows his head.

III Ad OrationesAt the Collects

In eodem loco vertat se ad populum a sinistro latere ad dexterum corporis, id est, per eam partem, quae respicit cornu Epistolae (excepto quando celebrat in altari, in quo est SS. Eucharistiae Sacramentum), manibus rectis, elevatis ut humeros non excedant, extensis ut a retro videri queant: quod in omni manuum elevatione, et extensione observetur, nisi ad Unde et memores. Dicat: Dominus vobiscum. Manus conjungat. Minister respondeat: Et cum spiritu tuo.

In the same place he turns to the people from the left side to the right of his body, that is, by the side towards the Epistle corner (except when he celebrates on an altar on which is the Sacrament of the Holy Eucharist), with his hands straight, raised so that they do not go above the shoulders, and extended so that they may be seen from behind; this is to be observed whenever the hands are raised and extended, except at Unde et memores. He says: Dominus vobiscum, and joins his hands. The server answers: Et cum spiritu tuo.

Deinde reversus ad librum per idem latus, manibus junctis, inclinans paululum caput versus Crucem altaris, dicat: Oremus. Aperiantur manus ad Orationem: sicque, cum alia memoria dicenda fuerint, faciat ad Oremus, et ad Orationem, ut modo diximus. Ad Per Dominum nostrum conjungat manus, ac reverentiam Nomini Dei exhibeat, capite versus Crucem altaris paululum inclinato.

Then, returning to the book by the same side, with hands joined, bowing his head a little towards the cross of the altar, he says: Oremus. His hands are opened for the Collect; and when other commemorations are to be said, he does at the Oremus and at the prayer as just described. At Per Dominum nostrum he joins his hands and shows reverence to the Name of God, bowing his head a little towards the cross of the altar.

Si in Orationibus praemittendum sit post Oremus, Flectamus genua (Minister respondeat: Levate), accipiat utraque manu anteriorem partem casulae, hinc et inde, et super altare aptans, flectat genua, moxque surgens, ut supra dicat Orationem.

If after Oremus the Flectamus genua is to be said before the Collects (the server answering Levate), he takes the front of the chasuble on either side with both hands and, laying it on the altar, kneels, and rising at once says the Collect as above.

IV Ab Epistola ad EvangeliumFrom the Epistle to the Gospel

Cancellatis manibus super librum, aut super altare firmatis, legat Epistolam, seu Lectionem. Ad Graduale elevet manus, et conjunctas teneat usque ad Evangelii prolationem. Junctis manibus ante pectus dicat Graduale, Alleluia vel Tractum, et Sequentiam.

With his hands crossed upon the book, or resting on the altar, he reads the Epistle or Lesson. At the Gradual he raises his hands, and keeps them joined until the Gospel is pronounced. With hands joined before his breast he says the Gradual, the Alleluia or Tract, and the Sequence.

Finito Alleluia, vel Tractu, minister deferat Missale ad cornu Evangelii, et Sacerdos junctis manibus eo progrediatur, reverentiam exhibens Cruci in medio altaris, et dicens secreto: Dominus sit in corde meo, et in labiis meis, ad pronuntiandum sanctum Evangelium pacis. Missale autem ita collocet, ut caput ipsius ad cornu altaris respiciat.

When the Alleluia or Tract is finished, the server carries the Missal to the Gospel corner, and the priest goes there with hands joined, showing reverence to the cross in the middle of the altar and saying secretly: Dominus sit in corde meo, et in labiis meis, ad pronuntiandum sanctum Evangelium pacis. The server places the Missal so that its top faces the corner of the altar.

V Ad EvangeliumAt the Gospel

Manus junctas Missali decenter imponat, et dicat: Dominus vobiscum. Minister respondeat: Et cum spiritu tuo. Ad Sequentia sancti Evangelii Evangelio signum Crucis pollice dexterae manus imprimat. Minister respondeat: Gloria tibi Domine. Parvis signis Crucis pollice frontem, os, et pectus signet; deinde majori signo Crucis manu dextera se totum muniat, a fronte ad pectus et humeros.

He lays his joined hands becomingly on the Missal and says: Dominus vobiscum. The server answers: Et cum spiritu tuo. At Sequentia sancti Evangelii he signs the Gospel with the cross with the thumb of his right hand. The server answers: Gloria tibi Domine. With small crosses of the thumb he signs his forehead, mouth and breast; then with a larger sign of the cross with his right hand he signs his whole person, from forehead to breast and shoulders.

Manus junctas iterum Missali imponat, et sic totum Evangelium prosequatur, excepto cum dicet: Et procidentes adoraverunt eum; nam tunc genu flectat versus librum, quod semper observet dum in Evangelio erit genuflectendum: cum autem nomen Jesu, vel Mariae pronuntiat, inclinet caput.

He lays his joined hands again on the Missal and so continues the whole Gospel, except when he says Et procidentes adoraverunt eum; for then he genuflects towards the book, which he is always to do when there is a genuflexion in the Gospel; and when he says the name of Jesus or of Mary he bows his head.

Ad finem Evangelii majori Crucis signo se iterum signet. Deinde Evangelium signans Crucis signo, dicat secreto: Per Evangelica dicta deleantur nostra delicta, Evangelium exosculando, praeterquam in Missis Defunctorum, et cum Passiones dicuntur.

At the end of the Gospel he signs himself again with the larger sign of the cross. Then, signing the Gospel with the cross, he says secretly: Per Evangelica dicta deleantur nostra delicta, kissing the Gospel, except in Masses for the Dead and when the Passions are read.

VI Ad CredoAt the Credo

Ad medium altaris, positis manibus, et extensis super altare, dicat: Credo; ad in unum elevet manus; ad Deum conjungat, et caput Cruci inclinet, et statim revertatur ad cornu Evangelii, et ibi in libro reliqua prosequatur, manibus ante pectus junctis. Ad Jesum Christum caput inclinet versus librum.

At the middle of the altar, his hands laid extended on the altar, he says: Credo; at in unum he raises his hands; at Deum he joins them and bows his head to the cross, and returns at once to the Gospel corner, and there continues the rest in the book with hands joined before his breast. At Jesum Christum he bows his head towards the book.

Ad Et incarnatus est veniat ad medium altaris, et genua flectat, et dicto Homo factus est, surgat, et manus, ut prius, jungat, et revertatur statim ad librum, reliqua perlecturus; ad simul adoratur caput inclinet; ad Et vitam venturi saeculi signet se signo Crucis a fronte ad pectus et humeros.

At Et incarnatus est he comes to the middle of the altar and kneels, and having said Homo factus est he rises, joins his hands as before, and returns at once to the book to read the rest; at simul adoratur he bows his head; at Et vitam venturi saeculi he signs himself with the cross from forehead to breast and shoulders.

VII Ad OffertoriumAt the Offertory

Et ad medium altaris veniens, et ad populum versus aperiens, et conjungens manus, ut ad primum, dicat: Dominus vobiscum, et per eamdem viam revertatur ad medium. Minister respondeat: Et cum spiritu tuo. Apertis manibus, et elevatis, caput Cruci inclinans incipiat: Oremus, et ad ultimam syllabam conjungat ante pectus.

And coming to the middle of the altar and turning to the people, opening and joining his hands as at the first, he says: Dominus vobiscum, and returns to the middle by the same way. The server answers: Et cum spiritu tuo. With hands opened and raised, bowing his head to the cross, he begins: Oremus, and at the last syllable joins them before his breast.

Manibus junctis, vadat ad librum, et Offertorio dicto, librum appropinquans, ad medium altaris regrediatur, manuque laeva, et dextera velum cum palla e calice levet, et ad latus Epistolae seponat, et postea manus in altare demittat, et dicat Quid retribuam, elevando ipsas, et ad Calicem salutaris conjungendo.

With hands joined he goes to the book, and having said the Offertory, bringing the book nearer, he returns to the middle of the altar; with his left hand and his right he lifts the veil with the pall from the chalice and sets it aside on the Epistle side; then he lays his hands on the altar and says Quid retribuam, raising them, and at Calicem salutaris joining them.

Ad Suscipe sancta Trinitas Calicem cum patena, et hostia supraposita, accipiat ambabus manibus, et elevatum super corporale usque ad pectus, manu dextera medium calicis, laeva autem pedem tenens, dicat, intentis sursum ad Deum oculis. Deponat dextera super corporale in medio calicem: et mox sinistra patenam e calice levet, et dextera etiam patenae adhibita, hostiam ante calicis pedem demittat taliter, quod ipsum non tangat. Patenam ad dexteram corporalis super medietatem purificatorii, et sub corporali reponat. Deinde palla calicem cooperiat.

At Suscipe sancta Trinitas he takes the chalice, with the paten and the host upon it, in both hands, and raising it above the corporal to the height of his breast, holding the middle of the chalice with the right hand and the foot with the left, he says the prayer with his eyes raised to God. With his right hand he sets the chalice down on the corporal in the middle; and at once with his left he lifts the paten from the chalice, and, putting his right hand also to the paten, lets the host down before the foot of the chalice so that it does not touch it. He puts the paten back at the right of the corporal, half over the purificator and under the corporal. Then he covers the chalice with the pall.

Junctisque illico manibus, et reverentia Cruci exhibita capite, ad abluendas manus ad cornu Epistolae accedat, dicens: Lavabo inter innocentes... Domine dilexi decorem domus tuae, et locum habitationis gloriae tuae. Et non plus ultra. Lotis prioribus quatuor digitis, et extersis, revertatur, junctis manibus ante pectus, ad medium altaris; quo cum pervenerit, incipiat In spiritu humilitatis, inclinando se ad altare profunde, et ad et placeat tibi erigat se. Digitis, quibus Corpus Domini tractaturus est, ne vertat folia, aut aliquid tangat.

With hands joined at once, and bowing his head to the cross, he goes to the Epistle corner to wash his hands, saying: Lavabo inter innocentes... Domine dilexi decorem domus tuae, et locum habitationis gloriae tuae; and no further. Having washed the first four fingers and dried them, he returns with hands joined before his breast to the middle of the altar; when he has arrived there, he begins In spiritu humilitatis, bowing profoundly to the altar, and at et placeat tibi he stands erect. With the fingers with which he is to handle the Body of the Lord he is not to turn the pages or touch anything.

Ad populum conversus, elevatis et extensis manibus, voce mediocri dicat: Orate fratres. Junctis manibus ante pectus, secreto prosequens: ut meum, ac vestrum pariter in conspectu Domini sit acceptum sacrificium, versus cornu Evangelii perficiens circulum, ad Missale revertatur. Junctis manibus, ut prius, dicat: Domine exaudi orationem meam, et clamor meus ad te veniat. Junctis item manibus, dicat: Oremus; apertis, et extensis, ut supra, dicat Secretam; et ad Per Dominum conjungat, capite paululum inclinato; quod si aliae memoriae sint faciendae, praemittat iterum ante primam memoriam Oremus, manibus junctis, et ad Orationem apertis.

Turned to the people, with hands raised and extended, he says in a moderate voice: Orate fratres. With hands joined before his breast, continuing secretly: ut meum, ac vestrum pariter in conspectu Domini sit acceptum sacrificium, he completes the circle towards the Gospel corner and returns to the Missal. With hands joined as before he says: Domine exaudi orationem meam, et clamor meus ad te veniat. With hands likewise joined he says: Oremus; with them opened and extended as above, he says the Secret; and at Per Dominum he joins them, bowing his head a little; and if other commemorations are to be made, he again says Oremus before the first of them, with hands joined, opening them for the prayer.

VIII Ad PraefationemAt the Preface

Quibus finitis, in medio altaris stans, manibus super altare hinc inde demissis, incipiat clara et intelligibili voce: Per omnia saecula saeculorum. Minister respondeat: Amen. Manibus demissis, ut antea, dicat: Dominus vobiscum. Minister respondeat: Et cum spiritu tuo. Ad Sursum corda elevet manus, et extendat. Minister respondeat: Habemus ad Dominum. Ad Gratias agamus jungat manus, et ad Deo nostro inclinato capite Cruci, eas postea aperiat. Minister respondeat: Dignum et justum est.

These finished, standing in the middle of the altar with his hands laid on the altar on either side, he begins in a clear and audible voice: Per omnia saecula saeculorum. The server answers: Amen. With his hands laid down as before, he says: Dominus vobiscum. The server answers: Et cum spiritu tuo. At Sursum corda he raises his hands and extends them. The server answers: Habemus ad Dominum. At Gratias agamus he joins his hands, and at Deo nostro, his head bowed to the cross, he afterwards opens them. The server answers: Dignum et justum est.

Manibus extensis et elevatis more solito, dicat Praefationem communem, vel propriam, ut dies requirit. Ad supplici confessione dicentes conjungat manus ante pectus. Junctis manibus, ut prius, eadem voce prosequatur: Sanctus, Sanctus, Sanctus. Ad Benedictus, qui venit signo Crucis se muniat a fronte ad pectus et humeros.

With hands extended and raised in the usual way, he says the common or proper Preface as the day requires. At supplici confessione dicentes he joins his hands before his breast. With hands joined as before he continues in the same voice: Sanctus, Sanctus, Sanctus. At Benedictus, qui venit he signs himself with the cross from forehead to breast and shoulders.

IX Ad CanonemAt the Canon

Junctis manibus ante pectus, et inclinando profunde, dicat: Te igitur. Ad uti erigat se, et ad benedicas demittat laevam super altare, et signet dextera manu totum sacrificium, tribus, ut signatum est, crucibus, nempe tribus tantum prioribus digitis extensis, reliquis inflexis versus palmam, et ad In primis manibus junctis ante pectus prosequatur; et ad Papa exprimat nomen proprium Papae (Sede vacante, omittuntur praedicta verba). Et ad Antistite, specificatur nomen proprium Episcopi, in propria Dioecesi, et non alterius Superioris: et si vita functus fuerit, vel cum celebratur Romae, omittuntur praedicta verba. Ad Rege, exprimitur nomen in dominiis alicujus Regis.

With hands joined before his breast and bowing profoundly, he says: Te igitur. At uti he stands erect, and at benedicas he lays his left hand on the altar and with his right signs the whole sacrifice with three crosses, as marked, that is with only the first three fingers extended and the others bent towards the palm; and at In primis he continues with hands joined before his breast; and at Papa he says the proper name of the Pope (when the See is vacant these words are left out). At Antistite the proper name of the Bishop is said, in his own diocese, and not that of any other superior; if he is dead, or when Mass is celebrated in Rome, these words are left out. At Rege the name is said in the dominions of a king.

Item manibus junctis ante pectus dicat: Memento Domine. Post tuarum, junctis manibus, ut antea, oculis demissis, meminerit brevi mora vivorum, quos Deo commendatos voluerit, hoc modo: Patris, Matris, Fratrum, Sororum, Parentum, Benefactorum, Superiorum, et secundum intentionem eorum omnium, pro quibus orare tenetur; et hoc non verbis, sed mente tantum concipiatur; postea reliqua prosequatur.

Likewise with hands joined before his breast he says: Memento Domine. After tuarum, with hands joined as before and eyes lowered, he remembers for a short while the living whom he wishes to commend to God, in this way: his father, mother, brothers, sisters, kindred, benefactors, superiors, and according to the intention of all those for whom he is bound to pray; and this is done not in words but in the mind only; then he continues the rest.

Communicantes dicat extensis et elevatis de more manibus, et ad nomen Mariae, et Jesu caput inclinet. Ad Per eumdem Christum manus jungat, et inclinet caput. Manus extendens et elevans dicat: Hanc igitur. Sicque cetera prosequatur.

He says the Communicantes with hands extended and raised in the usual way, and bows his head at the names of Mary and of Jesus. At Per eumdem Christum he joins his hands and bows his head. Extending and raising his hands he says: Hanc igitur. And so he continues the rest.

Ad Quam oblationem, sinistra super altare demissa, dextera tribus prioribus digitis extensis, ut supra, cruces faciat, tres priores super totum sacrificium, et alias duas, unam ad Corpus super Hostiam tantum, et alteram ad Sanguis super Calicem tantum. Ad Jesu Christi caput inclinet.

At Quam oblationem, his left hand laid on the altar, with the first three fingers of the right extended as above, he makes the crosses: the first three over the whole sacrifice, and the other two, one at Corpus over the Host only, and the other at Sanguis over the chalice only. At Jesu Christi he bows his head.

X Ad consecrationemAt the consecration

Ad Qui pridie extergat extremos pollices et indices manuum super corporale; sinistra manus indice deprimat Hostiam, ut, altera parte elevata, a dextera capi possit, indice et pollice utriusque manus eam tenens; ad elevatis oculis in caelum oculos in caelum levet, et statim demittat, et ad benedixit, sinistra retinens Hostiam inter pollicem et indicem, dextera tribus primis digitis extensis, ut supra, eam semel signo Crucis signet, et verba prosequatur usque ad Accipite; nam tunc iterum pollice et indice dexterae manus accipiet Hostiam.

At Qui pridie he wipes the tips of the thumbs and forefingers of both hands over the corporal; with the forefinger of the left hand he presses on the Host so that, the other side being raised, it can be taken by the right, holding it with the forefinger and thumb of each hand; at elevatis oculis in caelum he raises his eyes to heaven and at once lowers them; and at benedixit, holding the Host with the left hand between thumb and forefinger, he signs it once with the cross with the first three fingers of the right extended, as above, and continues the words as far as Accipite; for then he will take the Host again with the thumb and forefinger of the right hand.

Secreto, attente, devote, et continuate, et cum summa reverentia consecrationis Corporis verba proferat, non super altare procumbens, sed aliquantulum corpore inclinans: quibus prolatis, Hostiam tenens super altare inter pollices et indices utriusque manus, reliquis manuum digitis extensis et simul junctis, genuflexus adoret: moxque se erigens, eam ambabus manibus elevet decenter, quantum commode potest, ut a retro stantibus cerni possit, et intentis in eam semper oculis, populo adorandam offerat, ac sine mora reverenter deponens, prius auferat sinistram manum, ac dextera Hostiam super corporale collocet, ubi erat prius. Reposita Hostia consecrata super corporale, genuflexus iterum eam adoret. Post haec pollicem et indicem utriusque manus conjungat, nec disjungat (nisi quando Hostiam consecratam tangere vel tractare debet) usque post ablutionem digitorum.
