Secretly, attentively, devoutly and without interruption, and with the greatest reverence, he pronounces the words of the consecration of the Body, not leaning on the altar but bending his body a little; having said them, holding the Host above the altar between the thumbs and forefingers of both hands, the other fingers extended and held together, he adores it kneeling; and rising at once, he raises it becomingly with both hands as high as he conveniently can, so that it can be seen by those standing behind, and with his eyes ever fixed on it, he offers it to the people to adore; and setting it down reverently without delay, he first withdraws his left hand and with the right places the Host on the corporal where it was before. The consecrated Host being laid on the corporal, he adores it again kneeling. After this he joins the thumb and forefinger of each hand, and does not separate them (except when he must touch or handle the consecrated Host) until after the ablution of the fingers.

Ad Simili modo discooperiat Calicem, et illico digitos ambarum manuum, quibus Hostiam elevavit, leviter excutiat in Calicem. Ad accipiens et hunc praeclarum Calicem accipiat Calicem dextera manu per medium, et sinistra per pedem: et ad Item tibi gratias agens parum oculos, et Calicem elevet, et statim deponat; et ad benedixit, sinistra tribus extremis digitis firmatis super pedem Calicis, dextera aliis tribus extremis digitis crucem super Calicem a labio ad labium faciat, prosequens verba, quae sequuntur; et ad Accipite, et bibite accipiat dextera digitis, pollice et indice conjunctis a parte anteriori, reliquis tribus a tergo, Calicis medium: quem parum versus se incurvans, ponat pollicem et indicem sinistrae manus super pedem Calicis, reliquos tres digitos subtus pedem, et sic retineat Calicem.

At Simili modo he uncovers the chalice and at once lightly shakes into the chalice the fingers of both hands with which he raised the Host. At accipiens et hunc praeclarum Calicem he takes the chalice by the middle with the right hand and by the foot with the left; at Item tibi gratias agens he raises his eyes a little and the chalice, and at once sets it down; and at benedixit, with the three last fingers of the left hand holding the foot of the chalice steady, with the three last fingers of the right he makes a cross over the chalice from lip to lip, continuing the words that follow; and at Accipite, et bibite he takes the middle of the chalice with the fingers of the right hand, the thumb and forefinger joined in front and the other three behind; and tilting it a little towards himself, he puts the thumb and forefinger of the left hand on the foot of the chalice and the other three fingers under the foot, and so holds the chalice.

Stans, inclinato capite, secreto, attente, continuate, devote, ac reverenter proferat verba consecrationis Sanguinis: Hic est enim calix Sanguinis mei. Quibus prolatis, reponat Calicem super corporale, et genuflexus Sanguinem reverenter adoret. Tum se erigens, Calicem discoopertum semper aspiciendo, ambabus manibus ut prius illum tenens, elevet, ut eum populus adoret; sic tamen, ut pes Calicis caput Sacerdotis non excedat. Deponensque super corporale in priori loco, dicat: Haec quotiescumque feceritis, in mei memoriam facietis, et manu dextera palla eum cooperiat, ac genuflexus Sanguinem adoret.

Standing, with head bowed, secretly, attentively, without interruption, devoutly and reverently he pronounces the words of the consecration of the Blood: Hic est enim calix Sanguinis mei. Having said them, he sets the chalice down on the corporal and, kneeling, reverently adores the Blood. Then, rising, with his eyes always on the uncovered chalice, holding it with both hands as before, he raises it for the people to adore, yet so that the foot of the chalice does not rise above the head of the priest. And setting it down on the corporal in its former place, he says: Haec quotiescumque feceritis, in mei memoriam facietis, covers it with the pall with his right hand, and kneeling adores the Blood.

XI Post consecrationemAfter the consecration

Ad Unde et memores extendat brachia plus solito, mediocriter tamen, usquequo ad signa crucis facienda deponere necesse sit. Ad Hostiam puram sinistram manum super corporale ponat, et dextera, tribus extremis digitis extensis, tres cruces super totum sacrificium faciat, et ad Panem sanctum semel super Hostiam tantum, et ad Calicem salutis semel super Calicem tantum.

At Unde et memores he extends his arms more than usual, but moderately, until he must lower them to make the signs of the cross. At Hostiam puram he puts his left hand on the corporal and with the right, the three last fingers extended, makes three crosses over the whole sacrifice; and at Panem sanctum one over the Host only, and at Calicem salutis one over the chalice only.

Ad Supra quae manibus elevatis et extensis, more solito brachia extendens, dicat. Ad Supplices te rogamus inclinet profunde, cancellatis manibus ante pectus, brachio sinistro supposito dextero: et ad ut quotquot incipiat paulatim se erigere, ut ad altaris participatione possit exosculari dexteram partem corporalis, brachiis adhuc cancellatis: quo facto, erigat se, et sinistram manum super corporale reponat, dextera vero Hostiam ad Corpus benedicat, et ad Sanguinem Calicem, etiam ad benedictione caelesti ante se ipsum dextera a fronte ad pectus et humeros signum Crucis faciat (sinistra posita infra pectus), non tamen se tangens, nec primis tribus digitis, quibus sacrificium signavit, sed pollice et indice clausis, tribus posterioribus digitis: ad Per eumdem jungat manus ante pectus.

At Supra quae he says the prayer with hands raised and extended, spreading his arms in the usual way. At Supplices te rogamus he bows profoundly, his hands crossed before his breast, the left arm beneath the right; and at ut quotquot he begins little by little to rise, so that at altaris participatione he can kiss the right side of the corporal, his arms still crossed; this done, he stands erect, puts his left hand back on the corporal, and with the right blesses the Host at Corpus and the chalice at Sanguinem, and at benedictione caelesti makes the sign of the cross before himself with the right hand from forehead to breast and shoulders (the left being placed below the breast), yet without touching himself, and not with the first three fingers with which he signed the sacrifice, but with the three last fingers, thumb and forefinger being closed; at Per eumdem he joins his hands before his breast.

Manibus junctis, ut prius, dicat: Memento etiam, et ad N. N. memoriam mortuorum, oculis in sacrificium intentis, mente recolat, ut de vivorum memoria dictum est, et postea apertis manibus cetera dicat; ad Per eumdem jungat manus. Tria priora verba Nobis quoque peccatoribus dicat clara et intelligibili voce, pectus leviter tundens dexterae tribus ultimis digitis; et manibus extensis, ut prius, reliqua prosequatur.

With hands joined as before he says: Memento etiam; and at N. N., with his eyes fixed on the sacrifice, he calls to mind the dead, as was said of the memory of the living, and then says the rest with hands opened; at Per eumdem he joins his hands. The first three words, Nobis quoque peccatoribus, he says in a clear and audible voice, lightly striking his breast with the three last fingers of the right hand; and with hands extended as before he continues the rest.

Ad Per quem haec omnia sinistram manum super altare demittat; dexterae extremis tribus digitis ad sanctificas totum sacrificium tribus vicibus signet; Calicem discooperiat, et dicto et praestas nobis genuflexus Sacramentum adoret, moxque se erigat. Indice sinistrae manus Hostiam leviter deprimat, ut ex altera parte attollatur; tunc pollice et indice dexterae eam accipiat, et statim tribus posterioribus digitis sinistrae manus pedem Calicis firmet super altare, et dextera manu cum Hostia ad Per ipsum tres cruces super Calicem formet, primam exterius a labio ad labium, secundam intra Calicem a labio ad labium, tertiam profundius, quam secundam, quartam vero ad Deo Patri ante labium Calicis in alto aliquantulum; qua facta sinistram manum a pede Calicis reducat, et super corporale collocet, et crucem ad Spiritus Sancti ante pedem Calicis in imo signet; qua facta, Hostiam, reverentia capite aliquantulum ei exhibita, super corporale reponat suo loco, non tamen populo ostendat: et illis duobus digitis dexterae super Calicem excussis, eumdem palla cooperiat, et genuflexus Sacramentum adoret.

At Per quem haec omnia he lays his left hand on the altar; at sanctificas he signs the whole sacrifice three times with the three last fingers of the right; he uncovers the chalice and, having said et praestas nobis, adores the Sacrament kneeling and rises at once. With the forefinger of the left hand he presses lightly on the Host so that it is raised on the other side; then he takes it with the thumb and forefinger of the right, and at once with the three last fingers of the left hand holds the foot of the chalice steady on the altar, and with the right hand holding the Host makes three crosses over the chalice at Per ipsum, the first outside from lip to lip, the second within the chalice from lip to lip, the third deeper than the second; and the fourth at Deo Patri before the lip of the chalice, somewhat raised; then he withdraws his left hand from the foot of the chalice, places it on the corporal, and at Spiritus Sancti makes a cross low down before the foot of the chalice; this done, bowing his head a little to the Host, he puts it back in its place on the corporal, but does not show it to the people; and shaking those two fingers of the right hand over the chalice, he covers it with the pall and adores the Sacrament kneeling.

XII Ad Pater noster et fractionemAt the Pater noster and the fraction

Et manus super corporale hinc inde extensas demittens, dicat: Per omnia saecula saeculorum. Minister respondeat: Amen. Eodem modo manus tenens dicat: Oremus. Praeceptis salutaribus moniti; et ad Pater noster elevet manus, et extendat more solito. Minister respondeat: Sed libera nos a malo. Et Sacerdos submissa voce dicat: Amen. Eadem voce prosequatur: Libera nos, quaesumus Domine, manibus ut prius extensis, et ad Maria inclinet caput.

And laying his hands on the corporal on either side, he says: Per omnia saecula saeculorum. The server answers: Amen. Holding his hands in the same way he says: Oremus. Praeceptis salutaribus moniti; and at Pater noster he raises his hands and extends them in the usual way. The server answers: Sed libera nos a malo. And the priest says in a low voice: Amen. In the same voice he continues: Libera nos, quaesumus Domine, with hands extended as before, and at Maria he bows his head.

Ad da propitius pacem demissa sinistra super corporale, dextera accipiat patenam inter indicem et medium digitum, et se signet cum ea tangendo frontem, et pectus, deinde sinistram pectoris partem, et dexteram; postea osculetur patenam, et mox ipsam super altare deponat seorsum a corporali, et ad et ab omni perturbatione securi, manu dextera pollice et indice non disjunctis, detegat Calicem, et genuflexus Sacramentum adoret; tum se erigens, sinistrae indice leviter deprimat Hostiam, ut dexterae indice et pollice capi possit: qua accepta, statim sinistrae tribus posterioribus digitis pedem Calicis teneat, dextera statim Hostiam super Calicem levet, ita ut etiam eadem manu Calix teneatur; tunc sinistrae manus pollice et indice Hostiam accipiat, et incipiens Per eumdem, Hostiam decenter in duas partes, per medium a summo usque deorsum dividens, frangat, diligenter cavens ne digiti fracturas attingant.

At da propitius pacem, his left hand laid on the corporal, he takes the paten with the right between the forefinger and middle finger and signs himself with it, touching his forehead and breast, then the left side of the breast and the right; then he kisses the paten and at once lays it on the altar apart from the corporal; and at et ab omni perturbatione securi, with the thumb and forefinger of the right hand not parted, he uncovers the chalice and adores the Sacrament kneeling; then, rising, he presses lightly on the Host with the forefinger of the left hand so that it can be taken with the forefinger and thumb of the right; having taken it, at once he holds the foot of the chalice with the three last fingers of the left hand, and with the right lifts the Host over the chalice, so that the chalice is held by the same hand; then he takes the Host with the thumb and forefinger of the left hand, and beginning Per eumdem he breaks the Host becomingly into two parts, dividing it through the middle from top to bottom, taking care that his fingers do not touch the broken edges.

Ad Qui tecum vivit illam mediam partem Hostiae, quam dextera tenet, in transversum alteri supponat, de eadem etiam frangat cum pollice et indice dexterae manus particulam, et sic partes tenendo quasi junctas, dicat clara voce: Per omnia saecula saeculorum. Minister respondeat: Amen. Ad Pax Domini sinistra manu duas partes Hostiae tenendo semper usque ad sumptionem super Calicem, dextera manu aliam partem Hostiae tenens, has tres cruces cum particula Hostiae intra Calicem faciat, et postea iterum ambas manus sic super Calicem teneat, ut Hostiae partes junctae videantur.

At Qui tecum vivit he places the half of the Host he holds in the right hand crosswise beneath the other, and from it also breaks off a particle with the thumb and forefinger of the right hand, and so, holding the parts as if joined, says in a clear voice: Per omnia saecula saeculorum. The server answers: Amen. At Pax Domini, holding the two parts of the Host in the left hand over the chalice always until the Communion, and holding the other part in the right hand, he makes these three crosses with the particle of the Host within the chalice; and afterwards again holds both hands over the chalice so that the parts of the Host appear joined.

XIII Ad Agnus Dei, pacem et communionemAt the Agnus Dei, the peace and the Communion

Clara voce dicat: Agnus Dei. Deinde secreto dicat: Haec sacrosancta commixtio, et tunc statim illam particulam Hostiae, quam dextera tenet, reverenter in Calicem demittat, verba prosequendo; iterumque excussis super Calicem digitis, statim medium Calicis dextera teneat, et dicto Amen, exosculetur extremum labium Calicis, et dans pacem ministro tenenti pallam, vel imaginem pacis, ubi consuevit hoc fieri, dicat: Pax tibi, et Ecclesiae sanctae Dei (nisi cum pro mortuis celebratur; tunc enim, et in triduo ante Pascha, dimittitur, uti et osculum).

In a clear voice he says: Agnus Dei. Then he says secretly: Haec sacrosancta commixtio, and at once reverently lets fall into the chalice the particle of the Host which he holds in the right hand, continuing the words; and having again shaken his fingers over the chalice, he at once holds the middle of the chalice with the right hand, and having said Amen, kisses the outer lip of the chalice, and giving the peace to the server, who holds the pall or the image of the peace where this is the custom, says: Pax tibi, et Ecclesiae sanctae Dei (except when Mass is celebrated for the dead; for then, and in the Triduum before Easter, it is omitted, as is the kiss).

Qua poterit devotione Orationem dicat: Domine Jesu Christe, Fili Dei vivi; qua finita, brevi oratione mentali seipsum Domino commendet, nulla alia addita oratione, nec pectus tundat, sed inclinato tantum capite, dextera manu tenens Calicem, de sinistra mox sumat ore sacrum Christi Corpus, dicens: Corpus et Sanguis Domini nostri Jesu Christi custodiant me in vitam aeternam. Amen; quo facto, statim, absumpta Hostia, Calicem palla cooperiat, et aliquantulum quiescat in meditatione SS. Sacramenti; deinde genu flectat, et mox surgens Calicem detegat, et dextera medium Calicis, sinistra pedem accipiat, et sic Sanguinem cum particula in Calice demissa sumat, nihil dicens.

With what devotion he can he says the prayer Domine Jesu Christe, Fili Dei vivi; when it is finished he commends himself to the Lord in a short mental prayer, adding no other prayer, nor does he strike his breast; but with his head only bowed, holding the chalice in his right hand, he at once takes the sacred Body of Christ into his mouth from his left, saying: Corpus et Sanguis Domini nostri Jesu Christi custodiant me in vitam aeternam. Amen. This done, and the Host consumed, he at once covers the chalice with the pall and rests a little in meditation on the most holy Sacrament; then he genuflects, and rising at once uncovers the chalice, takes the middle of the chalice with the right hand and the foot with the left, and so consumes the Blood with the particle put into the chalice, saying nothing.

Post primum haustum, sinistram super altare demittat, sicque post secundam lambitionem: et dextera accipiat patenam, cum qua diligenter colligat particulas Hostiae super corporale, si quae in eo sint; sinistra interim elevando paululum corporale, ut facilius colligere possit: deinde cum indice dexterae manus eas in Calicem immittat, et postea excutiat in Calicem ipsum indicem cum pollice, ne aliquid fragmentorum in eo remaneat, easque absumat, sive parvae sint, sive magnae.

After the first draught he lays his left hand on the altar, and so after the second; and with the right he takes the paten, with which he carefully gathers any particles of the Host there may be on the corporal, meanwhile lifting the corporal a little with the left so as to gather them more easily; then with the forefinger of the right hand he puts them into the chalice, and afterwards shakes the forefinger and thumb themselves into the chalice, lest any fragment remain on them, and consumes them, whether small or large.

XIV Ad ablutiones et PostcommunionemAt the ablutions and the Postcommunion

Postea cum Calice, utraque manu delato, ad cornu Epistolae pro ablutione, quae ex solo vino infundetur, accedat, nihil dicens: et deinde eam sumat. Secundam ablutionem ex vino, et aqua, super digitos accipiat, dicens secreto: Quod ore sumpsimus; deinde purificatorio extergat ipsos digitos, et postea dextera Calicem sumens ebibat, os tergens cum purificatorio, et tandem Calicem.

Then, carrying the chalice in both hands, he goes to the Epistle corner for the ablution, which is poured of wine alone, saying nothing, and then consumes it. The second ablution, of wine and water, he receives over his fingers, saying secretly: Quod ore sumpsimus; then he wipes the fingers with the purificator, and afterwards, taking the chalice in the right hand, drinks it, wiping his mouth with the purificator, and lastly the chalice.

Quo exterso, Calicem super corporale demittat, purificatorium desuper extendens, patenam eidem imponens, palla, et velo cooperiens, ac inclinato capite Cruci, ad Missale se conferens (quod a ministro ad cornu Epistolae delatum erit), junctis manibus ante pectus dicat Communionem. Deinde ad populum se vertens dicat: Dominus vobiscum. Minister respondeat: Et cum spiritu tuo, ut supra ante primam Orationem fecit. Ad Oremus junctis manibus ante pectus, inclinet paululum caput versus Crucem. Postea dicat Postcommunionem apertis manibus et de more extensis, et ad Per Dominum nostrum junctis ante pectus; et ad Jesum Christum ad medium altaris veniat, si est ultima Oratio; alioquin, ut dictum est, alias memorias addat, et ad ultimum Per Dominum ad medium altaris accedat.

When it is wiped he sets the chalice on the corporal, spreading the purificator over it, putting the paten on it, and covering it with the pall and veil; and bowing his head to the cross, he goes to the Missal (which the server will have carried to the Epistle corner) and with hands joined before his breast says the Communion. Then, turning to the people, he says: Dominus vobiscum. The server answers: Et cum spiritu tuo, as he did before the first Collect. At Oremus, with hands joined before his breast, he bows his head a little towards the cross. Then he says the Postcommunion with hands opened and extended in the usual way, and at Per Dominum nostrum joined before his breast; and at Jesum Christum he comes to the middle of the altar if it is the last prayer; otherwise, as was said, he adds the other commemorations, and at the last Per Dominum goes to the middle of the altar.

Humiliate capita vestra Deo dicat inclinando caput, ante Orationem super populum, quando Missa est de Feria in Quadragesima.

He says Humiliate capita vestra Deo, bowing his head, before the Prayer over the people, when the Mass is of a Lenten feria.

XV Ad finem MissaeAt the end of Mass

Ad populum conversus, elevatis et extensis manibus, dicat: Dominus vobiscum, et ad vobiscum junctis ante pectus. Minister respondeat: Et cum spiritu tuo. Verso vultu, ut antea, ad populum, ac junctis manibus, dicat: Ite Missa est. Minister respondeat: Deo gratias. Cum autem non dicitur Gloria in excelsis in Missa, non dicatur Ite Missa est, sed Benedicamus Domino, quod verso vultu ad altare dicendum est. Et eodem modo dicatur Requiescant in pace, quando Missa est pro defunctis (et tunc minister respondeat Amen).

Turned to the people, with hands raised and extended, he says: Dominus vobiscum, joining them before his breast at vobiscum. The server answers: Et cum spiritu tuo. With his face turned to the people as before, and hands joined, he says: Ite Missa est. The server answers: Deo gratias. When the Gloria in excelsis is not said in the Mass, Ite Missa est is not said, but Benedicamus Domino, which is to be said with the face turned to the altar. And Requiescant in pace is said in the same way when the Mass is for the dead (and then the server answers Amen).

Dicto Ite Missa est, revertatur ad altare eadem via, qua ad populum se verterat; et tunc inclinans profunde (etiamsi Benedicamus Domino, vel Requiescant in pace dixerit) junctis manibus dicat secreto: Placeat tibi sancta Trinitas; quo dicto, ad Per Christum erectus exosculetur altare. Ad populum conversus det Benedictionem manu dextera totaliter extensa, sinistram pectori applicando, unica cruce populum signans: et perficiens circulum (nisi sit expositum, vel in tabernaculo occlusum SS. Sacramentum) ad cornu Evangelii accedat.

Having said Ite Missa est, he turns back to the altar by the same way by which he had turned to the people; and then, bowing profoundly (even if he has said Benedicamus Domino or Requiescant in pace), with hands joined he says secretly: Placeat tibi sancta Trinitas; this said, at Per Christum he stands erect and kisses the altar. Turned to the people, he gives the blessing with the right hand fully extended, laying the left on his breast, signing the people with a single cross; and completing the circle (unless the most holy Sacrament is exposed or shut in the tabernacle) he goes to the Gospel corner.

Dicendo Dominus vobiscum crucem cum pollice super altare in loco Evangelii, vel super librum signet. Minister respondeat. Dicendo Initium, etc. signet pollice frontem, os, et pectus Crucis signaculo, et se totum, ut in primo Evangelio factum est. Minister respondeat: Gloria tibi Domine. Interim, dum Evangelium prosequitur, junctis manibus, intento vultu ad Evangelii cornu respiciat, neque tunc temporis complicet corporale. Interim cum ad Et Verbum caro factum est pervenerit, genu dexterum flectat, et ad et habitavit in nobis surgat. Minister respondeat: Deo gratias; et Sacerdos signet se totum, ut supra.

In saying Dominus vobiscum he signs a cross with his thumb on the altar at the place of the Gospel, or on the book. The server answers. In saying Initium, etc., he signs his forehead, mouth and breast with the sign of the cross with his thumb, and his whole person, as was done at the first Gospel. The server answers: Gloria tibi Domine. Meanwhile, as he continues the Gospel with hands joined, he looks towards the Gospel corner, and does not fold the corporal at that time. When he comes to Et Verbum caro factum est, he genuflects on the right knee, and at et habitavit in nobis rises. The server answers: Deo gratias; and the priest signs his whole person as above.

Cum ad finem Evangelii perventum est, tum ad medium altaris revertatur, et calicem a corporali ad cornu Evangelii seponat; complicet corporale, complicatum in bursam recondat, et ad cornu Epistolae locet, et calice in medio altaris reposito, velo bursam superponat, veli partem anteriorem super bursam reducendo, ut fecerat, cum ad Missam accesserat. Deinde caput Cruci devotius inclinet, et cooperto ambabus manibus capite caputio et amictu, Deum devote laudans, accipiens sinistra calicem per medium, et dextera posita super bursam modo quo in initio Missae dictum est, descendat infra infimum gradum altaris, et ibi in medio conversus ad Crucem, profunde inclinet (vel si in eo sit tabernaculum SS. Sacramenti, genu flectat) et ad sacristiam dicendo Canticum Benedicite omnia opera, ordine, quo venerat, revertatur.

When the end of the Gospel is reached, he returns to the middle of the altar and puts the chalice aside from the corporal towards the Gospel corner; he folds the corporal, puts it folded into the burse and lays it at the Epistle corner; and having set the chalice back in the middle of the altar, he lays the burse on the veil, drawing the front of the veil over the burse as he had done when he came to Mass. Then he bows his head devoutly to the cross, and covering his head with both hands with the capuce and amice, praising God devoutly, taking the chalice by the middle with the left hand and laying the right on the burse in the manner described at the beginning of Mass, he goes down below the lowest step of the altar, and there in the middle, turned to the cross, bows profoundly (or genuflects, if the tabernacle of the most holy Sacrament is on it), and returns to the sacristy in the order in which he came, saying the canticle Benedicite omnia opera.

De quibusdam in Missa observandisOf certain things to be observed in Mass

I De genuflexionibus faciendis in Missis tam privatis, quam solemnibusOf the genuflexions in Masses both private and solemn

1. Semper genu flectit in Missa privata Sacerdos quando dicit Evangelium sancti Joannis, In principio, ad illa verba Et Verbum caro factum est, et in Evangelio Epiphaniae, Cum natus esset Jesus, ad illa verba Et procidentes adoraverunt eum, item in Evangelio Feriae quartae post Dominicam quartam Quadragesimae ad illa verba in fine Et procidens adoravit eum. Item genu flectit Dominica palmarum, et in Missis de Cruce, ad illa verba in Epistola, In nomine Jesu omne genu flectatur. Item genu flectit ad illa verba Tractus in Quadragesima, Adjuva nos Deus salutaris noster, et in omnibus Missis de Spiritu Sancto, dum dicit Versum Veni Sancte Spiritus, usque ad reple. Item quotiescumque dicit Flectamus genua, et quando legitur Passio ad illa verba exspiravit, vel emisit spiritum, et ad illa verba Symboli, Et incarnatus est, etc. utrumque genu flectit.

1. In a private Mass the priest always genuflects when he says the Gospel of St John, In principio, at the words Et Verbum caro factum est; in the Gospel of the Epiphany, Cum natus esset Jesus, at the words Et procidentes adoraverunt eum; and in the Gospel of the Wednesday after the fourth Sunday of Lent at the words at the end Et procidens adoravit eum. He also genuflects on Palm Sunday and in Masses of the Cross at the words of the Epistle In nomine Jesu omne genu flectatur. He also genuflects at the words of the Lenten Tract Adjuva nos Deus salutaris noster, and in all Masses of the Holy Ghost while he says the verse Veni Sancte Spiritus, as far as reple. Also whenever he says Flectamus genua, and when the Passion is read at the words exspiravit or emisit spiritum, and at the words of the Creed Et incarnatus est, etc., he kneels on both knees.

2. In Missa solemni etiam Sacerdos genu flectit ad omnia supradicta, praeterquam ad Flectamus genua, quia tunc ipse solus stat, Diaconus vero, et omnes alii genua flectunt; et ad Versum Veni Sancte Spiritus, si prius sedebat, non genu flectit, sed surgit, et caput tantum cum ministris profunde inclinat. In praedictis autem omnes ministri, quando Sacerdos genu flectit, debent etiam genu flectere, exceptis Acolythis ad Evangelium tenentibus candelabra, qui tunc non genu flectunt; et dum Diaconus cantat illa verba, ad quae est genu flectendum, ipse versus librum, Sacerdos et alii omnes versus altare genu flectunt.

2. In a solemn Mass the priest also genuflects at all the above, except at Flectamus genua, for then he alone stands, while the deacon and all the others kneel; and at the verse Veni Sancte Spiritus, if he was sitting before, he does not kneel but rises and only bows his head profoundly with the ministers. In all these, when the priest genuflects, all the ministers must genuflect too, except the acolytes holding the candlesticks at the Gospel, who then do not genuflect; and while the deacon sings the words at which one genuflects, he genuflects towards the book, the priest and all the others towards the altar.

3. Insuper genu flectit toties, quotiescumque in Rubricis notatur, quod debeat genu flectere.

3. Moreover he genuflects as often as the rubrics note that he should genuflect.

II De his, quae addenda sunt in Missa, quae dicitur in altari, in quo expositum est Sanctissimum Sacramentum, vel in tabernaculo occlusumOf what is to be added in a Mass said at an altar on which the Most Holy Sacrament is exposed, or shut in the tabernacle

1. In primis abstinendum est, quantum fieri potest, a celebranda Missa, praeter solemnem, coram SS. Sacramento, etiam in tabernaculo occluso. Quod si ferat necessitas, aut suadeat justa causa, diligenter observandi sunt actus reverentiales, et genuflexiones sequentes.

1. First, as far as possible one should refrain from celebrating Mass, other than a solemn Mass, before the Most Holy Sacrament, even when it is shut in the tabernacle. If necessity requires it or a just cause recommends it, the following acts of reverence and genuflexions are to be carefully observed.

2. Quandocumque exponitur SS. Sacramentum in diebus aliis ab ejus festo et Octavis, Missa solemnis celebranda est de SS. Sacramento cum debitis memoriis; sed tunc etiam dici debet Missa conventualis de eo, de quo fit Officium; vel una Missa cantetur de die cum memoria Sacramenti; sicut et id fieri debet in diebus, in quibus sola propria Missa dici potest, et prohibentur dici Missae votivae, etiam solemnes, si in illis diebus Sacramentum exponitur.

2. Whenever the Most Holy Sacrament is exposed on days other than its feast and octave, a solemn Mass of the Most Holy Sacrament is to be celebrated with the due commemorations; but then the conventual Mass must also be said of the Office of the day; or one Mass is sung of the day with a commemoration of the Sacrament; and this must also be done on days on which only the proper Mass may be said and votive Masses, even solemn, are forbidden, if the Sacrament is exposed on those days.

3. In ingressu altaris, in quo patet Sacramentum, omnes, tam Celebrans quam ministri, debent utrumque genu flectere.

3. On coming to the altar on which the Sacrament is exposed, all, the celebrant as well as the ministers, must kneel on both knees.

4. Accedens Celebrans ad medium altaris, et recedens a medio, vel transiens ante medium, toties quoties semper unicum genu flectat, quod et alii ministri similiter observent.

4. Each time the celebrant comes to the middle of the altar, leaves the middle, or passes before the middle, he always genuflects on one knee; and the other ministers do likewise.

5. Quotiescumque debet converti ad Conventum, seu populum, dicturus Dominus vobiscum: stans in medio altaris, facta genuflexione, accedat ad cornu Evangelii, et ibi sistat se, renibus versis circa cornu altaris, et facie versa ad populum, dicat Dominus vobiscum; et tum per eamdem viam, facta iterum genuflexione in medio, redeat ad librum, ibique more solito dicat Oremus, et Orationem.

5. Whenever he must turn to the community or the people to say Dominus vobiscum, standing in the middle of the altar he genuflects, goes towards the Gospel corner and stands there with his back towards the corner of the altar and his face to the people, and says Dominus vobiscum; then by the same way, genuflecting again in the middle, he returns to the book and there says Oremus and the prayer in the usual way.

6. In thurificatione observentur dicenda in Titulo De Officio ministrorum altaris; praeterquam quod post thurificationem calicis, Sacerdos non stans, sed utroque genu flexo, cum ministris debite ordinatis, in supremo gradu, incensat SS. Sacramentum, inclinando caput profunde, ante et post incensationem ipsi Sacramento. Deinde erectus incensabit altare, ut Titulo supra citato dicitur, dexterum genu flectens, quoties transibit per medium altaris. Item Diaconus non debet tunc osculari manus Sacerdotis in traditione thuribuli, sicut neque in traditione quarumcumque aliarum rerum. Item cum incensandus est Sacerdos, descendens a supremo gradu in cornu Epistolae, sistat se versa facie ad populum, et ibidem post thurificationem lavet manus. Insuper animadvertant omnes, dum movent se, ne umquam terga vertant Sacramento.

6. At the incensation what is said in the title Of the office of the ministers of the altar is to be observed, except that after incensing the chalice the priest, not standing but kneeling on both knees on the top step with the ministers duly ranged, incenses the Most Holy Sacrament, bowing his head profoundly to the Sacrament before and after the incensation. Then, standing, he will incense the altar as is said in the title cited above, genuflecting on the right knee each time he passes the middle of the altar. Also the deacon must not then kiss the priest's hand when handing the thurible, nor when handing anything else. Also, when the priest is to be incensed, he comes down from the top step at the Epistle corner and stands there with his face to the people, and there after the incensation washes his hands. Moreover all are to take care, when they move, never to turn their backs to the Sacrament.

7. Dicitur Orate fratres, et Ite Missa est, et datur benedictio a Celebrante, ubi dictum est Dominus vobiscum; sed non perficit circulum sed revertitur ad medium altaris, ubi iterum genu flectit coram SS. Sacramento: et post benedictionem, facta genuflexione, accedit ad cornu Evangelii, ultimum Evangelium dicturus.

7. The Orate fratres and the Ite Missa est are said, and the blessing is given by the celebrant, where the Dominus vobiscum was said; but he does not complete the circle, but returns to the middle of the altar, where he genuflects again before the Most Holy Sacrament; and after the blessing, having genuflected, he goes to the Gospel corner to say the last Gospel.

8. Recedit Celebrans cum ministris ab altari genuflexione utroque genu facta.

8. The celebrant leaves the altar with the ministers, kneeling on both knees.

9. Si vero in altari sit Sacramentum in tabernaculo occlusum, quotiescumque Celebrans dicit Dominus vobiscum, ne terga vertat tabernaculo, similiter ad medium altaris procedat, factaque capitis inclinatione, paululum ad cornu Evangelii secedens, vertat se ad populum per latus dexterum, et per idem latus, facta iterum coram tabernaculo capitis inclinatione, ad librum redeat, ibique dicat pro more Oremus, et Orationem, seu Orationes.

9. But if the Sacrament is shut in the tabernacle on the altar, whenever the celebrant says Dominus vobiscum, so as not to turn his back to the tabernacle, he likewise goes to the middle of the altar and, bowing his head, steps a little towards the Gospel corner, turns to the people by his right side, and by the same side, having bowed his head again before the tabernacle, returns to the book and there says Oremus and the prayer or prayers as usual.

10. Idem faciat ad Orate fratres, et Ite Missa est, et dum benedicit populum, advertendo etiam, quod tunc non perficit circulum, sed revertitur eadem via ad medium altaris; ubi iterum caput inclinat coram tabernaculo: et data benedictione, ad cornu Evangelii accedit, dicturus, ut supra, ultimum Evangelium.

10. He does the same at the Orate fratres, at the Ite Missa est, and while he blesses the people, noting also that then he does not complete the circle, but returns by the same way to the middle of the altar, where he bows his head again before the tabernacle; and having given the blessing he goes to the Gospel corner to say the last Gospel, as above.

11. Haec omnia observentur in Missa privata, praeter specialia Missae solemnis.

11. All this is to be observed in a private Mass, apart from what is proper to the solemn Mass.

III De celebratione Missae coram Summo Pontifice, Cardinale Legato Sedis Apostolicae, Archiepiscopo, aut Episcopo in sua residentiaOf the celebration of Mass before the Supreme Pontiff, a Cardinal Legate of the Apostolic See, an Archbishop, or a Bishop in his residence

1. Celebraturus Sacerdos coram Summo Pontifice, sistat se ante infimum gradum altaris a cornu Evangelii ante ipsum Pontificem, ubi genuflexus exspectat; accepta benedictione erigit se, et stans aliquantulum versus ad altare, incipiat Missam. Si autem sit coram Cardinale legato Sedis Apostolicae, Patriarcha, Archiepiscopo, aut Episcopo, in sua residentia, vel loco jurisdictionis: stans ante infimum gradum ad cornu Evangelii, ut supra, exspectat; dato signo, facit profundam reverentiam Praelato, et versus ad altare incipit Missam.

1. A priest who is to celebrate before the Supreme Pontiff stands before the lowest step of the altar on the Gospel side, before the Pontiff himself, where he waits kneeling; having received the blessing he rises, and standing turned somewhat towards the altar begins Mass. If it is before a Cardinal Legate of the Apostolic See, a Patriarch, an Archbishop or a Bishop in his residence or place of jurisdiction, he waits standing before the lowest step on the Gospel side, as above; at the sign given he makes a profound reverence to the prelate and, turned to the altar, begins Mass.

2. Solemniter celebrans coram Summo Pontifice, aut alio ex Praelatis praedictis, in Ecclesiis eorum jurisdictionis, stans a sinistris Praelati faciat cum eo Confessionem; et ubi dicit vobis fratres, dicat tibi Pater; et similiter in fine, ubi dicit vos fratres, dicat te Pater.

2. Celebrating solemnly before the Supreme Pontiff or another of the prelates named, in the churches of their jurisdiction, he makes the Confession with the prelate, standing at his left; and where he says vobis fratres he says tibi Pater, and likewise at the end, where he says vos fratres, he says te Pater.

3. Facta Confessione, si coram Summo Pontifice celebret, genu flectat; si coram aliis Praelatis, inclinet profunde, et ascendens ad medium altaris ante infimum gradum, ibi incipiat secreto Aufer a nobis.

3. The Confession made, if he celebrates before the Supreme Pontiff he genuflects; if before the other prelates, he bows profoundly; and going up to the middle of the altar before the lowest step, he there begins secretly Aufer a nobis.

4. Dicto Evangelio, Summo Pontifici, aut aliis praedictis liber Evangeliorum defertur osculandus: et tunc Celebrans non osculatur librum, nec dicit Per Evangelica dicta. Si adsint plures Praelati simul, digniori tantum defertur liber osculandus; si sint aequales, nulli. Neque defertur Vicario Apostolico, neque Commendatario, seu ejusdem Procuratori, neque aliis in Rubrica non nominatis. Principibus autem magnis saecularibus (non autem inferioribus laicis) alius liber deferendus est, non idem ipse Celebrantis, qui proprium eo casu semper debet osculari librum.

4. After the Gospel the book of the Gospels is carried to the Supreme Pontiff or the others named to be kissed; and then the celebrant does not kiss the book, nor say Per Evangelica dicta. If several prelates are present together, the book is carried to be kissed only to the most dignified; if they are equal, to none. Nor is it carried to a Vicar Apostolic, a Commendatory or his procurator, nor to others not named in the rubric. To great secular princes (but not to lesser lay people) another book is to be carried, not the celebrant's own, who in that case must always kiss his own book.

5. In Missa solemni Diaconus, porrecto Celebranti instrumento pacis osculando, accedat ad Praelatum, et alios supradictos, quibus idem instrumentum porrigat.

5. In a solemn Mass the deacon, having offered the instrument of the peace to the celebrant to kiss, goes to the prelate and the others named above and offers them the same instrument.

6. Celebrans, dicto Placeat tibi sancta Trinitas, dicat Benedictio Dei omnipotentis, et convertens se ad Summum Pontificem (quando adest) genuflexus, ad Cardinalem vero, vel alium ex supradictis Praelatis, capite inclinato, quasi licentiam petens benedicendi, prosequatur Patris, et Filii, etc., benedicens astantes a parte, ubi non assistit Pontifex, aut alius ex Praelatis praedictis. Si autem celebravit coram Patriarcha, Archiepiscopo, et Episcopo extra ejus jurisdictionem, absque alio respectu, ut ceteris, qui intersunt, more consueto benedicet.

6. The celebrant, having said Placeat tibi sancta Trinitas, says Benedictio Dei omnipotentis, and turning to the Supreme Pontiff (when he is present) kneeling, or to a Cardinal or another of the prelates named with his head bowed, as if asking leave to bless, continues Patris, et Filii, etc., blessing those present on the side where the Pontiff or other prelate named is not. If he has celebrated before a Patriarch, Archbishop or Bishop outside his jurisdiction, he blesses in the usual way without other regard, as for the rest who are present.

7. Finito Evangelio, faciat convenientem reverentiam, juxta qualitatem praedictorum Praelatorum.

7. When the Gospel is finished, he makes a fitting reverence according to the rank of the prelates named.

IV De his, quae omittuntur in Missa pro DefunctisOf what is left out in Mass for the dead

Missa pro Defunctis dicitur modo ut supra, excepto quod aqua non benedicitur, quando infunditur in calice; et non dicitur Gloria Patri post Introitum, sed dicto Te decet repetitur Requiem aeternam; neque Gloria in excelsis, nec Flectamus genua, nec Alleluia, neque Orationes quae sunt tantum pro vivis, nec Dominus sit in corde meo, nec osculandus est liber, dicto Evangelio; non dicitur Credo, non dicuntur haec verba, Quid retribuam Domino pro omnibus, quae retribuit mihi? non dicitur ad Agnus Dei miserere nobis vel dona nobis pacem, sed ejus loco dicitur dona eis requiem, et in ultimo additur sempiternam; nec osculandus est calix post Pax Domini neque pax datur; nec dicitur Humiliate capita, etc., et in fine non dicitur Ite Missa est, nec Benedicamus Domino, sed Requiescant in pace, verso vultu ad altare; nec benedictio datur; nec aliud Evangelium umquam dicatur in fine, quam In principio erat Verbum. Alia omnia serventur, ut in aliis Missis.

Mass for the dead is said in the manner above, except that the water is not blessed when it is poured into the chalice; and the Gloria Patri is not said after the Introit, but after the verse Te decet the Requiem aeternam is repeated; nor is the Gloria in excelsis said, nor Flectamus genua, nor Alleluia, nor the prayers which are only for the living, nor Dominus sit in corde meo, nor is the book kissed after the Gospel; the Credo is not said; the words Quid retribuam Domino pro omnibus, quae retribuit mihi? are not said; at the Agnus Dei, miserere nobis or dona nobis pacem is not said, but in its place dona eis requiem, and sempiternam is added at the last; the chalice is not kissed after Pax Domini nor is the peace given; Humiliate capita, etc. is not said; and at the end neither Ite Missa est nor Benedicamus Domino is said, but Requiescant in pace, with the face turned to the altar; nor is the blessing given; nor is any other Gospel ever said at the end than In principio erat Verbum. All else is observed as in other Masses.

V De his, quae clara voce, mediocri, aut secreto dicenda sunt in MissaOf what is to be said in Mass aloud, in a moderate voice, or secretly

Clara autem, et intelligibili voce a Celebrante dicuntur in principio Missae In nomine Patris, et Filii, etc., Confitemini Domino, quoniam bonus; Introitus; Kyrie eleison; Gloria in excelsis; Dominus vobiscum, Oremus, Flectamus genua, Levate, Oratio vel Orationes, Prophetiae seu Lectiones ante Epistolam; Epistola; Responsorium, Alleluia, Tractus, Sequentia; Evangelium; Credo; Offertorium; Praefatio; et haec tria verba Nobis quoque peccatoribus; item Per omnia saecula saeculorum, cum Pater noster; Per omnia saecula saeculorum, cum Pax Domini; Agnus Dei, Communio, Postcommunio, Humiliate capita vestra Deo, Ite Missa est, vel Benedicamus Domino, vel Requiescant in pace.

The celebrant says in a clear and audible voice: at the beginning of Mass, In nomine Patris, et Filii, etc., and Confitemini Domino, quoniam bonus; the Introit; Kyrie eleison; Gloria in excelsis; Dominus vobiscum, Oremus, Flectamus genua, Levate, the Collect or Collects, the Prophecies or Lessons before the Epistle; the Epistle; the Responsory, Alleluia, Tract and Sequence; the Gospel; the Credo; the Offertory; the Preface; and the three words Nobis quoque peccatoribus; also Per omnia saecula saeculorum with the Pater noster; Per omnia saecula saeculorum with the Pax Domini; the Agnus Dei, the Communion, the Postcommunion, Humiliate capita vestra Deo, Ite Missa est or Benedicamus Domino or Requiescant in pace.

Alia autem omnia dicuntur secreto, ita ut Sacerdos audiat tantum se ipsum, excepta Confessione in principio Missae, et Adjutorium nostrum in nomine Domini, et his duobus verbis Orate fratres, et benedictione cum Evangelio in fine, quae neque secreto, neque clara voce, sed mediocri dicuntur.

All else is said secretly, so that the priest hears only himself, except the Confession at the beginning of Mass, the Adjutorium nostrum in nomine Domini, the two words Orate fratres, and the blessing with the Gospel at the end, which are said neither secretly nor aloud, but in a moderate voice.

VI De servitoribus Missarum privatarumOf the servers of private Masses

1. In Missis privatis frater ministrans deferat cappam, saltem diebus Dominicis et festivis, semperque mundas habeat manus. In incessu, statu, habitu, et in omnibus motibus suis, nihil faciat, quod cujusquam offendat aspectum, sed quod suam deceat sanctitatem. Diligenter attendat, quod vestes Sacerdotis, dum induit se, decenter componantur. Dum ei porrigit amictum, manipulum, stolam, vel quid aliud, semper capite discooperto, reverenter ejus deosculetur manus. Deferat Sacerdoti Missale, ad hoc ut Missam praeparare possit, antequam pergat ad altare.

1. In private Masses the brother serving wears the cappa, at least on Sundays and feasts, and always has clean hands. In his walk, his bearing, his dress and all his movements he does nothing that might offend anyone's eye, but only what befits his holy state. He takes care that the priest's vestments are arranged becomingly as he vests. When he hands him the amice, maniple, stole or anything else, always with his head uncovered, he reverently kisses his hand. He brings the priest the Missal, so that he may prepare the Mass before going to the altar.

2. Mappas altaris (coopertorium auferendo) explicet mature et diligenter, et alia circa altare disponat et ordinet. Provideat ne cum vestibus suis vestes sacras, vel alia sacra circa altare irreverenter tangat. Pergens ad altare, portando Missale ante pectus, cum religiosa modestia, et gravitate incedat.

2. He spreads the altar cloths (taking off the cover) in good time and carefully, and sets out and arranges the other things about the altar. He takes care not to touch the sacred vestments or other sacred things about the altar irreverently with his own clothes. Going to the altar, carrying the Missal before his breast, he walks with religious modesty and gravity.

3. Cum ad altare pervenerit, reverenter inclinet, vel genu flectat cum Sacerdote; deinde Missale reponat super pulvinar in cornu Epistolae, candelas accendat, vinum et aquam reverenter ministret, et attendat quod vinum sit bene sanum et mundum; similiter videat si aqua sit recens et munda; et caveat sollicite ne unum pro alio ministret; et aquam porrigens dicat Benedicite. Si non sit in majoribus Ordinibus, non tangat calicem nuda manu, nec ministret propria manu hostiam, aut vinum et aquam, sed omnia Sacerdoti exhibeat accipienda.

3. When he has come to the altar he bows reverently, or genuflects, with the priest; then he puts the Missal on the cushion at the Epistle corner, lights the candles, and reverently ministers the wine and water, taking care that the wine is quite sound and clean, and likewise seeing that the water is fresh and clean; and he takes great care not to give the one for the other; and offering the water he says Benedicite. If he is not in major Orders, he does not touch the chalice with his bare hand, nor put in the host or the wine and water with his own hand, but holds everything out for the priest to take.

4. Ad Confiteor Deo, recipiat se ad sinistram Sacerdotis, et genu flectens, ipsi devote respondeat Misereatur tui, postea Kyrie cum Sacerdote alternatim dicat: ac inde non recedat, donec, dicta Epistola, et Alleluia cum Versu, post ipsum, vel Tractum, aut Sequentiam surgens, librum accipiet, et ad cornu Evangelii portabit.

4. At the Confiteor Deo he goes to the priest's left and, kneeling, devoutly answers him Misereatur tui; afterwards he says the Kyrie alternately with the priest; and he does not leave that place until, the Epistle and the Alleluia with its verse having been said, or after it the Tract or Sequence, he rises, takes the book and carries it to the Gospel corner.

5. Animadvertat quod, quando liber est in cornu Epistolae, debet ipse semper esse in cornu Evangelii, et e contra: semperque genua flectens, nisi cum legitur Evangelium, vel cum ministraturus est Sacerdoti vinum, aut aquam, cereum accensurus ad Elevationem, seu librum transportaturus.

5. He is to note that when the book is at the Epistle corner he must always be at the Gospel corner, and the reverse; and always kneeling, except when the Gospel is read, or when he is to minister the wine or water to the priest, light the candle for the Elevation, or carry the book across.

6. Transiens ab una ad alteram partem, in medio altaris reverenter semper inclinet, vel genu flectat, si SS. Sacramentum sit expositum. Dum Sacerdos dicit Offertorium, ad cornu Epistolae accedat, mappulam explicet et extendat, aquam praeparet, ut venienti Sacerdoti pro digitis abluendis, omni cum reverentia ministret.

6. Passing from one side to the other, he always bows reverently in the middle of the altar, or genuflects if the Most Holy Sacrament is exposed. While the priest says the Offertory he goes to the Epistle corner, unfolds and spreads out the towel and prepares the water, so as to minister it with all reverence to the priest when he comes to wash his fingers.

7. Ad Sanctus aliquot ictibus campanulae astantes moneat; deinde surgat, et cereum accendat: in elevatione Sanctissimi Corporis, et Sanguinis Domini nostri Jesu Christi, campanulam, genibus flexis retro Sacerdotem, modeste pulset, et elevare casulam non omittat.

7. At the Sanctus he warns those present with a few strokes of the bell; then he rises and lights the candle; at the elevation of the most holy Body and Blood of our Lord Jesus Christ, kneeling behind the priest, he rings the bell modestly, and does not fail to raise the chasuble.

8. Postquam Sacerdos communicavit, praesto surgat; vinum in prima ablutione, in secunda autem vinum et aquam, pro ablutione, ministret; librum a cornu Evangelii ad cornu Epistolae asportet; cereum, qui fuit in Elevatione accensus, exstinguat, et ad cornu Evangelii genu flectat.

8. When the priest has communicated he rises promptly; he ministers wine for the first ablution, and wine and water for the second; he carries the book from the Gospel corner to the Epistle corner; he puts out the candle that was lit at the Elevation, and kneels at the Gospel corner.

9. Non stet prostratus in tota Missa, sed oculos et mentem habeat circa altare, ut videat quae fuerint facienda, et audiat cum Sacerdoti fuerit respondendum; cui prompte et devote semper respondere debebit.

9. He is not to remain prostrate through the whole Mass, but to keep his eyes and mind on the altar, so as to see what is to be done and to hear when the priest is to be answered; and he must always answer him promptly and devoutly.

10. Data benedictione in fine Missae, surgat, librum accipiat, si non sit deferendus ad cornu Evangelii pro eo dicendo, juxta qualitatem diei, ut dicitur in Ritu Missae, et candelas exstinguat tempore opportuno, ita ut, expleto Evangelio, quod post Missam dici solet, paratus sit, ut Sacerdotem praeeundo ad sacristiam reducat.

10. The blessing having been given at the end of Mass, he rises and takes the book, if it is not to be carried to the Gospel corner for a Gospel to be read from it according to the day, as is said in the Rite of Mass, and puts out the candles at the proper time, so that when the Gospel that is usually said after Mass is finished he is ready to lead the priest back to the sacristy, going before him.

11. Ubi, deposito Missali, et cereo, prostratus ad pedes Sacerdotis, veniam petat, si in aliquo circa servitium deliquit; postea Sacerdotem adjuvet ad deponendas sacras vestes, et quando ab ipso stolam, manipulum, cingulum, vel quid aliud accipiet, tunc ejus (ut dictum est) deosculetur manus. Quibus peractis, dicat Sacerdoti Benedictus Deus; quo subjungente Pater, et Filius, et Spiritus Sanctus, minister respondeat Amen; et deinde recedat, accepta benedictione.

11. There, having put down the Missal and the candle, prostrate at the priest's feet he asks pardon if he has failed in anything in his service; then he helps the priest to take off the sacred vestments, and when he receives from him the stole, maniple, girdle or anything else, he kisses his hand (as was said). This done, he says to the priest Benedictus Deus; and the priest adding Pater, et Filius, et Spiritus Sanctus, the server answers Amen; and then he withdraws, having received the blessing.

De officio ministrorum altarisOf the office of the ministers of the altar (the conventual and solemn Mass)

I De praeparatione ministrorumOf the preparation of the ministers

1. Quando Missa in Conventu fuerit celebranda, ministri altaris, audito signo, se praeparare non differant. Et in primis Acolythi superpelliceis induti, vel albis in Duplicibus, et Totis Duplicibus alios ministros juvent. Nihilominus tamen Diaconus et Subdiaconus juvent se mutuo, et ambo Sacerdotem. In Conventibus autem, ubi pauci sunt fratres, audito signo, poterunt Diaconus et Subdiaconus se praeparare absque sericis indumentis, et ita Officium, si erit dicendum, cum aliis in choro persolvere, quousque cum Sacerdote discedent a choro, ut ipsum adjuvent. In hujusmodi autem praeparatione, caputia sic aptanda sunt sub vestibus ecclesiasticis, ne gibbus aliquis notabilis umquam appareat in humeris Fratrum.

1. When Mass is to be celebrated in the convent, the ministers of the altar are not to delay to vest when the signal is given. First the acolytes, vested in surplices, or in albs on Doubles and Totum Duplex feasts, help the other ministers. Nevertheless the deacon and subdeacon help each other, and both help the priest. In convents where the brethren are few, at the signal the deacon and subdeacon may vest without the silk vestments and so say the Office, if it is to be said, with the others in choir, until they leave the choir with the priest to assist him. In this vesting the capuces are to be so arranged under the church vestments that no noticeable hump ever appears on the brethren's shoulders.

Postmodum Acolythi altare praeparent coopertorium mapparum auferendo, et ipsas mappas decenter explicando; et unum pulvinar ad Missale in cornu Epistolae reponendum ex parte anteriori, et alterum in cornu Evangelii ex parte posteriori, et Epistolarum librum collocandum in cornu Epistolae ex parte posteriori, et ibidem appodiandum deferant; et de hostiis, et ampullis cum vino et aqua suo loco in cornu Epistolae ponendis, et de aliqua mappula ad tergendas nares, juxta Missale ponenda, provideant: hora competenti cereos suos, et altaris, ante inchoationem Missae semper accendant.

Then the acolytes prepare the altar, taking off the cover of the cloths and spreading the cloths becomingly; they bring one cushion for the Missal, to be placed at the Epistle corner towards the front, and another at the Gospel corner towards the back, and the book of Epistles, to be laid at the Epistle corner towards the back and propped there; and they see to the hosts and the cruets with wine and water, to be put in their place at the Epistle corner, and to a small cloth for wiping the nose, to be put beside the Missal; at the proper time, before Mass begins, they always light their own candles and those of the altar.

In Feria iij. quando de beato Dominico, Feria iv. si de beato Thoma, aut alio Sancto nostri Ordinis dicatur Missa, Feria v. cum de SS. Corpore Christi, et Sabbato, quando de beata Virgine celebratur, et in festis Simplicibus non accendantur plures quam duo; in Dominicis vero, et festis Duplicibus, et Totis Duplicibus, non plures quam quatuor, magnis solemnitatibus exceptis, quando sex ad minus accendi debebunt. Idem servetur in Vesperis, et in Matutinis. In reliquis Missis etiam ferialibus, vel Defunctorum, minus quam duo accendendi non erunt. Provideat Sacrista, ut tempore frigoris magni, prunae accensae in patella decenti habeantur, per Acolythos ministrandae tempore opportuno.

On Tuesday when the Mass is of St Dominic, on Wednesday when it is of St Thomas or another saint of our Order, on Thursday when it is of the most holy Body of Christ, and on Saturday when it is of the Blessed Virgin, and on Simple feasts, no more than two candles are lit; on Sundays and on Double and Totum Duplex feasts no more than four, except on the great solemnities, when at least six must be lit. The same is observed at Vespers and Matins. At the other Masses, even ferial or of the dead, not fewer than two are to be lit. In time of great cold the sacristan sees that lighted coals are kept in a fitting pan, to be brought by the acolytes at the proper time.

II De aspersione aquae benedictaeOf the sprinkling of holy water

2. In Dominicis autem diebus provideat etiam de aqua benedicta, qua cum fuerit aspergenda, Acolythis praecedentibus, Subdiaconus et Diaconus et Sacerdos albis induti, et stolis ac manipulis tantum, si non sit facienda Processio, alioquin induti, Sacerdos cappa serica, et Diaconus et Subdiaconus dalmaticis, Hora praecedente Missam finita, accedant ad presbyterium; ubi cum pervenerint, Diaconus cum Acolytho seniore stet a dextris Sacerdotis, Subdiaconus cum juniore a sinistris, et Sacerdos in medio, qui omnes simul profunde inclinent, vel genuflectant ante altare, ut alibi dicitur.

2. On Sundays the sacristan also provides holy water. When it is to be sprinkled, the acolytes going before, the subdeacon, deacon and priest, vested in albs with stoles and maniples only if there is to be no procession (otherwise the priest in a silk cope and the deacon and subdeacon in dalmatics), come to the sanctuary when the Hour before Mass is finished; when they arrive, the deacon stands with the senior acolyte at the priest's right, the subdeacon with the junior at his left, and the priest in the middle; and all together bow profoundly, or genuflect, before the altar, as is said elsewhere.

Tunc senior Acolythorum deferat aquam acceptam de gradibus altaris (ibidem prius a Sacrista in decenti vase collocatam) Sacerdoti, qui, porrigente ei aspersorium Diacono, Cantoribus incipientibus Antiphonam Asperges me, prius ipsum altare majus aspergat, et caveat ne nimis de aqua super illud projiciat: postmodum Diaconum, Subdiaconum, et Acolythos: deinde Magistrum Ordinis (si adest): postea Cantores, si fuerit Duplex, vel Totum Duplex: postmodum Fratres, incipiens a dextris, nisi quando, nullo Praelato existente in Choro dextero, Prior aliquis fuerit in sinistro, vel quando, Priore Conventus exsistente in dextero Choro, major Praelatus est in Choro sinistro; tunc enim aspergendus est major Praelatus, deinde Prior, et postea immediate Chorus dexter. Si tamen Praelatus ille, qui est in sinistro Choro, habet aliquem fratrem Magistrum, superiorem locum obtinentem, tunc aspergendus est prius Chorus dexter. Sicut etiam fiet, quando, existente Praelato aliquo non Magistro in sinistro Choro, Magister aliquis est in dextero.

Then the senior acolyte brings the priest the water, taken from the altar steps (where the sacristan has first placed it in a fitting vessel); and the deacon handing him the sprinkler, as the cantors begin the antiphon Asperges me, the priest first sprinkles the high altar itself, taking care not to throw too much water on it; then the deacon, subdeacon and acolytes; then the Master of the Order, if present; then the cantors, if it is a Double or Totum Duplex; then the brethren, beginning on the right, except when, there being no prelate in the right choir, some prior is in the left; or when, the prior of the convent being in the right choir, a greater prelate is in the left; for then the greater prelate is sprinkled first, then the prior, and then at once the right choir. If, however, the prelate in the left choir has some brother who is a Master holding a higher place, then the right choir is sprinkled first. So also when, some prelate who is not a Master being in the left choir, some Master is in the right.

Post Fratres Clericos, aspergantur Conversi, postmodum saeculares tantum in choro existentes, nisi ubi fuerit consuetudo, quod et alii extra chorum aspergantur. Praedictus ordo servetur in danda pace, et in thurificando, et in aspersione aquae post Completorium, ubi ab uno tantum aspergi consuevit. Si autem chorus non existat juxta altare, alius Sacerdos aspergat Fratres modo praedicto. Et dicto Asperges me a Conventu, Sacerdos, stando ante gradus presbyterii, sicut a principio, dicat Ostende nobis, etc. Dominus vobiscum. Oremus. Exaudi nos, etc. Hoc dicto, praedicti ministri in sacristiam revertantur.

After the clerical brethren the lay brothers are sprinkled, then only those seculars who are in choir, unless it is the custom that others outside the choir are sprinkled too. This order is kept in giving the peace, in incensing, and in the sprinkling of water after Compline, where it is the custom for it to be done by one only. If the choir is not next to the altar, another priest sprinkles the brethren in the same manner. When the Asperges me has been said by the community, the priest, standing before the sanctuary steps as at the beginning, says Ostende nobis, etc., Dominus vobiscum, Oremus, Exaudi nos, etc. This said, the ministers return to the sacristy.

Si sit facienda Processio, dum aqua aspergitur, Cruciferarius crucem a Sacrista praeparatam accipiet; et aspersione facta, Acolythi ad sacristiam redeuntes, cereos deferant, et cum Cruciferario accedant ad presbyterium, et ordine solito ad Processionem cum Conventu incedent: qua peracta, Sacerdos cum ministris ad sacristiam revertatur, ubi, deposita cappa, induatur casula.

If there is to be a procession, while the water is sprinkled the crossbearer takes the cross prepared by the sacristan; and after the sprinkling the acolytes, returning to the sacristy, bring the candles and come with the crossbearer to the sanctuary, and go in procession with the community in the usual order; when it is over, the priest returns with the ministers to the sacristy, where he takes off the cope and puts on the chasuble.

III De ingressu ad altareOf the entrance to the altar

3. Incoepto itaque a Choro post Introitum Gloria Patri, vel Versu, si Gloria Patri non dicitur, praedicti ministri procedant ad altare, cooperto capite cum caputio et amictu, primo Acolythi cum candelabris decentibus, et candelis accensis: deinde Subdiaconus librum Evangeliorum, vel calicem paratum (quando non est deferendus post Gloria in excelsis, ut dicetur infra), et Diaconus Missale coram se deferentes pectori appodiatum, sinistra manu supposita, et dextera librum tenendo, ultimo Sacerdos junctis manibus ante pectus.

3. So when the choir has begun the Gloria Patri after the Introit, or the verse if the Gloria Patri is not said, the ministers go to the altar with heads covered with capuce and amice: first the acolytes with fitting candlesticks and lighted candles; then the subdeacon with the book of the Gospels, or with the prepared chalice (when it is not to be brought after the Gloria in excelsis, as will be said below), and the deacon with the Missal, each carrying his book before him resting against his breast, the left hand beneath and the right holding the book; last the priest with hands joined before his breast.

Venientibus autem ipsis ante altare, et stantibus ab invicem in aliqua distantia ante illud infra infimum gradum, Diaconus ad dexteram Sacerdotis, Subdiaconus ad sinistram, et simili modo Acolythi, unus hinc, et alius inde, omnes simul profunde inclinent, vel genu flectant, et discooperto capite, mox erectus Sacerdos, postquam dixerit secreto Orationem Actiones nostras, signans se a fronte ad pectus et humeros, intelligibili voce dicat: In nomine Patris, et Filii, etc. Confitemini, etc. et dum dicitur Confiteor Deo, etc. inclinent profunde, genibus non curvatis (quod in similibus inclinationibus semper observetur), stantibus tamen erectis Acolythis, et versis ad se mutuo vultibus, ac candelabra prae manibus habentibus.

When they come before the altar and stand before it below the lowest step at some distance from one another, the deacon at the priest's right, the subdeacon at his left, and the acolytes likewise one on each side, all together bow profoundly, or genuflect, and uncover their heads; and the priest, rising at once, having said secretly the prayer Actiones nostras, signing himself from forehead to breast and shoulders, says in an audible voice: In nomine Patris, et Filii, etc., Confitemini, etc.; and while the Confiteor Deo, etc. is said they bow profoundly without bending the knees (which is always to be observed in such bows), the acolytes, however, standing upright, facing each other and holding the candlesticks in their hands.

Ita confessione facta, et absolutione, se erigant, et dicat Sacerdos Adjutorium, etc. et appropinquans ad altare, inclinatus non ex transverso, sed in directum versus medium altaris (quod semper est observandum in hujusmodi inclinationibus), dicat Orationem Aufer a nobis, etc. et denuo erectus, secreto dicat In nomine Patris, etc. Interim Diaconus Missale ponat in cornu Epistolae, et Subdiaconus ad cornu Evangelii librum Evangeliorum, appodiando illum in posteriore parte altaris, et Acolythi deponant cereos cum candelabris super gradus presbyterii, exstinguentes eosdem.

The confession and absolution thus made, they stand erect, and the priest says Adjutorium, etc., and going up to the altar, bowing not sideways but straight towards the middle of the altar (which is always to be observed in such bows), says the prayer Aufer a nobis, etc., and standing erect again says secretly In nomine Patris, etc. Meanwhile the deacon puts the Missal at the Epistle corner, and the subdeacon the book of the Gospels at the Gospel corner, propping it at the back of the altar; and the acolytes set down the candles in their candlesticks on the sanctuary steps and put them out.

Tunc omnes convenientes ad librum, a Diacono jam apertum (qui etiam toties quoties oportuerit, folia vertere, et Sacerdoti dicenda ostendere debebit), et stantes a dextris Sacerdotis ordinati secundum gradus suos, dicant cum Celebrante Introitum, et Kyrie eleison, nec flectant genua cum dicitur Salve sancta parens.

Then all come together at the book, already opened by the deacon (who must also turn the pages, and point out to the priest what is to be said, as often as is needed), and standing at the priest's right in order of rank they say the Introit and the Kyrie eleison with the celebrant; nor do they kneel when Salve sancta parens is said.

4. Deinde post Sacerdotem ordinet se Diaconus, et Subdiaconus post Diaconum. Acolythi autem infra gradus presbyterii, hinc inde (vel in primis sedibus chori, maxime ubi sunt pauci fratres) stent parati ad sua ministeria peragenda: ita tamen, quod dum Canon, vel aliud a Sacerdote in medio altaris stante dicitur, si fuerint duo Acolythi, unus ad dexteram Diaconi, et alter ad sinistram in modum quasi crucis stent ordinati, dum non sunt in aliquo ministerio occupati. Numquam autem in tota Missae celebratione ascendant ad supremum gradum presbyterii, in quo Sacerdos assistit.

4. Then the deacon places himself behind the priest, and the subdeacon behind the deacon. The acolytes stand below the sanctuary steps, one on each side (or in the first stalls of the choir, especially where the brethren are few), ready to perform their duties; yet so that while the Canon or anything else is said by the priest standing in the middle of the altar, if there are two acolytes, one stands at the right of the deacon and the other at the left, ranged in the form of a cross, while they are not busy with some duty. But throughout the whole celebration of Mass they never go up to the top step of the sanctuary, on which the priest stands.

Si vero Dominica, vel Duplex, et supra fuerit, poterunt omnes ire sessum, tali servato ordine, ut Sacerdote primo in presbyterio a cornu Epistolae (in qua parte semper sedes hujusmodi sunt parandae) sedente, ad ejus sinistram Diaconus sedeat, et ad hujus sinistram Subdiaconus; deinde etiam ibi Acolythi sedeant, vel alibi, in scabello tamen inferiori (et hic ordo servetur ubicumque sederint ministri altaris). Qui antequam sessum eant, mappulam extendent super genua Sacerdotis, Diaconi, et Subdiaconi, taliter ut possint etiam super eam manus appodiare; quam postea alter Acolythorum, ad ultimum Kyrie eleison surgens, levabit, incipiendo a Sacerdote, et manum ejus osculando.

If it is a Sunday or a Double or higher, all may go to sit, in this order: the priest sitting first in the sanctuary on the Epistle side (on which side such seats are always to be prepared), the deacon at his left, and the subdeacon at the deacon's left; then the acolytes sit there too, or elsewhere, but on a lower bench (and this order is kept wherever the ministers of the altar sit). Before they go to sit, the acolytes spread a small cloth over the knees of the priest, deacon and subdeacon, so that they can also rest their hands on it; which one of the acolytes, rising at the last Kyrie eleison, afterwards takes up, beginning with the priest and kissing his hand.

IV Ad Gloria in excelsis DeoAt the Gloria in excelsis Deo

5. Quo facto, surgant omnes, et ante medium altaris stantes, inclinent profunde, et Sacerdos, postquam ascenderit ad altare, incipiat Gloria in excelsis, si dicendum fuerit (vel si non, dicat conversus ad populum Dominus vobiscum), ceteris ministris, ut dictum est, ordinatis post ipsum. Deinde simul ad cornu Epistolae venientes, prosequantur incoeptum Gloria cum Sacerdote, stantes a dextris ejus ordine supradicto. Quo dicto, Sacerdos et Diaconus stent, donec Conventus impleverit illud. Et cavendum est ministris, ne transeant, dum aliquid instat agendum, inter ministros gradus majoris, sed retro eos.

5. This done, all rise and, standing before the middle of the altar, bow profoundly; and the priest, having gone up to the altar, begins the Gloria in excelsis if it is to be said (if not, he turns to the people and says Dominus vobiscum), the other ministers being ranged behind him as was said. Then, coming together to the Epistle corner, they continue the Gloria with the priest, standing at his right in the order given above. When it is said, the priest and deacon stand until the community has finished it. And the ministers are to take care, when something is to be done, not to pass between the ministers of higher rank, but behind them.

Interim dum cantatur Gloria in excelsis in Dominicis diebus, et in festis Duplicibus, vel Kyrie eleison, quando Gloria in excelsis a Septuagesima usque ad Pascha non dicitur, Subdiaconus, altero Acolythorum praecedente, in sacristiam pergat, et ibi pulchram et mundam mappam praeparatam super humeros suos (juvante Acolytho) decenter extendat: calicem praeparatum, ut dictum est in Missa privata, accipiat: et sic, praecedente Acolytho, vinum et aquam portante, calicem elevatum ante pectus suum deferat reverenter, et cum ad altare pervenerint, in medio inclinent profunde. Deinde Subdiaconus calicem superponat altari, mappam deponat et super cornu Epistolae decenter extendat, et super eam calicem praeparatum accommodet. Acolythus autem in fenestella, aut super scamnum ad cornu Epistolae vinum et aquam reponat.

Meanwhile, while the Gloria in excelsis is sung on Sundays and Double feasts, or the Kyrie eleison when the Gloria in excelsis is not said from Septuagesima to Easter, the subdeacon, one of the acolytes going before, goes to the sacristy, and there spreads a fair, clean, prepared veil becomingly over his shoulders (the acolyte helping); he takes the chalice prepared as was said for private Mass; and so, the acolyte going before carrying the wine and water, he carries the chalice reverently, raised before his breast; and when they reach the altar they bow profoundly in the middle. Then the subdeacon sets the chalice on the altar, takes off the veil and spreads it becomingly on the Epistle corner, and sets the prepared chalice on it. The acolyte puts the wine and water in the niche, or on a bench at the Epistle corner.

V Ad OrationesAt the Collects

6. Dicto Gloria in excelsis, vel Kyrie eleison pro tempore, dum incipit Sacerdos Dominus vobiscum, Diaconus, non flexis genibus, nisi Episcopus celebraverit, leviter casulam per inferiorem elevet extremitatem, et ita tenendo casulam cum Sacerdote pergat ad librum, et eidem Orationem dicendam ostendat, quod etiam faciet ad Offertorium, Orate fratres, et Postcommunionem. Dicto Dominus vobiscum, dicat Orationem.

6. When the Gloria in excelsis, or the Kyrie eleison according to the season, has been said, as the priest begins Dominus vobiscum the deacon, without kneeling unless a bishop is celebrating, lightly raises the chasuble by its lower edge, and so holding the chasuble goes with the priest to the book and points out to him the prayer to be said; which he will also do at the Offertory, the Orate fratres and the Postcommunion. Dominus vobiscum having been said, the priest says the prayer.

Quod si Missa fuerit de Quadragesima, Diaconus post Oremus, flectendo genua cum aliis inferioribus ministris, dicat clara voce Flectamus genua, et surgendo, immediate dicat Levate. Sacerdos vero non flectat genua, sed subjungat Orationem; qua finita, dicat Per omnia saecula saeculorum, et responso a Choro Amen, Sacerdos et Diaconus ad medium altaris euntes, usque ad infimum gradum descendant, et omnes ministri simul cum eis ante altare profunde inclinent, et sessum eant.

If the Mass is of Lent, after Oremus the deacon, kneeling with the other lower ministers, says aloud Flectamus genua, and rising says at once Levate. The priest does not kneel, but goes on with the prayer; when it is finished he says Per omnia saecula saeculorum, and the choir having answered Amen, the priest and deacon go to the middle of the altar and down to the lowest step, and all the ministers together with them bow profoundly before the altar and go to sit.

VI Ad EpistolamAt the Epistle

7. Dum ultima Oratio dicitur, altero Acolythorum praecedente, Subdiaconus librum Epistolarum ferat, secundum modum supradictum de Evangelio et Missali, super pectus suum reclinatum, ad locum, in quo Epistola legenda est: hic vero semper erit ante gradus presbyterii, super pulpitum ad hoc ibidem praeparatum in medio, verso vultu ad altare. In Conventibus autem, in quibus consuevit legi Dominicis diebus, et festis Duplicibus, et supra, in loco eminenti supra pulpitum, servetur consuetudo. Praedictus vero Acolythus, Epistola perlecta, librum, qui sibi traditur a Subdiacono, deferat super altare, ponens illum in loco suo.

7. While the last prayer is said, one of the acolytes going before, the subdeacon carries the book of Epistles, resting against his breast in the manner described above for the Gospel book and the Missal, to the place where the Epistle is to be read; this will always be before the sanctuary steps, at a lectern prepared there in the middle, facing the altar. But in convents where it is the custom on Sundays and on Doubles and higher to read it in a raised place, from the pulpit, the custom is to be kept. The acolyte, when the Epistle has been read, carries the book handed him by the subdeacon to the altar and puts it in its place.

8. Sacerdotem vero Diaconus et alter Acolythorum astantes, in sede sua reverenter una collocent; et Acolythus, vel ipsemet Diaconus, si desit Acolythus, aliquam super ejus genua, ut supra, superponat mappulam.

8. The deacon and the other acolyte, standing by, together reverently seat the priest in his seat; and the acolyte, or the deacon himself if there is no acolyte, lays a small cloth over his knees as above.

9. Deinde Diaconus, ablutis primo digitis, explicet corporale in medio altaris, et postea Missale accipiens, tradat ipsum Acolytho, et sessum veniat; Sacerdos vero tunc sedendo, ac Acolytho, si sint duo, tenente Missale ante ipsum, vel Diacono, si desit Acolythus, dicat Epistolam, et deinde quae de Responsorio, vel Tractu, et Sequentia dicenda sunt, illaque, si Acolythus teneat Missale, per versus a Sacerdote et Diacono alternatim dicantur, incipiente Sacerdote: quibus dictis, surgant, et Sacerdos stans perlegat Evangelium, manibus junctis, appositis super ipsum, signans se signo crucis in initio, et fine, uti supra dictum est in Ritu Missae.

9. Then the deacon, having first washed his fingers, spreads the corporal in the middle of the altar, and afterwards, taking the Missal, hands it to the acolyte and comes to sit. The priest, then sitting, the acolyte (if there are two) or the deacon (if there is no acolyte) holding the Missal before him, says the Epistle, and then what is to be said of the Responsory or Tract and Sequence; and if the acolyte holds the Missal these are said verse by verse alternately by the priest and deacon, the priest beginning. These said, they rise, and the priest standing reads the Gospel with his joined hands laid upon the book, signing himself with the cross at the beginning and end, as was said above in the Rite of Mass.
