Quo dicto, Subdiaconus, dum fuerit reversus, Missale ad cornu Evangelii pro cantando Credo, vel Offertorio deferat; deinde aliquantulum manus lavet, mappam super humeros suos, ut dictum est supra, juvante Acolytho, extendat, calicem velo discooperiat, et sinistra manu per nodum cuppae accipiat, dextera desuper pallam et patenam firmans, et accedens, in oculis Sacerdotis, et Diaconi sedentis, praeparet hoc modo: Diacono patenam cum hostia tradat, qui eam Sacerdoti exhibebit, et tum vinum de manu Acolythi assistentis accipiat, illudque in calice infundat, sed ab una parte, et in illa quantitate, prout Sacerdos innuerit; postmodum aquam benedicendam Sacerdoti offerat, et dicto Benedicite, et responso a Diacono Dominus, subjungat Sacerdos benedictionem In nomine Patris, et Filii, et Spiritus Sancti, et Acolythus respondeat Amen, et parum, ut dictum est, aquae in medio calicis instillet; deinde patenam cum hostia recipiat a Diacono, et firmet, ut supra, et ad medium altaris reportet, ac cooperiat, sicut prius erat, et deinde sessum veniat.

This said, the subdeacon, when he has come back, carries the Missal to the Gospel corner for the singing of the Credo or the Offertory; then he washes his hands a little, spreads the veil over his shoulders with the acolyte's help as said above, uncovers the chalice, and takes it with his left hand by the knot of the cup, steadying the pall and paten on top with his right; and coming before the eyes of the priest and of the deacon, who are seated, he prepares it in this way: he hands the paten with the host to the deacon, who will show it to the priest; then he takes the wine from the hand of the assisting acolyte and pours it into the chalice, but at one side, and in such quantity as the priest indicates; then he offers the water to the priest to be blessed, and when he has said Benedicite and the deacon has answered Dominus, the priest adds the blessing In nomine Patris, et Filii, et Spiritus Sancti, the acolyte answering Amen; and he lets a little water fall into the middle of the chalice, as was said; then he receives the paten with the host from the deacon, steadies it as above, carries it back to the middle of the altar, covers it as it was before, and then comes to sit.

VII Ad TractumAt the Tract

10. Cum dicitur in Missa Veni sancte Spiritus, in inceptione ejus surgant Sacerdos et ministri, et non flectant genua, sed stent quamdiu cantatur Spiritus, usque ad reple; deinde, Choro surgente, sedeant ministri. Dum autem Sacerdos sedet, ministri, cum non sunt occupati in aliquo ministerio, possunt ire ad adjuvandum Fratres in choro, maxime in parvis Conventibus.

10. When the Veni sancte Spiritus is said in the Mass, at its beginning the priest and ministers rise, and do not kneel, but stand while Spiritus is sung, as far as reple; then, when the choir rises, the ministers sit. While the priest sits, the ministers, when they are not busy with some duty, may go to help the brethren in choir, especially in small convents.

Circa finem cantus Alleluia, vel Tractus, vel Responsorii, vel Sequentiae pro tempore, Acolythi cereos accendant, et Thuriferarius adsit in festis Simplicibus, et supra, et in Dominicis diebus, et in tertia Feria, quando fit de B. Dominico, in quarta, quando de B. Thoma, vel de alio Sancto Ordinis, in quinta, quando de SS. Corpore Christi, et in Sabbato, quando de B. Maria Virgine, et in die octava Simplicium Octavarum: qui stabit in presbyterio cum thuribulo ante medium altaris.

Towards the end of the chant of the Alleluia, Tract, Responsory or Sequence, according to the season, the acolytes light the candles; and the thurifer is present on Simple feasts and above, on Sundays, on Tuesday when it is of St Dominic, on Wednesday when it is of St Thomas or another saint of the Order, on Thursday when it is of the most holy Body of Christ, on Saturday when it is of the Blessed Virgin Mary, and on the octave day of simple octaves; he will stand in the sanctuary with the thurible before the middle of the altar.

In Totis autem Duplicibus, in quibus solis est crux ad Evangelium deferenda, cantata Epistola, dum cantatur Responsorium, vel Alleluia, vel Tractus, aut Sequentia, prout dicenda fuerint, Acolythi deferentes candelabra cum cereis accensis in sacristiam eant, Thuriferario praecedente, indeque crucem comitentur, praeeunte Thuriferario, et sequentibus Acolythis, in quorum medio Cruciferarius veniat, sicque procedant ante gradus altaris, Cruciferario in medio duorum Acolythorum stante, et Thuriferario ad dexteram Acolythi, qui est a dextris Cruciferarii, et sic omnes (excepto Cruciferario) profunde inclinent. Tunc Thuriferarius vadat ad Sacerdotem, et oblato ei thure, dicat Benedicite, et Sacerdos, facto signo crucis super thus, dicat In nomine Patris, et Filii, et Spiritus Sancti. Amen.

On Totum Duplex feasts, on which alone the cross is to be carried at the Gospel, after the Epistle has been sung, while the Responsory, Alleluia, Tract or Sequence is sung, as the case may be, the acolytes go to the sacristy carrying the candlesticks with lighted candles, the thurifer going before, and from there accompany the cross, the thurifer leading and the acolytes following with the crossbearer between them; and so they come before the altar steps, the crossbearer standing between the two acolytes and the thurifer at the right of the acolyte who is at the crossbearer's right; and so all (except the crossbearer) bow profoundly. Then the thurifer goes to the priest and, offering him the incense, says Benedicite; and the priest, making the sign of the cross over the incense, says In nomine Patris, et Filii, et Spiritus Sancti. Amen.

VIII Ad EvangeliumAt the Gospel

11. Mox surgat Diaconus, et accipiens ambabus manibus librum Evangeliorum, inclinatus parum corpore coram Sacerdote, dicat Jube domne benedicere, et Sacerdos surgens, subjungat Dominus sit in corde tuo, et in labiis tuis, ad pronuntiandum sanctum Evangelium pacis: In nomine Patris, et Filii, etc. Amen; et sic procedant ad pulpitum, primo Thuriferarius, secundo Ceroferarii, in quorum medio stet qui crucem fert, tertio Subdiaconus cum pulvinari pro Evangelio praeparato, ultimo Diaconus, librum Evangeliorum reclinatum supra pectus deferens, secundum modum supradictum.

11. Then the deacon rises and, taking the book of the Gospels in both hands, bowing a little before the priest, says Jube domne benedicere; and the priest, rising, answers Dominus sit in corde tuo, et in labiis tuis, ad pronuntiandum sanctum Evangelium pacis: In nomine Patris, et Filii, etc. Amen. And so they go to the pulpit: first the thurifer, second the candle-bearers with the crossbearer between them, third the subdeacon with the cushion prepared for the Gospel, last the deacon carrying the book of the Gospels resting against his breast in the manner described above.

Pulpitum autem erit praeparatum ad cornu Evangelii in loco parum eminenti, versus Aquilonem. In Conventibus tamen, in quibus fuerit pulpitum retro chorum, vel alibi in loco superiori, legetur Evangelium super illud Dominicis diebus, et festis Duplicibus, et supra. Postquam autem ad pulpitum pervenerint, Subdiaconus pulvinar libro supponat; ille vero qui defert crucem, et Ceroferarii, unus a dextris, et alius a sinistris ejus, a retro stent ante pulpitum, versis vultibus propriis et vultu Crucifixi ad Diaconum; Subdiaconus vero post Diaconum.

The lectern will be prepared at the Gospel corner in a slightly raised place, facing north. But in convents where there is a pulpit behind the choir, or elsewhere in a higher place, the Gospel is read from it on Sundays and on Doubles and above. When they have reached the lectern, the subdeacon puts the cushion under the book; the crossbearer and the candle-bearers, one at his right and the other at his left, stand behind, before the lectern, with their own faces and the face of the Crucified turned towards the deacon; the subdeacon stands behind the deacon.

Cum autem dicit Diaconus Sequentia, vel Initium sancti Evangelii, faciat unam crucem super Evangelii principium cum pollice, et etiam super frontem, aliam super os, et aliam super pectus; deinde signet se totum a fronte ad pectus et humeros signo crucis (idem faciant Sacerdos et Subdiaconus, versis vultibus ad altare): postea, accepto thuribulo de manu Subdiaconi, ter librum Evangelii thurificet, et junctis manibus ante pectus prosequatur Evangelium. Thuriferarius interim ad sinistram Acolythi, qui est a sinistris Cruciferarii, vel post Subdiaconum, ubi commode poterit, se collocet.

When the deacon says Sequentia or Initium sancti Evangelii, he makes a cross with his thumb on the beginning of the Gospel, and also one on his forehead, another on his mouth and another on his breast; then he signs his whole person with the sign of the cross from forehead to breast and shoulders (the priest and subdeacon do the same, facing the altar); then, taking the thurible from the subdeacon's hand, he incenses the book of the Gospel three times, and with hands joined before his breast continues the Gospel. Meanwhile the thurifer places himself at the left of the acolyte who is at the crossbearer's left, or behind the subdeacon, wherever it is convenient.

Cum autem Fratres dicunt Gloria tibi Domine, debent se vertere ad altare, et munire se signo crucis, ut supra dictum est. Dum autem Evangelium legitur, stet Sacerdos ad cornu Epistolae, versa facie ad Evangelium, junctis manibus ante pectus donec fuerit lectum; similiter omnes Fratres vertere debent faciem ad Evangelium, ubicumque legatur; et ipso finito, munire debent se signo crucis, a capite usque ad pectus et humeros.

When the brethren say Gloria tibi Domine, they must turn to the altar and sign themselves with the cross, as said above. While the Gospel is read, the priest stands at the Epistle corner, facing the Gospel, with hands joined before his breast until it has been read; likewise all the brethren must turn their faces to the Gospel, wherever it is read; and when it is finished they must sign themselves with the cross from the head to the breast and shoulders.

12. Finito Evangelio, tradat Diaconus librum apertum Subdiacono cum pulvinari; et revertantur ad altare, ordine quo venerunt. Si tamen pulpitum ita fuerit dispositum in aliquibus Conventibus, ut eundo ad illud transeant per chorum, Thuriferario transeunte per medium chori, et uno Ceroferario juxta unum Chorum, alio vero juxta alium, ille qui defert crucem, et Subdiaconus, et Diaconus vadant juxta Chorum dexterum, redeundo vero vadant juxta sinistrum.

12. When the Gospel is finished, the deacon hands the open book with the cushion to the subdeacon, and they return to the altar in the order in which they came. But if in some convents the pulpit is so placed that in going to it they pass through the choir, the thurifer passes through the middle of the choir and one candle-bearer along one side and the other along the other, while the crossbearer, the subdeacon and the deacon go along the right side, and on returning along the left.

IX Ad CredoAt the Credo

13. In fine Evangelii, Sacerdos ad medium altaris veniens, dicat Credo in unum Deum, si dicendum fuerit. Cumque ministri pervenerint ad altare, Subdiaconus offerat librum Sacerdoti ad osculandum, Diacono ei cum digito Evangelium demonstrante (nisi pro Defunctis celebretur, vel Passio fuerit lecta), et Sacerdos pollice signans Evangelium lectum signo crucis, dicat deosculando illud Per Evangelica dicta, etc. et Diaconus respondeat Amen. Post eum, Diaconus osculetur. Et post haec, Subdiaconus ipsum librum reponat super altare, ubi et sicut erat prius, inde, cum expediens fuerit, ipsum ad sacristiam reportaturus.

13. At the end of the Gospel the priest, coming to the middle of the altar, says Credo in unum Deum, if it is to be said. And when the ministers have reached the altar, the subdeacon offers the book to the priest to kiss, the deacon pointing out the Gospel to him with his finger (unless Mass is celebrated for the dead, or the Passion has been read); and the priest, signing the Gospel that was read with the cross with his thumb, says as he kisses it Per Evangelica dicta, etc., and the deacon answers Amen. After him the deacon kisses it. After this the subdeacon puts the book back on the altar where and as it was before, to carry it back to the sacristy from there when convenient.

Deinde Diaconus, et omnes alii ministri, stantes ad sinistram Sacerdotis ordinati ad cornu Evangelii unus post alium, Credo in unum Deum prosequantur submissa voce; et cum Chorus pervenerit ad illa verba, Et incarnatus est, Sacerdos ante medium altaris genu flectat; similiter Diaconus et Subdiaconus hinc et inde in primo gradu altaris post Sacerdotem flectant genua; post ipsos vero in alio inferiori gradu flectant genua Acolythi ordinati hinc inde, ita ut in medio eorum sit Cruciferarius, et a tergo Cruciferarii Thuriferarius, omnes versis vultibus ad altare.

Then the deacon and all the other ministers, standing at the priest's left, ranged one behind another towards the Gospel corner, continue the Credo in unum Deum in a low voice; and when the choir has come to the words Et incarnatus est, the priest kneels before the middle of the altar; likewise the deacon and subdeacon kneel on either side on the first step of the altar behind the priest; and behind them, on another, lower step, the acolytes kneel ranged on either side, so that the crossbearer is between them and the thurifer behind the crossbearer, all facing the altar.

Postmodum surgentes omnes, Acolythi et Thuriferarius comitentur crucem in sacristiam eo ordine, quo venerunt, facta prius inclinatione ante altare, praeeunte Thuriferario, qui semper debet esse post Subdiaconum, nisi cum aliter in Ordinario notatur. Cum autem in Credo dicuntur illa verba, simul adoratur, etc. debent inclinare omnes.

Afterwards all rise, and the acolytes and thurifer, having first bowed before the altar, accompany the cross to the sacristy in the order in which they came, the thurifer going before, who must always be behind the subdeacon except when the Ordinarium directs otherwise. And when the words simul adoratur, etc. are said in the Credo, all must bow.

X Ad OffertoriumAt the Offertory

14. Finito vero Credo, vel Evangelio, si Credo non dicitur, Sacerdos dicat Dominus vobiscum, Oremus, et Offertorium, quod ministri cum eo prosequentur, stantes omnes ad sinistram ejus ordinati, ut supra; quo dicto, ad medium altaris rediens, dicat Quid retribuam Domino pro omnibus, quae retribuit mihi? et interim tunc Subdiaconus calicem discooperiat velo et palla, ac offerat Diacono, et Diaconus, tenendo eum per inferiorem partem pedis, Sacerdoti offerat, osculando manum ejus (nisi SS. Sacramentum sit expositum), et dicat Immola Deo sacrificium laudis, et redde Altissimo vota tua: quem accipiens Sacerdos, dicat Calicem salutaris accipiam, et nomen Domini invocabo, et deinde tenens eum ambabus manibus aliquantulum elevatum, dicat Suscipe, etc.

14. When the Credo is finished, or the Gospel if the Credo is not said, the priest says Dominus vobiscum, Oremus, and the Offertory, which the ministers continue with him, all standing ranged at his left as above; this said, returning to the middle of the altar he says Quid retribuam Domino pro omnibus, quae retribuit mihi? Meanwhile the subdeacon uncovers the chalice of veil and pall and offers it to the deacon; and the deacon, holding it by the lower part of the foot, offers it to the priest, kissing his hand (unless the Most Holy Sacrament is exposed), and says Immola Deo sacrificium laudis, et redde Altissimo vota tua; and the priest, taking it, says Calicem salutaris accipiam, et nomen Domini invocabo, and then, holding it a little raised in both hands, says Suscipe, etc.

XI De thurificatione altarisOf the incensing of the altar

15. Tunc Thuriferario tenente thuribulum a dextera parte altaris, et Diacono sumptum thus de vasculo, in quo servatur, offerente in cochleari Sacerdoti ad benedictionem, benedicat ipsum Sacerdos secundum modum qui scriptus est supra: quo facto, ponat ipsum Diaconus in thuribulo, et assumptum thuribulum tradat statim Sacerdoti, manum ipsius osculando (si SS. Sacramentum non sit expositum); qui assumens illud manu dextera, brevi intervallo e catenis suspensum, sinistra manu tenens catenarum extremitates, thuribulo signum crucis faciat super calicem, postea caput inclinet, et deinde ter ante eumdem thurificet, non vibrando in altum thuribulum, sed modice demissa cum thuribulo manu, modiceque subinde elevata, ita ut numquam supra humerum manus attollantur.

15. Then, the thurifer holding the thurible at the right side of the altar, and the deacon offering the priest in the spoon incense taken from the boat in which it is kept, for the blessing, the priest blesses it in the manner written above; this done, the deacon puts it into the thurible and, taking the thurible, hands it at once to the priest, kissing his hand (if the Most Holy Sacrament is not exposed). The priest, taking it in his right hand, hanging a short way down the chains, and holding the ends of the chains in his left, makes the sign of the cross with the thurible over the chalice, then bows his head, and then incenses three times before it, not swinging the thurible high, but with his hand lowered a little with the thurible and then raised a little, so that the hands are never lifted above the shoulder.

Postmodum tabernaculum SS. Corporis Christi, si fuerit super altare, ter incenset, vel crucem, si non fuerit; deinde inclinando cum Diacono profunde ad medium altaris (quod semper est servandum, dum fit transitus ab uno latere altaris ad aliud), inde incenset desuper altare versus candelabra, ter ducens thuribulum aequali distantia usque ad cornu Epistolae; hinc, demissa manu, revertitur ad medium altaris, ex quo procedendo thurificet aliud latus eodem modo usque ad cornu Evangelii; deinde incenset anteriorem partem, seu frontem altaris similiter triplici ductu dum procedit a cornu Evangelii ad medium, et postea etiam triplici ductu reliquam partem anteriorem usque ad cornu Epistolae. Interea dum Sacerdos thurificat, Diaconus decenter casulam ejus sublevet, ut expedite possit incensare.

Then he incenses three times the tabernacle of the most holy Body of Christ, if it is on the altar, or the cross if it is not; then, bowing profoundly with the deacon at the middle of the altar (which is always to be done in passing from one side of the altar to the other), he incenses the top of the altar towards the candlesticks, swinging the thurible three times at equal distances as far as the Epistle corner; from there, with his hand lowered, he returns to the middle of the altar, and going on from there incenses the other side in the same way as far as the Gospel corner; then he incenses the front of the altar, likewise with three swings, going from the Gospel corner to the middle, and afterwards, also with three swings, the rest of the front as far as the Epistle corner. While the priest incenses, the deacon becomingly raises his chasuble, so that he can incense freely.

16. Si vero in altari Reliquiae, sive imagines Sanctorum fuerint, incensato tabernaculo, aut cruce, si illud non fuerit, et facta ei reverentia ut supra, priusquam discedat a medio altaris, prius incenset eas, quae a dexteris sunt, idest a parte Evangelii prope crucem, bis ducens thuribulum; et interim facta tabernaculo, aut cruci reverentia, similiter incensabit alias, quae sunt a sinistris, idest a parte Epistolae; quo facto, incensationem altaris prosequetur, ut supra, ter ducens thuribulum in unoquoque latere, quamvis in eo plures Reliquiae, vel imagines essent, sive etiam plura, vel pauciora candelabra (idem servetur in Vesperis, et in Matutinis, dum incensatio fieri debet).

16. If there are relics or images of the saints on the altar, when the tabernacle, or the cross if there is none, has been incensed and reverence made to it as above, before he leaves the middle of the altar he first incenses those on the right, that is on the Gospel side near the cross, swinging the thurible twice; and, having meanwhile made reverence to the tabernacle or cross, he likewise incenses the others, on the left, that is on the Epistle side; this done, he continues the incensing of the altar as above, swinging the thurible three times on each side, however many relics or images there may be on it, or more or fewer candlesticks (the same is observed at Vespers and Matins when the incensing is to be done).

Interim alii ministri, dum in Missa fit incensatio, ordinent se in cornu Epistolae, ita ut Subdiaconus sit prope altare, et a sinistris ipsius Acolythi, et Thuriferarius suo ordine, unus infra alium. Dum autem Sacerdos desierit thurificare, manus junctas teneat ante pectus, stans in fine altaris, ubi incensare desiit, verso vultu ad Diaconum. Diaconus vero thuribulum de manu Sacerdotis accipiens, triplici ductu eum incenset, sublevando casulam leviter manu sinistra, et dextera tenendo thuribulum; postmodum reddat illud Thuriferario.

Meanwhile, while the incensing is done at Mass, the other ministers range themselves at the Epistle corner, so that the subdeacon is near the altar, and at his left the acolytes and the thurifer in their order, one below the other. When the priest has finished incensing he holds his hands joined before his breast, standing at the end of the altar where he finished incensing, facing the deacon. The deacon, taking the thurible from the priest's hand, incenses him with three swings, lightly raising the chasuble with his left hand and holding the thurible in his right; then he gives it back to the thurifer.

XII Ad lotionem manuum, et ad SecretasAt the washing of the hands, and at the Secrets

17. Postquam autem Diaconus incensaverit Sacerdotem, Subdiacono aquam fundente, Sacerdos lavet manus. Dum vero Subdiaconus aquam superfundit, Diaconus accipiens linteolum, vel manutergium ab Acolytho, illud porriget Sacerdoti ad manus abstergendas; et dum abluit Sacerdos manus, dicat Lavabo, etc. et postea In spiritu, etc.

17. When the deacon has incensed the priest, the priest washes his hands, the subdeacon pouring the water. While the subdeacon pours the water, the deacon, taking the cloth or towel from the acolyte, hands it to the priest to dry his hands; and while the priest washes his hands he says Lavabo, etc., and afterwards In spiritu, etc.

18. Deinde dicat Orate Fratres, etc. solum haec duo verba mediocri voce, ita ut possit audiri a ministris; postea dicat secretas Orationes: Diaconus vero casulam elevet, ut supra dictum est. Tempore quoque muscarum debet eas Diaconus flabello amovere, ne molestent Sacerdotem, et abigere a sacrificio.

18. Then he says Orate fratres, etc., only these two words in a moderate voice, so that they can be heard by the ministers; afterwards he says the secret prayers, the deacon raising the chasuble as said above. In the season of flies, too, the deacon must keep them off with a fan, lest they trouble the priest, and drive them from the sacrifice.

XIII De thurificatione ministrorum, et ConventusOf the incensing of the ministers and the community

19. Cum dicitur Praefatio, Thuriferarius praeparet se ad thurificandum, et cantatis illis verbis Gratias agamus Domino Deo nostro, prius inclinatione facta ante altare, thurificet ministros altaris, primo Diaconum, deinde Subdiaconum, et Acolythos, qui ita se, ut thurificentur, collocabunt, ut Diaconus cum Acolytho a cornu Evangelii, et Subdiaconus cum alio a cornu Epistolae fere mutuo se respiciant, junctis manibus ante pectus, et paululum inclinantes caput, dum thurificantur.

19. While the Preface is said the thurifer prepares to incense, and when the words Gratias agamus Domino Deo nostro have been sung, having first bowed before the altar, he incenses the ministers of the altar: first the deacon, then the subdeacon and the acolytes, who place themselves to be incensed so that the deacon with one acolyte at the Gospel corner and the subdeacon with the other at the Epistle corner almost face each other, with hands joined before the breast and bowing the head a little while they are incensed.

His incensatis, Thuriferarius vadat in chorum, et thurificet Magistrum Ordinis, si adest; postea alios (secundum quod de aqua benedicta est dictum) de thure tamen non benedicto, quod de novo ponat in thuribulo, atque incipiat ab antiquioribus in sedibus superioribus, et a junioribus in sedibus inferioribus se gyrando, si fuerint ibi fratres, prosequendo thurificationem in utroque Choro, deinde Fratres laicos. Et his peractis, revertatur ad altare, in medio stans post Subdiaconum.

These incensed, the thurifer goes into the choir and incenses the Master of the Order, if present; then the others (according to what was said of the holy water), but with unblessed incense, which he puts afresh into the thurible; and he begins with the seniors in the upper stalls, and turning round to the juniors in the lower stalls, if there are brethren there, continues the incensing in both choirs, and then the lay brothers. This done, he returns to the altar and stands in the middle behind the subdeacon.

XIV Ad Sanctus, et ad CanonemAt the Sanctus, and at the Canon

20. Deinde dicta Praefatione, cum ventum fuerit ad supplici confessione, etc., Diaconus cum uno Acolythorum (aliquot ictibus campanulae astantes admonente) veniens ad cornu Evangelii, et Subdiaconus cum alio ad cornu Epistolae, versis vultibus ad invicem, dicant cum Sacerdote Sanctus, etc. et ad Benedictus qui venit, muniant se signo crucis a capite ad pectus et humeros. Quod etiam faciendum est a Fratribus in choro, cum ipsi dicent Benedictus, nec debent se tunc vertere ad altare.

20. Then, the Preface having been said, when supplici confessione, etc. is reached, the deacon with one of the acolytes (who warns those present with a few strokes of the bell) comes to the Gospel corner, and the subdeacon with the other to the Epistle corner, and facing each other they say the Sanctus, etc. with the priest, and at Benedictus qui venit sign themselves with the cross from head to breast and shoulders. The brethren in choir are also to do this when they say Benedictus, and they are not to turn to the altar then.

21. Tunc si Dominica fuerit, vel festum Simplex, aut majus, vel Octava Solemnis, aut dies octava Simplicium Octavarum, aut iij. Feria, in qua agitur de Beato Dominico, vel v. in qua de Sanctissimo Sacramento, vel Sabbatum, in quo de B. Virgine, vel alia, in qua de Sancto Ordinis celebratur, Subdiaconus mappam sumat, et eam super humeros suos extendat, et Diaconus ei patenam tradat, et cooperiat eam velo calicis, vel mappam replicet super eam, et Acolythi cereos accendant pro elevatione (ceteris vero diebus patena a Subdiacono non teneatur). Deinde Subdiaconus, stans post Diaconum, patenam usque post Pater noster teneat elevatam manu, sinistram dextero brachio supponens.

21. Then, if it is a Sunday, or a Simple feast or greater, or a solemn octave, or the octave day of simple octaves, or a Tuesday on which St Dominic is kept, or a Thursday of the Most Holy Sacrament, or a Saturday of the Blessed Virgin, or another day on which a saint of the Order is celebrated, the subdeacon takes the veil and spreads it over his shoulders, and the deacon hands him the paten and covers it with the chalice veil, or folds the veil back over it; and the acolytes light the candles for the elevation (on other days the paten is not held by the subdeacon). Then the subdeacon, standing behind the deacon, holds the paten raised in his hand until after the Pater noster, putting his left hand under his right arm.

XV Ad consecrationemAt the consecration

22. Interim dum Sacerdos profert verba consecrationis, Diaconus a dexteris Sacerdotis, et Subdiaconus a sinistris cum patena, flectant genua super gradum supremum altaris, uno Acolythorum in gradu inferiori stante juxta Diaconum, et altero juxta Subdiaconum, et unus pulset campanulam, et sic stent a tempore elevationis Hostiae usque post Sanguinis consecrationem et elevationem. Et in diebus, in quibus ministratur incensum, Diaconus accipiat thuribulum a Thuriferario, vel ab uno Acolythorum, ubi sunt pauci Fratres, et incenset continue in elevatione Corporis, et Sanguinis; postea reddat thuribulum ipsi Thuriferario, vel Acolytho, qui illud reportet in sacristiam, ac ibi dimittat.

22. Meanwhile, while the priest pronounces the words of consecration, the deacon at the priest's right and the subdeacon with the paten at his left kneel on the top step of the altar, one acolyte standing on a lower step beside the deacon and the other beside the subdeacon, and one rings the bell; and so they remain from the time of the elevation of the Host until after the consecration and elevation of the Blood. On the days on which incense is used, the deacon takes the thurible from the thurifer, or from one of the acolytes where the brethren are few, and incenses continuously during the elevation of the Body and of the Blood; afterwards he gives the thurible back to the thurifer or acolyte, who carries it back to the sacristy and leaves it there.

23. In omni autem tempore sic Chorus cavere debet a nimia prolixitate cantus, dum dicit Sanctus, etc., et Sacerdos sic morose debet perficere ea, quae dicuntur ante elevationem Hostiae, quod numquam fiat elevatio, quousque praedictus cantus non sit terminatus.

23. At all times the choir must so avoid excessive length of chant in singing the Sanctus, etc., and the priest must so deliberately complete what is said before the elevation of the Host, that the elevation never takes place before that chant has ended.

XVI Ad Pater nosterAt the Pater noster

24. Cum Sacerdos inchoat Pater noster, Subdiaconus manu sinistra discooperiat patenam, quam dextera tenet, diebus supra notatis; dum vero Sacerdos dicit Et ne nos inducas in tentationem, Diaconus cum aliis ministris respondeat submissa voce Sed libera nos a malo, et Sacerdos Amen; et tunc Subdiaconus porrigat Diacono patenam, qui eam recipiat manu nuda, ac tradat Sacerdoti, quando dicturus est Da propitius pacem: qui ipsam recipiet manu dextera inter indicem et medium digitum, pollice et indice non disjunctis, et Diaconus, cum tradit eam, ipsius humerum osculetur (nisi SS. Corpus Christi fuerit expositum super altare); et Sacerdos signans se patena, ut in Missa privata, et post osculans eam, super altare deponat seorsum a corporali.

24. When the priest begins the Pater noster, on the days noted above the subdeacon uncovers with his left hand the paten which he holds in his right; and while the priest says Et ne nos inducas in tentationem, the deacon with the other ministers answers in a low voice Sed libera nos a malo, and the priest Amen; and then the subdeacon hands the paten to the deacon, who receives it with his bare hand and gives it to the priest when he is about to say Da propitius pacem; the priest receives it in his right hand between the forefinger and middle finger, the thumb and forefinger not being parted; and the deacon, as he gives it, kisses his shoulder (unless the most holy Body of Christ is exposed on the altar); and the priest, signing himself with the paten as at private Mass and then kissing it, lays it on the altar apart from the corporal.

Et dum Sacerdos signat se patena, Acolythi reverenter plicent mappam, et qui est a sinistris, reponat eam super cornu Evangelii. Quando vero Subdiaconus patenam non tenuerit, nihilominus Diaconus post Pater noster, ut dictum est, eam accipiat de altari, et praedicto modo offerat Sacerdoti.

And while the priest signs himself with the paten, the acolytes reverently fold the veil, and the one on the left puts it on the Gospel corner. When the subdeacon has not held the paten, the deacon nevertheless takes it from the altar after the Pater noster, as was said, and offers it to the priest in the manner described.

XVII Ad Agnus Dei, et pacemAt the Agnus Dei and the peace

25. Postquam autem Sacerdos dixerit Pax Domini sit semper vobiscum, Diaconus cum uno Acolythorum ad cornu Evangelii, et Subdiaconus cum alio ad cornu Epistolae vadant, et mutuo se respicientes, dicant cum eo Agnus Dei, etc. Deinde dicto Haec sacrosancta commixtio, etc., calicis labium osculetur; postea patenam, vel aliquam sanctam imaginem (quae Pax communiter dicitur), sibi illico porrectam a Diacono osculetur, dicendo submissa voce Pax tibi, et Ecclesiae sanctae Dei (quod non faciet in triduo ante Pascha, et in Missis Defunctorum). Deinde Diaconus det eamdem Subdiacono osculandam, et Subdiaconus Acolythis, qui hac de causa in medio post Subdiaconum tunc esse debebunt.

25. When the priest has said Pax Domini sit semper vobiscum, the deacon with one acolyte goes to the Gospel corner and the subdeacon with the other to the Epistle corner, and facing each other they say the Agnus Dei, etc. with him. Then, Haec sacrosancta commixtio, etc. having been said, the priest kisses the lip of the chalice; afterwards he kisses the paten, or some holy image (which is commonly called the Pax), held out to him at once by the deacon, saying in a low voice Pax tibi, et Ecclesiae sanctae Dei (which he does not do in the Triduum before Easter, nor in Masses for the dead). Then the deacon gives it to the subdeacon to kiss, and the subdeacon to the acolytes, who for this reason must then be in the middle behind the subdeacon.

In Dominicis autem diebus, et festis Duplicibus, et supra, postquam Acolythi pacem a Subdiacono acceperint, ut dictum est, alter eorum Cantoribus (nisi Magister Ordinis adsit) dabit; Cantor vero ordine, quo ut supra de aqua benedicta dictum fuit, Fratribus dabit, postquam terminatus erit totus cantus Agnus Dei. In ceteris autem diebus numquam detur pax in Missa Conventuali, nisi ministris Missae.

On Sundays and on Doubles and above, after the acolytes have received the peace from the subdeacon as was said, one of them gives it to the cantors (unless the Master of the Order is present); and the cantor gives it to the brethren in the order described above for the holy water, after the whole chant of the Agnus Dei has ended. On other days the peace is never given at the conventual Mass except to the ministers of the Mass.

Porro in magnis solemnitatibus, scilicet Natalis, Paschatis, Ascensionis, Pentecostes, SS. Corporis Christi, Assumptionis Beatae Mariae Virginis, B. P. N. Dominici, et Omnium Sanctorum, et similibus, Subdiaconus, postquam pacem Acolythis, ut praefertur, dederit, deferat eam Cantoribus in choro, altero Acolythorum praecedente, deinde reliquis Fratribus ordine et modo, ut dictum est. Ubi autem chorus non fuerit juxta altare, Subdiaconus non deferat pacem, nisi commode reverti possit ante finem communionis, sed deportetur a Thuriferario, vel ab uno Acolythorum.

Further, on the great solemnities, namely Christmas, Easter, the Ascension, Pentecost, the most holy Body of Christ, the Assumption of the Blessed Virgin Mary, our Blessed Father Dominic, All Saints and the like, the subdeacon, after giving the peace to the acolytes as stated, carries it to the cantors in the choir, one of the acolytes going before, and then to the rest of the brethren in the order and manner described. But where the choir is not next to the altar, the subdeacon does not carry the peace unless he can conveniently return before the end of the Communion; it is carried instead by the thurifer or by one of the acolytes.

XVIII Ad CommunionemAt the Communion

26. Sacerdos itaque, data pace Diacono, dicat Orationem Domine Jesu Christe, etc., et Corpus et Sanguis Domini nostri, etc. nullas alias intermiscendo Orationes, neque pectus percutiendo; et postquam communicaverit, veniat ad cornu Epistolae, ibique Subdiaconus in ampulla vinum paratum habens, infundat in calice, postea vinum et aquam. Interim Diaconus Missale deferat ad cornu Epistolae, et ablutis primo digitis, corporale plicet, et reponat in bursam. Sacerdos autem, sumpta secunda ablutione, calicem deponat, et Subdiaconus, ablutis etiam prius digitis, eum accipiat, ac purificet, eique purificatorium, patenam, pallam, velum, bursam corporalis, et mappulam superponat: Acolythi vero cereos in elevatione accensos exstinguant.

26. So the priest, having given the peace to the deacon, says the prayer Domine Jesu Christe, etc., and Corpus et Sanguis Domini nostri, etc., inserting no other prayers and not striking his breast; and when he has communicated he comes to the Epistle corner, and there the subdeacon, having wine ready in a cruet, pours it into the chalice, and afterwards wine and water. Meanwhile the deacon carries the Missal to the Epistle corner and, having first washed his fingers, folds the corporal and puts it back in the burse. The priest, having taken the second ablution, sets down the chalice, and the subdeacon, having also first washed his fingers, takes it and purifies it, and puts on it the purificator, paten, pall, veil, the burse of the corporal and the small veil; and the acolytes put out the candles lit at the elevation.

XIX Ad PostcommunionemAt the Postcommunion

27. Postmodum Sacerdos dicat Dominus vobiscum, et Diaconus elevet casulam sicut prius.

27. Then the priest says Dominus vobiscum, and the deacon raises the chasuble as before.

28. Quando autem Missa erit de Feria in Quadragesima, post omnes memorias, dicto a Sacerdote, inclinante caput cum aliis ministris, Oremus, subjungat Diaconus clara voce Humiliate capita vestra Deo, et Sacerdos prosequatur Orationem signatam super populum.

28. When the Mass is of a Lenten feria, after all the commemorations, the priest having said Oremus, bowing his head with the other ministers, the deacon adds aloud Humiliate capita vestra Deo, and the priest continues with the prayer marked "over the people".

29. Dum autem ultimae Orationes dicuntur, Subdiaconus, altero Acolythorum praecedente, vinum et aquam deferente, calicem praeparatum, ut dictum est, in sacristiam reportet, non extensa mappa super humeros. Et cum iterum redierit ad altare, unus Acolythorum librum Evangelii ei tradat, et alter cereos super candelabra accendat. Si vero unica tantum Oratio dicenda sit, vel sacristia nimis distet ab altari, in quo celebratur, tum Subdiaconus calicem in sacristiam non reportet, nisi absoluta Missa, quando cum Sacerdote et aliis ad sacristiam redierit; et postea unus Acolythorum deferat librum Evangeliorum.

29. While the last prayers are said, the subdeacon, one of the acolytes going before carrying the wine and water, carries the chalice, arranged as was said, back to the sacristy, without the veil spread over his shoulders. When he has come back to the altar, one of the acolytes hands him the book of the Gospel, and the other lights the candles on the candlesticks. But if only one prayer is to be said, or the sacristy is too far from the altar at which Mass is celebrated, the subdeacon does not carry the chalice back to the sacristy until Mass is over, when he returns to the sacristy with the priest and the others; and afterwards one of the acolytes carries the book of the Gospels.

XX Ad Ite Missa est, et post benedictionemAt the Ite Missa est, and after the blessing

30. Cum Sacerdos, ad dicendum Dominus vobiscum, se vertit ad Conventum, Diaconus similiter se vertat, et dicat Ite Missa est, si Gloria in excelsis praecesserit: alioquin Diaconus non se vertat ad Conventum, sed versa facie ad altare, ipso Sacerdote similiter verso, dicat Benedicamus Domino, vel Requiescant in pace, si Missa fuerit de mortuis. Sacerdos vero, et Diaconus tantum stent versi ad Conventum, quamdiu dicitur Ite Missa est; deinde vertant se ad altare non se gyrando.

30. When the priest turns to the community to say Dominus vobiscum, the deacon turns likewise and says Ite Missa est, if the Gloria in excelsis has gone before; otherwise the deacon does not turn to the community, but facing the altar, the priest also facing it, says Benedicamus Domino, or Requiescant in pace if the Mass is of the dead. The priest and deacon stand turned to the community only while the Ite Missa est is said; then they turn back to the altar without going round.

31. Interim postquam Sacerdos dederit benedictionem, ad quam ministri debent caput inclinare, senior Acolythorum Missale Diacono tradat, et alter librum Evangeliorum Subdiacono (si non debeat deferre calicem), et deinde candelabra cum cereis accensis prae manibus teneant, dum legitur Evangelium: Diaconus vero et Subdiaconus ascendant ad sinistram Sacerdotis ad cornu Evangelii pro illo audiendo. Evangelio dicto, et facta inclinatione, vel genuflexione, sicut in ingressu, redeant omnes in sacristiam, eo ordine, quo venerunt. Tunc ministri, primo Sacerdotem exuant, postea se mutuo juvent ad exuendum.

31. Meanwhile, after the priest has given the blessing, at which the ministers must bow their heads, the senior acolyte hands the Missal to the deacon and the other the book of the Gospels to the subdeacon (if he is not to carry the chalice), and then they hold the candlesticks with lighted candles in their hands while the Gospel is read; and the deacon and subdeacon go up to the priest's left at the Gospel corner to hear it. When the Gospel has been said, and a bow or genuflexion made as at the entrance, all return to the sacristy in the order in which they came. Then the ministers first unvest the priest, and afterwards help one another to unvest.

32. Acolythi antequam vestes exuant, revertentes ad altare, cooperiant illud, et aptent decenter, reportantes in sacristiam, quae fuerint reportanda. Postea in Dominicis diebus deferant aquam benedictam per cellas, et reliquas officinas, eas aspergendo.

32. The acolytes, before they take off their vestments, go back to the altar, cover it and put it in good order, carrying back to the sacristy what is to be carried back. Afterwards, on Sundays, they carry holy water through the cells and the other offices, sprinkling them.

33. Ubi autem in diebus solemnibus, propter paucitatem Fratrum, praedicta ministrorum solemnitas servari non poterit, prout commodius et honestius poterit, observetur. In ferialibus vero diebus, et festis trium Lectionum, et infra Octavas non solemnes, unus sufficiat Acolythus: qui, dum fit confessio, et dum dicitur Sanctus, et Agnus Dei a Sacerdote, stare debet a dexteris juxta Diaconum, et etiam in genuflexione ad Corpus Domini.

33. Where on solemn days the solemnity of the ministers described cannot be kept because the brethren are few, it is to be observed as conveniently and becomingly as possible. On ferial days, on feasts of three lessons, and within non-solemn octaves, one acolyte suffices; he must stand at the right beside the deacon during the confession, while the Sanctus and the Agnus Dei are said by the priest, and also at the genuflexion at the Body of the Lord.

De sacra CommunioneOf Holy Communion (the Communion of the brethren in the convent)

1. Circa sacram Communionem, et modum communicandi in Conventu, notandum est, quod, ad reservandam sacram Communionem, haberi debet tabernaculum decens et honestum super altare, vel in alio decenti loco ad SS. Sacramentum Corporis Christi honorifice collocandum. Infra quod habenda est pyxis argentea, vel ex alia materia pretiosa, in qua servandae sunt hostiae consecratae; non tamen in magna multitudine. Provideatur autem, quod tam tabernaculum, quam pyxis praedicta sic caute stabiliantur, et claudantur, ne aliquo casu possint hostiae praedictae cadere, vel inde removeri, nisi per ministerium Fratrum, cum fuerit aliqua Communio facienda.

1. Concerning Holy Communion and the manner of communicating in the convent, it is to be noted that, for reserving Holy Communion, there must be a fitting and seemly tabernacle on the altar, or in another fitting place, in which the Most Holy Sacrament of the Body of Christ is honourably placed. Inside it there is to be a pyx of silver, or of other precious material, in which the consecrated hosts are kept, yet not in great number. Care is to be taken that both the tabernacle and the pyx are so securely fixed and closed that the hosts cannot by any chance fall or be removed, except by the ministry of the brethren when some Communion is to be given.

2. Die autem Communionis consuevit fieri aliqua exhortatio in Capitulo, vel absolutio generalis Fratribus tam Clericis, quam laicis, ut ad tantum Sacramentum dignius accedant praeparati. Debet autem Praelatus, si commode potest, vel aliquis ex gravioribus Patribus, in hujusmodi die Missam conventualem celebrare.

2. On a Communion day it is customary that some exhortation be given in Chapter, or a general absolution to the brethren, clerics and lay brothers alike, so that they may approach so great a Sacrament more worthily prepared. On such a day the prelate, if he conveniently can, or one of the senior Fathers, should celebrate the conventual Mass.

3. Cum igitur Subdiaconus ministraturus fuerit necessaria pro Sacramento hujusmodi, a Sacrista, cujus interest scire, debet inquirere de numero hostiarum consecratarum, quae sunt in pyxide; debet etiam inquirere de numero Fratrum, qui sunt communicandi: et facta collatione, tot hostias consecrandas ponat, quot sufficiant Fratribus communicandis, et pro infirmis in necessitate.

3. When the subdeacon is to supply what is needed for this Sacrament, he must ask the sacristan, whose business it is to know, the number of consecrated hosts in the pyx; he must also ask the number of brethren who are to communicate; and having compared them, he puts out as many hosts to be consecrated as will suffice for the brethren communicating and for the sick in case of need.

4. Sacerdos celebrans consecraturus plures hostias, reponet eas in aliquo calice consecrato, aut pyxide consecrata prope calicem, semper tamen super corporale; quod si non fuerit vas distinctum, collocet eas super corporale ante calicem, si ob quantitatem super patenam manere non possint. In consecratione proferat verba formae super hostiam, quam manibus tenet, et super omnes alias, quas suis oculis habebit expositas. Post consecrationem decenti loco super corporale eas aptet, ita ut ceteras caeremonias sine impedimento prosequi possit.

4. The celebrating priest who is to consecrate several hosts puts them in a consecrated chalice, or in a consecrated pyx near the chalice, but always on the corporal; if there is no separate vessel, he places them on the corporal before the chalice, if because of their number they cannot stay on the paten. At the consecration he pronounces the words of the form over the host which he holds in his hands, and over all the others which he will have exposed before his eyes. After the consecration he arranges them in a fitting place on the corporal, so that he can continue the other ceremonies without hindrance.

5. Cum igitur hora Communionis appropinquaverit, Fratres communicandi accedant in modum Chori ordinati versus presbyterium, et ibi, facta inclinatione profunda, flectant genua. Diaconus vero, si in altari, ubi celebratur, non sit tabernaculum continens in pyxide hostias consecratas, Ceroferariis praecedentibus cum cereis, afferat pyxidem supradictam cum SS. Corpore Domini super corporale, aperiens statim eam; tunc vero omnes Fratres, praeter Sacerdotem celebrantem, qui solus genuflexus adoret, videntes ipsum deferentem pyxidem, prosternant se super genua Sanctissimum Sacramentum adorantes, quousque illud super corporale deposuerit.

5. So when the hour of Communion draws near, the brethren who are to communicate come towards the sanctuary, ranged as in choir, and there, having bowed profoundly, they kneel. The deacon, if there is no tabernacle containing consecrated hosts in a pyx on the altar where Mass is celebrated, brings the pyx with the most holy Body of the Lord, the candle-bearers going before with candles, and sets it on the corporal, opening it at once; and all the brethren, except the celebrating priest, who alone adores genuflecting, on seeing him carry the pyx prostrate themselves on their knees, adoring the Most Holy Sacrament, until he has laid it on the corporal.

Quo facto, super genua erecti, brevique oratione facta, iterum ministri, ut antea prostrati, alii vero ad modum, quo fit venia, in longum extensi, dicant omnes simul Confiteor, etc. et Sacerdos paululum secedens a medio altaris, ne terga vertat Sanctissimo Sacramento, faciat absolutionem, dicendo Misereatur, etc. et Absolutionem, etc.

This done, kneeling upright and having made a short prayer, the ministers again prostrate as before, and the others stretched at full length in the manner of the venia, all say the Confiteor, etc. together; and the priest, stepping a little aside from the middle of the altar so as not to turn his back to the Most Holy Sacrament, gives the absolution, saying Misereatur, etc. and Absolutionem, etc.

6. Postmodum erigant se omnes super genua, et primo communicent ministri secundum ordinem graduum suorum: qui accedentes ante medium altaris, simul infra infimum gradum faciant inclinationem profundam, et ad supremum ascendentes, iterum ibi profunde inclinent, et mox genua flectant, toto corpore reliquo stantes erecti, caventes a transversione oculorum, vel insolita vultus indispositione, seu motu indisciplinato, sed aperto mediocriter ore, cum debita reverentia suscipiant de manu Sacerdotis Sanctissimum Christi Corpus. Sumpta vero communione, simul se erigant, simulque etiam profunde inclinent, et discedant versis vultibus ad invicem in latus incedendo, ne terga vertant Sanctissimo Sacramento, et infra infimum gradum altaris iterum profunde coram Sanctissimo inclinent, ac postea in presbyterio genua flectant.

6. Afterwards all raise themselves on their knees, and first the ministers communicate according to the order of their rank: coming before the middle of the altar, they bow profoundly together below the lowest step, and going up to the top step bow profoundly there again, and at once kneel, keeping the rest of the body upright, avoiding any rolling of the eyes, unusual disarrangement of the face or undisciplined movement, and with the mouth moderately open they receive with due reverence the most holy Body of Christ from the priest's hand. Having received Communion, they rise together, bow profoundly together, and withdraw facing one another, stepping sideways so as not to turn their backs to the Most Holy Sacrament; and below the lowest step of the altar they again bow profoundly before the Most Holy, and then kneel in the sanctuary.

7. Interim dum ministri communicant, ceteri Fratres, superinduti cappa Ordinis, bini et bini juxta ordinem suum successive accedant ante medium altaris, et cum eo pervenerint, infra gradus similiter profunde inclinent: et dum ministri a supremo gradu recedunt, in eorum locum succedant, factaque etiam inclinatione profunda, genua flectant; et recepta communione, eodem ordine, et modo, quo jam dictum est de ministris, inde recedant, nisi quod non in presbyterio genuflexi maneant, sed in chorum ad loca sua revertantur, ac seorsum secreto in gratiarum actionem orent: caveantque omnes, ne post communionem cito spuant: quod si cavere non possint, debent in loco honesto projicere sputum.

7. Meanwhile, while the ministers communicate, the other brethren, wearing the cappa of the Order, come two by two in their order one after another before the middle of the altar, and when they arrive there bow profoundly below the steps likewise; and as the ministers withdraw from the top step they take their place, and having also bowed profoundly they kneel; and having received Communion they withdraw in the same order and manner as described for the ministers, except that they do not remain kneeling in the sanctuary, but return to their places in choir and pray apart in silence in thanksgiving. And all are to take care not to spit soon after Communion; if they cannot avoid it, they must spit in a seemly place.

8. In primis autem cum ministri communicare debent, Diaconus et Subdiaconus, flectentes genua in superiori gradu altaris, hinc et inde, versis vultibus ad invicem, teneant mappulam mundam et honestam, ad hoc praeparatam, inter Sacerdotem et communicantes extensam, illamque sub mento ambabus manibus communicantes elevent; postquam vero Acolythi communicaverint, teneant praedictam mappulam praedicto modo, quousque communicaverint omnes.

8. First, when the ministers are to communicate, the deacon and subdeacon, kneeling on the upper step of the altar on either side and facing each other, hold a clean and seemly cloth prepared for this, stretched between the priest and those communicating, and those communicating raise it under their chin with both hands; after the acolytes have communicated, they hold this cloth in the same way until all have communicated.

9. Sacerdos vero ministret primo hostias consecratas, quae erant reservatae in pyxide, et postea, quae recenter sunt consecratae, ita ut paucae remaneant post Communionem in pyxide reponendae et asservandae. Dum autem quamlibet hostiam ministrat, accipiat eam pollice et indice dexterae manus, et pyxidem, seu patenam sinistra, supponendo illam hostiae, et sic transferat usque ad Fratrem communicandum. Et cum tradit eam, signum crucis cum hostia ante os communicantis formans, dicat, nihil aliud praemittendo: Corpus Domini nostri Jesu Christi custodiat te in vitam aeternam. Amen.

9. The priest first gives the consecrated hosts that were reserved in the pyx, and afterwards those newly consecrated, so that few remain after Communion to be put back and kept in the pyx. As he gives each host, he takes it with the thumb and forefinger of the right hand, and the pyx or paten with the left, holding it beneath the host, and so carries it to the brother who is to communicate. And as he gives it, making the sign of the cross with the host before the mouth of the communicant, he says, putting nothing else before it: Corpus Domini nostri Jesu Christi custodiat te in vitam aeternam. Amen.

Neque illis, Communione peracta, det benedictionem, sed pyxide super corporale deposita, prosequatur Missam, utpote in fine benedictionem daturus. Si vero finita Missa, vel etiam extra Missam Sacerdos det Communionem, tum, illa peracta, deposita pyxide in tabernaculo, det benedictionem, dicens: Benedictio Dei omnipotentis, Patris, et Filii, et Spiritus Sancti descendat super vos, et maneat semper. Amen.

Nor does he give them a blessing when the Communion is over, but, setting the pyx on the corporal, he continues the Mass, since he will give the blessing at the end. But if the priest gives Communion after Mass, or outside Mass, then when it is over, having put the pyx in the tabernacle, he gives the blessing, saying: Benedictio Dei omnipotentis, Patris, et Filii, et Spiritus Sancti descendat super vos, et maneat semper. Amen.

10. Quod si etiam extranei aliqui apud nos communicare voluerint, poterit eos, si fuerint hostiae sufficientes, communicare. Si autem non sint, in aliquo altari minori communicari poterunt. Notandum tamen est, quod si adsit magna multitudo exspectans finem Missae, vel propter aliud impedimentum, poterit differri Communio usque post Missam (nisi in die Cenae) si visum fuerit Priori: alioquin facienda est antequam Communio Missae dicatur. Qui vero non communicant, et sunt in choro, interim jaceant super formas prostrati.

10. If some people from outside also wish to communicate with us, he may give them Communion if there are enough hosts. If there are not, they may be given Communion at some lesser altar. It is to be noted, however, that if a great crowd is present waiting for the end of Mass, or because of some other hindrance, the Communion may be put off until after Mass (except on Maundy Thursday) if the prior sees fit; otherwise it is to be given before the Communion of the Mass is said. Those in choir who do not communicate lie prostrate on the forms meanwhile.

11. Peracta autem Communione, si viderit Sacerdos, quod aliquae hostiae consecratae remanserint, etiamsi hae in magna multitudine fuerint, debet eas omnes in pyxide reponere, sicque positae per Diaconum intra tabernaculum, si sit super altare, recondantur: aut secus, praecedentibus Ceroferariis, ad locum debitum honorifice deferantur, Fratribus prostratis modo supradicto.

11. When Communion is over, if the priest sees that some consecrated hosts remain, even if they are many, he must put them all back in the pyx, and so placed they are put away by the deacon in the tabernacle, if it is on the altar; otherwise they are carried honourably to the proper place, the candle-bearers going before and the brethren prostrate as described above.

12. Ubi autem est consuetudo vinum post communionem sumendi, consumpta hostia, accedant ad illud sumendum; quod stando est recipiendum, et in modica quantitate, ad abluendum os diligenter, ne aliquae particulae hostiae remaneant inter dentes.

12. Where it is the custom to take wine after Communion, when the host has been consumed they come to take it; it is to be received standing, and in a small quantity, to rinse the mouth carefully, lest any particles of the host remain between the teeth.

13. Porro Diaconus debet recipere vinum hujusmodi in calice alio, quam sit calix, cum quo celebratur, et postea aliis ministrare, stando in cornu Evangelii. Debet autem tenere pedem calicis cum mappa munda et decenti, cum qua etiam Fratres, post ablutionem oris, labia detergant.

13. The deacon must receive this wine in a chalice other than the chalice with which Mass is celebrated, and then minister it to the others, standing at the Gospel corner. He must hold the foot of the chalice with a clean and seemly cloth, with which the brethren also wipe their lips after rinsing the mouth.

14. Si tamen fuerit magnus communicandorum numerus (uti in die Cenae Domini), Subdiaconus, in cornu Epistolae stans, poterit vinum in altero calice eodem modo ministrare, ut citius omnes expediantur; vel poterunt etiam Fratres ex se vinum seorsum a Sacrista praeparatum accipere retro altare.

14. If, however, there is a great number communicating (as on Maundy Thursday), the subdeacon, standing at the Epistle corner, may minister wine in another chalice in the same way, so that all may be finished more quickly; or the brethren may also take for themselves wine prepared separately by the sacristan behind the altar.

15. Debent autem Fratres communicare in qualibet Dominica Adventus, et Quadragesimae, in die Natalis Domini, Circumcisionis, Epiphaniae, Cenae Domini, Resurrectionis, Ascensionis, Pentecostes, Trinitatis, et SS. Corporis Christi: Item in omnibus festis B. Mariae semper Virginis, et in festo Omnium Sanctorum: Item in festo Apostolorum Petri et Pauli, et in festis S. Dominici P. N. et Sanctorum Ordinis, et in omnibus Dominicis diebus, deputatis pro confratribus sanctissimi Rosarii, et S. Nominis Dei, propter Indulgentiam plenariam in praefatis diebus concessam. Ceterum bis in mense, videlicet de quindena in quindenam communicabunt, si Praelato visum fuerit expedire. Nullus vero ab hac Communione dictis diebus facienda remaneat, sine speciali licentia: qui autem sunt extra Conventum, debent extra communicare, vel cum redierint, quam cito et commode possunt.

15. The brethren must communicate on every Sunday of Advent and of Lent; on Christmas Day, the Circumcision, the Epiphany, Maundy Thursday, Easter, the Ascension, Pentecost, Trinity Sunday and the most holy Body of Christ; also on all feasts of Blessed Mary ever Virgin, and on the feast of All Saints; also on the feast of the Apostles Peter and Paul, on the feasts of our holy Father Dominic and of the saints of the Order, and on all the Sundays appointed for the confraternities of the most holy Rosary and of the Holy Name of God, because of the plenary indulgence granted on those days. Otherwise they will communicate twice a month, that is every fortnight, if the prelate sees fit. No one is to stay away from this Communion on the said days without special leave; those who are outside the convent must communicate outside, or when they return, as soon as they conveniently can.

16. Dum autem plures dies Communionis immediate succedent, poterunt Fratres ad arbitrium Praelati in aliquibus dispensari a Communione.

16. When several Communion days follow one another immediately, the brethren may be dispensed from Communion on some of them at the prelate's discretion.

Rubricae generales MissarumGeneral rubrics of the Mass

Missa quotidie celebrari debet secundum ordinem Officii, de quo fit illo die, idest de festo Toto Duplici, vel Duplici, vel Semiduplici, vel Simplici, vel trium Lectionum; de Dominica vel Vigilia, vel Feria, vel Octava: et extra ordinem Officii, Votiva vel pro Defunctis.

Mass is to be celebrated every day according to the order of the Office said that day, that is, of a feast Totum Duplex, Duplex, Semiduplex, Simplex or of three Lessons; of a Sunday, a Vigil, a Feria or an Octave; and, outside the order of the Office, a Votive Mass or a Mass for the Dead.

I De FestisOf Feasts

1. Festa Tota Duplicia, Duplicia, Semiduplicia, Simplicia et trium Lectionum semper celebrantur proprio die, nisi ab Officio vel festo nobiliori impediantur, ut habetur infra in Rubrica tit. VII.

1. Feasts Totum Duplex, Duplex, Semiduplex, Simplex and of three Lessons are always celebrated on their own day, unless they are impeded by a higher Office or feast, as is set out below in title VII.

2. In festis Totis Duplicibus et Duplicibus dicitur una tantum Oratio, nisi aliqua Memoria occurrat facienda, quae tamen aliquando juxta Rubricam tit. VIII omittenda est.

2. On feasts Totum Duplex and Duplex only one Prayer is said, unless some Commemoration occurs which must be made; this, however, is sometimes to be left out, according to title VIII.

3. In festis Semiduplicibus et infra, extra Octavam solemnem vel majorem, tres saltem Orationes dicantur, ut habetur infra in Rubrica tit. X.

3. On feasts Semiduplex and below, outside a solemn or greater Octave, at least three Prayers are said, as set out below in title X.

II De OctavisOf Octaves

1. Missa infra Octavas, si de ipsis fiat Officium, dicitur sicut in die, nisi pro singulis Octavae diebus Missa propria habeatur. Credo tamen quotidie infra Octavas solemnissimas tantum dicitur.

1. Within Octaves, when their Office is said, the Mass is said as on the feast itself, unless a proper Mass is given for each day of the Octave. The Credo, however, is said daily only within the most solemn Octaves.

2. Si Officium Octavae ab aliquo festo vel Dominica impediatur, in Missa festi vel Dominicae fit vel omittitur Memoria Octavae, prout in Rubricis Breviarii de singulis dicitur.

2. If the Office of the Octave is impeded by a feast or a Sunday, the Commemoration of the Octave is made or omitted in the Mass of the feast or Sunday, as the Rubrics of the Breviary say for each case.

3. Dum autem fit de Octava solemni et supra, una tantum Oratio dicatur, nisi Memoria facienda sit. Per Octavam simplicem et in die Octava trium Lectionum plures Orationes dicendae sunt, prout habetur in Rubrica tit. X.

3. When the Office is of a solemn Octave or above, only one Prayer is said, unless a Commemoration must be made. Through a simple Octave and on an Octave day of three Lessons, several Prayers are said, as set out in title X.

III De DominicaOf the Sunday

1. Per totum annum, nisi festum, de quo in Dominicis sit agendum, impediat, et praeterquam in Vigiliis Natalis Domini, et Epiphaniae, occurrentibus in Dominicis, semper est Missa de Dominica celebranda.

1. Throughout the year the Mass of the Sunday is always to be celebrated, unless a feast which is to be kept on Sundays impedes it, and except when the Vigils of Christmas and of the Epiphany fall on a Sunday.

2. Dicuntur autem tres Orationes, ut hic infra tit. X praescribitur, exceptis Dominicis Palmarum et in Albis, in quibus una sola dicitur, et exceptis casibus, de quibus in tit. VIII.

2. Three Prayers are said, as prescribed below in title X, except on Palm Sunday and Low Sunday, when only one is said, and except in the cases given in title VIII.

3. Quando vero ob dicta impedimenta Missa Dominicae proprio die celebrari non potest, semper est dicenda (nisi ubi aliter notatur in propriis locis) infra hebdomadam propriam in aliqua Feria vacante, in qua legitur Homilia Dominicae: tunc autem celebratur juxta ritum Officii, de quo illo die fit, cum memoria, vel memoriis convenientibus.

3. When, because of these impediments, the Sunday Mass cannot be celebrated on its own day, it is always to be said (unless otherwise noted in its proper place) within its own week on a free Feria on which the Homily of the Sunday is read; it is then celebrated according to the rite of the Office said that day, with the fitting Commemoration or Commemorations.

4. In hujusmodi porro resumptionis die, occurrente festo Toto Duplici, Duplici, Semiduplici, vel Simplici, duae dicuntur Missae conventuales, una in Choro, altera extra Chorum ut infra tit. IV n. 2 dicetur; in Festis vero et diebus Octavis trium Lectionum et infra Octavas, et in Feriis una tantum Missa conventualis de ipsa Dominica. Missae autem privatae dici possunt aut de Officio diei cum Memoria Dominicae aut de ipsa Dominica cum Memoria Officii diei; additis in utraque Missa aliis Memoriis forte occurrentibus. Idem fiat (etiam omissa Homilia) de Missa alicujus Dominicae, quae post Oct. Epiphaniae vel Trinitatis resumi nequit, cujus tamen Evangelium legitur in fine omnium Missarum, in quibus de tali Dominica facta fuerit Memoria. — Si in Dominica IV Adventus evenerit Vigilia Nativitatis Domini, in Choro dicitur Missa Vigiliae, extra Chorum Missa de Dominica proprio ritu. Item Missa Dominicae IV post Oct. Paschae impeditae ipsa die Dominica dicitur extra Chorum. — Similiter Missa Dominicae, cujus Homilia infra hebdomadam legi nequit, dicitur ipsa die Dominica extra Chorum. — Si eodem die infra hebdomadam occurrant Missa Dominicae et Vigiliae, dicitur in Choro Missa Dominicae cum Memoria Vigiliae.

4. On such a day of resumption, if a feast Totum Duplex, Duplex, Semiduplex or Simplex occurs, two conventual Masses are said, one in Choir and the other outside Choir, as will be said below in title IV, n. 2; but on feasts and Octave days of three Lessons, within Octaves, and on Ferias, only one conventual Mass, of the Sunday itself. Private Masses may be said either of the Office of the day with a Commemoration of the Sunday, or of the Sunday with a Commemoration of the Office of the day, adding in either Mass any other Commemorations that occur. The same is done (even though the Homily is omitted) with the Mass of a Sunday which cannot be resumed after the Octave of the Epiphany or after Trinity; its Gospel, however, is read at the end of all the Masses in which that Sunday has been commemorated. — If the Vigil of Christmas falls on the fourth Sunday of Advent, the Mass of the Vigil is said in Choir, and outside Choir the Mass of the Sunday with its own rite. Likewise the Mass of the fourth Sunday after the Octave of Easter, when impeded, is said on the Sunday itself outside Choir. — Similarly the Mass of a Sunday whose Homily cannot be read during the week is said on the Sunday itself outside Choir. — If on the same weekday the Mass of a Sunday and that of a Vigil occur, the Mass of the Sunday is said in Choir with a Commemoration of the Vigil.

IV De Feria et VigiliaOf the Feria and the Vigil

1. Missa de Feria celebratur, quando non occurrit festum, vel Octava simplex et supra, vel non fit Missa pro mortuis. In Feriis, quae propriam non habent, dicitur Missa Dominicae praecedentis; vel dici possunt Missae votivae juxta ordinem Feriarum de Sancto vel Officio in Ordine nostro privilegiato, vel pro Missis privatis etiam aliae votivae, de quibus in sequenti titulo V agitur.

1. The Mass of the Feria is celebrated when no feast or Octave, simple or above, occurs, and no Mass for the dead is said. On Ferias which have no Mass of their own, the Mass of the preceding Sunday is said; or the votive Masses may be said, according to the order of the Ferias, of the Saint or Office privileged in our Order, or, for private Masses, other votive Masses as well, which are treated in title V below.

2. In omnibus Feriis Quadragesimae post diem Cinerum ac Passionis ante Dominicam Palmarum, in Feriis Quatuor Temporum extra Octavam Pentecostes, et in Vigiliis communibus quae propriam Missam habent, si occurrat Officium Totum Duplex, Duplex, Semiduplex vel Simplex, in Ecclesiis in quibus adest obligatio Chori, duae dicuntur Missae conventuales: una de Officio diei, sine Memoria Feriae aut Vigiliae, sed cum aliis Memoriis forte occurrentibus; altera de Feria aut Vigilia, sine Memoria Officii diei. Harum tamen una tantum dicitur in Choro cum assistentia Choralium; altera vero, sine hujusmodi assistentia Choralium, legitur extra Chorum. Missa porro in Choro dicenda cum assistentia Choralium, in Totis Duplicibus I et II classis et per Octavas privilegiatas Epiphaniae et SS. Corporis Christi, est Missa de Officio currenti; in reliquis Totis Duplicibus, Duplicibus, Semiduplicibus, et Simplicibus, Missa de Feria aut Vigilia. In Totis Duplicibus tamen I classis nihil umquam fit de Vigilia non privilegiata (excepta Vigilia Ascensionis, quae est feria major), juxta Rubricas. De die item infra Octavam non privilegiatam, si in Feriis aut Vigiliis supra recensitis occurrat, non dicitur Missa conventualis, sed fit tantum Memoria in Missa de Feria vel Vigilia. Missae autem privatae omnibus his diebus ad libitum Celebrantium dici possunt aut de Feria vel Vigilia cum Memoria Officii diei; aut de Officio diei cum Memoria Feriae vel Vigiliae et ultimo Evangelio Feriae. Si tamen Officium occurrens sit Totum Duplex I aut II classis, aut Octava privilegiata Epiphaniae et SS. Corporis Christi, Missa privata de Feria vel Vigilia non permittitur.

2. On all the Ferias of Lent after Ash Wednesday and of Passiontide before Palm Sunday, on Ember days outside the Octave of Pentecost, and on common Vigils that have a Mass of their own, if an Office Totum Duplex, Duplex, Semiduplex or Simplex occurs, two conventual Masses are said in churches bound to Choir: one of the Office of the day, without a Commemoration of the Feria or Vigil but with any other Commemorations that occur; the other of the Feria or Vigil, without a Commemoration of the Office of the day. Only one of them, however, is said in Choir with the choir assisting; the other is read outside Choir without the choir. The Mass said in Choir with the choir assisting is, on feasts Totum Duplex of the I and II class and through the privileged Octaves of the Epiphany and of Corpus Christi, the Mass of the current Office; on the other feasts Totum Duplex, Duplex, Semiduplex and Simplex, the Mass of the Feria or Vigil. On feasts Totum Duplex of the I class, however, nothing at all is done of a non-privileged Vigil (except the Vigil of the Ascension, which is a greater feria), according to the Rubrics. Of a day within a non-privileged Octave falling on the Ferias or Vigils listed above, no conventual Mass is said; it is only commemorated in the Mass of the Feria or Vigil. Private Masses on all these days may be said, as the celebrant chooses, either of the Feria or Vigil with a Commemoration of the Office of the day, or of the Office of the day with a Commemoration of the Feria or Vigil and the Gospel of the Feria at the end. If, however, the occurring Office is Totum Duplex of the I or II class, or the privileged Octave of the Epiphany or of Corpus Christi, a private Mass of the Feria or Vigil is not allowed.

3. Si qua ex dictis Feriis et Vigilia simul occurrant, Missa conventualis dicitur de Feria cum Memoria Vigiliae; et Missae privatae dici possunt aut de Feria aut de Vigilia cum Memoria alterius. Aut si fiat Officium Totius Duplicis, vel Duplicis, vel Octavae privilegiatae, vel Semiduplicis seu Simplicis, duae dicuntur Missae conventuales ut supra: una de Officio diei, sine Memoria Feriae et Vigiliae, sed cum Memoriis forte occurrentibus; altera de Feria, cum Memoria Vigiliae; et Missae privatae dici possunt aut de Feria, aut de Vigilia, aut de Officio diei cum Memoria de eis de quibus non dicitur Missa et ultimo Evangelio Feriae.

3. If one of these Ferias and a Vigil occur together, the conventual Mass is of the Feria with a Commemoration of the Vigil, and private Masses may be either of the Feria or of the Vigil with a Commemoration of the other. Or, if the Office is of a feast Totum Duplex, Duplex, a privileged Octave, Semiduplex or Simplex, two conventual Masses are said as above: one of the Office of the day, without a Commemoration of the Feria and Vigil but with any Commemorations that occur; the other of the Feria with a Commemoration of the Vigil. Private Masses may then be of the Feria, of the Vigil, or of the Office of the day, with a Commemoration of those whose Mass is not said and the Gospel of the Feria at the end.

4. Item die 25 Aprilis, vel, si die 25 Aprilis Pascha fuerit, Feria III post Pascha, et in diebus Rogationum, si occurrat Officium Totius Duplicis, Duplicis, Octavae privilegiatae, Semiduplicis vel Simplicis (sive Processio fiat, sive non), duae dicuntur Missae conventuales ut supra n. 2 dictum est: una de Rogationibus (vel de Vigilia Ascensionis cum Memoria Rogationum), altera vero de Officio diei. In festis tamen Totis Duplicibus I et II classis aut infra Octavam Paschae, si fiat Processio, in Choro dicitur tum Missa de Officio diei tum Missa de Rogationibus; si vero Processio non fiat, dicitur unica Missa conventualis de die cum memoria Feriae (vel Vigiliae) et ultimo Evangelio Rogationum. In omnibus Litaniarum diebus, dummodo Officium de Feria dictum non fuerit, Missae privatae non permittuntur de Rogationibus, sed Memoria Feriae (vel Vigiliae) et ultimum Evangelium Rogationum dicitur in omnibus Missis privatis, quae non sunt Defunctorum.

4. Likewise on 25 April (or, if 25 April is Easter Day, on the Tuesday after Easter) and on the Rogation days, if an Office Totum Duplex, Duplex, of a privileged Octave, Semiduplex or Simplex occurs (whether or not the Procession is held), two conventual Masses are said, as said above in n. 2: one of the Rogations (or of the Vigil of the Ascension with a Commemoration of the Rogations), the other of the Office of the day. On feasts Totum Duplex of the I and II class, however, or within the Octave of Easter, if the Procession is held, both the Mass of the Office of the day and the Mass of the Rogations are said in Choir; if it is not held, a single conventual Mass is said, of the day, with a Commemoration of the Feria (or Vigil) and the Gospel of the Rogations at the end. On all the days of the Litanies, unless the Office was of the Feria, private Masses of the Rogations are not allowed; instead the Commemoration of the Feria (or Vigil) and the last Gospel of the Rogations are said in all private Masses that are not Masses for the Dead.

5. Propter paucitatem Fratrum in praedictis Feriis vel Vigiliis occurrentibus cum festo, una tantum Missa conventualis celebretur, prout dicitur in sequenti n. 6.

5. Where the brethren are few, only one conventual Mass is celebrated on these Ferias or Vigils when they fall with a feast, as is said in n. 6 following.

6. In Ecclesiis autem in quibus una tantum habeatur Missa conventualis, quoties occurrat aliqua ex Feriis vel Vigiliis n. 2 et 3 recensitis, et si non fiat Processio in diebus Rogationum, ipsa Missa conventualis in Totis Duplicibus I et II classis et per Octavas privilegiatas Epiphaniae et SS. Corporis Christi dicitur de Officio diei cum Memoria et ultimo Evangelio Feriae, et extra Tota Duplicia I classis, etiam cum Memoria Vigiliae. In Totis Duplicibus vero, et Duplicibus, Semiduplicibus et Simplicibus, eadem Missa dicitur de Feria aut Vigilia cum Memoria Officii diei; et, si Feria et Vigilia simul occurrant, de Feria cum Memoria Officii diei ac Vigiliae et Memoriis occurrentibus (vel in Vigilia Ascensionis, de ipsa Vigilia cum Memoria Rogationum). Item in diebus Litaniarum si fiat Processio et in Ecclesia habeatur unica Missa, ea dicitur de Rogationibus (vel Vigilia Ascensionis) cum Memoria Officii diei; nisi hoc fuerit Totum Duplex I classis, quo in casu dicitur Missa de Festo cum Memoria Feriae (vel Vigiliae) et ultimo Evangelio Rogationum.

6. In churches that have only one conventual Mass, whenever one of the Ferias or Vigils listed in nn. 2 and 3 occurs, and when the Procession is not held on the Rogation days, that conventual Mass is said, on feasts Totum Duplex of the I and II class and through the privileged Octaves of the Epiphany and of Corpus Christi, of the Office of the day with a Commemoration of the Feria and its Gospel at the end, and, outside feasts Totum Duplex of the I class, with a Commemoration of the Vigil as well. On the other feasts Totum Duplex, and on Duplex, Semiduplex and Simplex feasts, the same Mass is said of the Feria or Vigil with a Commemoration of the Office of the day; and if a Feria and a Vigil occur together, of the Feria, with Commemorations of the Office of the day, of the Vigil and of any others that occur (or, on the Vigil of the Ascension, of the Vigil itself with a Commemoration of the Rogations). Likewise on the days of the Litanies, if the Procession is held and the church has only one Mass, it is said of the Rogations (or of the Vigil of the Ascension) with a Commemoration of the Office of the day; unless that is a feast Totum Duplex of the I class, in which case the Mass of the feast is said with a Commemoration of the Feria (or Vigil) and the Gospel of the Rogations at the end.

V De Missis votivisOf Votive Masses

1. In Feriis minoribus, in quibus nec Missa Dominicae resumenda, nec Missa pro defunctis celebranda est, loco Missae Dominicae praecedentis, dici potest Missa conventualis votiva juxta Ordinem Feriarum de Sancto vel Officio in Ordine nostro olim privilegiato, idest: Feria III de S. P. N. Dominico, Feria IV de S. Thoma Aquinate, et Feria V de SS. Sacramento, aut de D. N. J. C. Summo et Aeterno Sacerdote. Hae Missae votivae, excepta Missa de D. N. J. C. Sacerdote, dicuntur extra Septuagesimam cum Gloria et tria Alleluia post Epistolam vel ℟., vel infra hoc tempus cum Tractu. Feria III dicitur etiam Sequentia. Feria VI dici potest Missa de sancta Cruce, vel pro Fratribus mortuis illo anno, vel de S. Vincentio (semel in mense) vel alio, juxta consuetudinem Provinciarum, sed feriali ritu. Sabbato dicitur Missa votiva de B. Maria V. pro tempore, si Officium votivum de ipsa actum fuerit.

1. On lesser Ferias, when neither the Mass of a Sunday is to be resumed nor a Mass for the dead to be celebrated, instead of the Mass of the preceding Sunday a votive conventual Mass may be said, according to the order of the Ferias, of the Saint or Office formerly privileged in our Order: on Tuesday of our holy Father Dominic, on Wednesday of St Thomas Aquinas, and on Thursday of the Blessed Sacrament or of our Lord Jesus Christ the High and Eternal Priest. These votive Masses, except that of our Lord Jesus Christ the Priest, are said outside Septuagesima with the Gloria and three Alleluias after the Epistle or Responsory, and within that time with the Tract. On Tuesday the Sequence is also said. On Friday the Mass of the Holy Cross may be said, or for the brethren who have died that year, or of St Vincent (once a month), or another, according to the custom of the Provinces, but with the ferial rite. On Saturday the votive Mass of Our Lady proper to the season is said, if her votive Office has been said.

2.
